# CLAUDE.md

This file provides comprehensive guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) and Codex CLI when working with code in this repository.

## Commonly Used Commands

### Environment & Dependencies
```bash
# Set up virtual environment and install dependencies
python3 -m venv .venv
.venv/bin/pip install -r requirements.txt

# If system Chrome is not installed, install Playwright Chromium
.venv/bin/playwright install chromium
```

### Running the Gateway
```bash
# Start the FastAPI server on 127.0.0.1:8000
./run.sh
# Or directly via uvicorn (note: `python -m uvicorn`, not `.venv/bin/uvicorn`):
.venv/bin/python -m uvicorn server:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000
```

**Why `python -m uvicorn` and not `.venv/bin/uvicorn`**: a venv's console scripts bake in the **absolute** interpreter path from creation time, so a venv that was moved or copied (as this machine's `.venv` was — created under a different project directory) fails with `bad interpreter: No such file or directory` while the venv itself is perfectly usable. `run.sh` therefore resolves an interpreter that can actually `import uvicorn` (project venv first, system `python3` as fallback) and launches it as a module, which needs no shebang and works in both cases. Repairing the moved venv's scripts is `sed`-level work on the stale shebang line; recreating it (`python3 -m venv .venv && .venv/bin/python -m pip install -r requirements.txt`) is the heavier alternative.

**Before the first start after a long idle period**, run the login pass first: the startup sequence performs a silent token refresh and an initial health probe for each provider, and with stale credentials each of those is a real upstream request — exactly the "automated credential attempt" signature the risk-control line exists to avoid. `.venv/bin/python login.py all` first, then `./run.sh`.

### Authentication & Token Management (Five Major Models)
```bash
# Interactive login to capture session tokens (opens browser)
.venv/bin/python login.py all        # Log in all providers (qwen, deepseek, doubao, kimi, glm)
.venv/bin/python login.py qwen       # Log in Tongyi Qianwen only (Aliyun SSO / WAF)
.venv/bin/python login.py deepseek   # Log in DeepSeek only (Bearer token / Cloudflare)
.venv/bin/python login.py doubao     # Log in Doubao only (sessionid / device tokens)
.venv/bin/python login.py kimi       # Log in Kimi only (refresh_token)
.venv/bin/python login.py glm        # Log in Zhipu GLM only (chatglm_token)

# Silent token refresh (headless, reuses existing browser profiles)
.venv/bin/python login.py all --refresh
.venv/bin/python login.py qwen --refresh
.venv/bin/python login.py deepseek --refresh
.venv/bin/python login.py doubao --refresh
.venv/bin/python login.py kimi --refresh
.venv/bin/python login.py glm --refresh
```

### Running Tests
Tests are standalone Python scripts using mocked transports (completely offline, no external test runner or network access needed):
```bash
# Run ALL suites. A glob, not a hand-kept list: the list used to omit 13 of the
# 79 files (measured) -- including tests/test_report_read_only.py, the runtime
# tripwire that found the read-endpoint write bug -- because every new suite had
# to be remembered in two places. The exit status is the only verdict.
fail=0
for f in tests/test_*.py; do
  .venv/bin/python "$f" >/tmp/$(basename "$f").log 2>&1 || { echo "FAIL $f"; fail=$((fail+1)); }
done
echo "failures: $fail / $(ls tests/test_*.py | wc -l)"   # must print 0

# Run individual test files
.venv/bin/python tests/test_providers.py   # Provider protocols, SSE chunk parsing, and PoW wasm loading
.venv/bin/python tests/test_server.py      # Server routing, OpenAI/Anthropic error transformation, and SSE streaming
.venv/bin/python tests/test_session.py     # Session persistence, POSIX atomicity, file permissions, and env priority
.venv/bin/python tests/test_healing.py     # HealthMonitor state machine, HealingEngine, and AgentRunner discovery
.venv/bin/python tests/test_responses.py   # OpenAI Responses protocol (/v1/responses) and Codex compatibility
.venv/bin/python tests/test_tools.py       # Streaming tool calling, speculative streaming, and self-healing JSON engine
.venv/bin/python tests/test_affinity.py    # Session affinity, cross-model state handoff, LRU/TTL, and telemetry headers
.venv/bin/python tests/test_singleflight.py # Deterministic SHA-256 fingerprinting, streaming broadcast fanout, and debounce coalescing
.venv/bin/python tests/test_failover.py     # Capability rings, fallback candidate ordering/cooldown demotion, risk metadata, and transparent failover
.venv/bin/python tests/test_disconnect.py   # Client-disconnect propagation, upstream stream reclamation, and orphaned flight recovery
.venv/bin/python tests/test_token_cache.py  # Deterministic token-count memoization, bounded LRU, and count_tokens endpoint integration
.venv/bin/python tests/test_lifecycle.py    # Request lifecycle finalization, flight-group abort terminality, and load-balancer quota reclamation
.venv/bin/python tests/test_risk_ledger.py  # Risk-control severity tiers, durable cross-restart cooldowns, and the JSONL interception ledger
.venv/bin/python tests/test_probe_gate.py   # Cooldown-aware health probe gating + read-time cooling overlay and healer-election avoidance (banned nodes are never re-probed or used for healing)
.venv/bin/python tests/test_refresh_gate.py # Cooldown-aware token refresh gating with bounded deferred retry
.venv/bin/python tests/test_adaptive_pacing.py # Evidence-driven pacing multiplier from the risk ledger
.venv/bin/python tests/test_graceful_drain.py # In-flight request drain on shutdown (streaming-safe) and inflight telemetry
.venv/bin/python tests/test_conversation_gc.py # Durable retry queue for remote temporary-conversation deletion (survives restarts)
.venv/bin/python tests/test_quarantine.py   # Operator long-duration quarantine leases (folded into every cooldown gate + admin API)
.venv/bin/python tests/test_quarantine_hardstop.py # Quarantine hard-stop at the dispatch boundary (quarantined nodes are never dispatched, even as failover fallbacks)
.venv/bin/python tests/test_probation.py    # Post-avoidance recovery probation: serialized canary handshakes, evidence-based graduation (window renewal) and widened pacing after a ban lifts
.venv/bin/python tests/test_risk_dossier.py # All-time interception dossier, posture derivation, and bypass/re-login avoidance verdicts
.venv/bin/python tests/test_risk_correlation.py # Cross-provider risk correlation: systemic escalation detection and the global pacing governor
.venv/bin/python tests/test_credential_watch.py # Whole-ring credential outage: distinct-node threshold, TTL probe, zero-upstream-call fast-fail
.venv/bin/python tests/test_credential_renewal.py # Credential-renewal detection: content fingerprint of session/<provider>.json, cause-scoped release of the 24h auth cooldown, quarantine/WAF immunity, unblocks the refresh gate
.venv/bin/python tests/test_ban_inference.py # Account-level ban inference: "a provably-renewed credential is still rejected" discriminator, self-clearing auto lease, operator-lease precedence, zero-touch dispatch blocking
.venv/bin/python tests/test_waf_escalation.py # Human-verification (RGV587/captcha) long avoidance: repeated-WAF escalation by powers of two, rate-limit zero-regression, human-gated release via credential fingerprint, cause-scoped release in both directions
.venv/bin/python tests/test_device_identity.py # Stable per-node device identity: identity must be constant across every request/restart (Kimi/GLM previously minted a new device per request), node-scoped distinctness, decoupling from credentials, priority of captured values, fail-safe degradation, UA-consistent client hints
.venv/bin/python tests/test_long_avoidance.py # Long-avoidance hard-stop at dispatch: threshold proven to sit in the vacuum band between the short-cooling cap and the escalation floor, ring-wide mixed-cause long avoidance fails closed with zero upstream calls, short-cooling "last resort" preserved, redirect leaves avoiding nodes untouched, dossier fail-closed posture
.venv/bin/python tests/test_disabled_propagation.py # Operator-disable propagation: DISABLED_PROVIDERS honoured by every upstream self-maintenance channel (token refresh, conversation GC, healer election, probe), the single `touch_avoidance_reason` judgement with honest causes (disabled > quarantined > cooldown), disabled is never deferred (waiting on a human, not a window), disabled survives the cooldown master switch, zero regression for healthy/cooling/leased nodes
.venv/bin/python tests/test_dossier_dispatch_agreement.py # Dossier/dispatch agreement: the dossier must never report a node dispatchable that the dispatch gate hard-blocks (measured bug: `DISABLED_PROVIDERS` was blocked at dispatch but reported `posture=clear, dispatchable=True`), the agreement is asserted as a *property over every per-node state* so the next missed cell turns red instead of waiting for an operator, the ring-wide credential-outage exception is explicitly locked rather than hidden, danger-first posture ordering (a banned+disabled node reports "ban" first so lifting the disable cannot re-dispatch a banned node), the query stays read-only
.venv/bin/python tests/test_dispatch_audit.py # Zero-upstream dispatch dry-run: read-only gate audit (`GET /v1/admin/dispatch-audit`), per-node blocked_by/remaining, ring-level fail-closed prediction, and — the case that found a real bug — the prediction is asserted to match what `execute_chat` actually does (redirect target and err_type), so the dry-run can never drift into a more convincing lie
.venv/bin/python tests/test_dispatch_gate.py # Single gate definition and operator-disable hard-stop: `DISABLED_PROVIDERS` now blocks at the dispatch boundary (measured bug: the dry-run reported the node blocked while `execute_chat` dispatched straight into it), the gate order has exactly one copy (proven by rigging that single gate and asserting the real dispatch obeys the tampering), one reading per configuration (`_is_provider_healthy` no longer re-parses the env), and short coolings keep the demote-only last resort
.venv/bin/python tests/test_tool_roundtrip.py # Tool-loop closure at the prompt layer: assistant `tool_calls` and `role:"tool"` results must both reach upstream (previously the whole call vanished, so the model saw a tool result it never asked for), JSON-string arguments must not be double-encoded, tool results carry their name via id lookup, and `continuation_content()` sends the tool result instead of repeating the stale question in continuation mode
.venv/bin/python tests/test_tool_fabrication.py # Anti-fabrication: a fenced block that merely looks like a tool call (python/ts code, no declared name, wrong shape) must never become an executable tool call — the three-layer defence (format whitelist → strict shape → declared-name allowlist) and that a rejected call is returned as text rather than silently swallowed
.venv/bin/python tests/test_tool_schema.py # [TOOL-SCHEMA] Prompt schema fidelity: nested object/array/enum survival, `required` preservation, Anthropic `input_schema` support, `tool_choice` none/required/named-tool directives, description truncation, and the sticky budget downgrade to compact summaries — the tool prompt is the only schema these upstreams ever see, so fidelity is the product
.venv/bin/python tests/test_responses_protocol.py # [RESPONSES-PROTOCOL] Wire contract Codex CLI actually dispatches on: `output_text.delta` (not the legacy `text.delta`), `type` matching the event name in every body, gap-free `sequence_number`, one `item_id` across added/delta/done, `output_text` content parts with `annotations`, reasoning items counting into usage, `function_call` items with `call_id`, and identical event skeletons between the tools and no-tools paths
.venv/bin/python tests/test_agent_loop.py # [AGENT-LOOP] Two-round agent loops per protocol, asserted on *the prompt upstream really receives*: OpenAI tool_calls + tool result, Anthropic tool_use + tool_result (with id), Responses function_call + function_call_output, continuation mode sending only the tool result, `tool_choice` reaching the prompt, `parallel_tool_calls:false` / `disable_parallel_tool_use` both reaching the prompt and capping the response to one call on all three protocols, a tool call that arrives only in the reasoning channel being rescued (and *not* rescued when the model also answered), and an undeclared tool name never surfacing as tool_calls
.venv/bin/python tests/test_stream_keepalive.py # [STREAM-KEEPALIVE] SSE heartbeats during upstream silence: `: keepalive` is a spec comment any client must ignore, the timeout must NOT cancel the pending pull (doing so closed the upstream generator and silently dropped every later chunk — caught by the first case), upstream errors still propagate, `GATEWAY_STREAM_KEEPALIVE=0` is value-for-value the old bare generator, and all six streaming exits are structurally asserted to be wrapped
.venv/bin/python tests/test_stop_sequences.py # [PROTOCOL] Client-declared output constraints that were parsed and then ignored: `stop`/`stop_sequences` truncate at the gateway (a stop sequence is hard semantics, a prompt only lowers the probability), a terminator split across two chunks is held back rather than leaked (`EN` + `D`), the terminator's tail is not eaten when nothing matches, a ```tool_call *after* the truncation point is never executed (the client cannot see it, so executing it is an unrequested call), per-protocol terminal enums (OpenAI has no `stop_sequence` — only `stop`; Anthropic must name the matched string; Responses is `status="incomplete"` + `incomplete_details.reason="stop"`), `response_format` / `text.format` really reaching the upstream prompt, and zero regression when `stop` is absent (the scanner returning `""` when unconfigured silently emptied every streaming body on all three protocols — the case exists to keep that shape from coming back). The suite installs a `_make_provider` tripwire and asserts a zero-hit counter at the end, because a case that forgot to install the scripted provider makes the gateway issue a **real** request with real credentials.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_stream_errors.py # [STREAM-AUDIT] Upstream stream error classification: Qwen and DeepSeek called `raise_for_status()` *inside* `async with client.stream(...)`, so the exception fired after the response was closed and the handler's `e.response.text` only ever raised `StreamClosed`/`ResponseNotRead` — a "could not read the body" exception covering the original status code, destroying risk classification entirely. 401 / 403 / 429 now read the body inside the stream context and keep their real status + `err_type` (`auth_error` / `waf_blocked` / `rate_limit_error`), 5xx stays a generic 502, and DeepSeek's pre-request session/PoW calls translate `HTTPStatusError` too (a Cloudflare 403 used to escape `chat()` as a bare httpx exception, so the banned node's interception never reached the ledger)
.venv/bin/python tests/test_stream_concurrency.py # [STREAM-AUDIT] Stream-lifetime resource ownership: the per-node concurrency semaphore must cover the **whole streamed body**, not the handshake (it was held in an `AsyncExitStack` that closed on `return`, so qwen 3 / deepseek 2 / kimi 2 bounded only a millisecond handshake — measured peak 6 in-flight on an upstream account limited to 3, which is exactly the "one account, many parallel automated sessions" signature); an upstream that yields **zero** frames is a failure, not a success (all five providers unconditionally yield a trailing metadata frame, so an immediately-empty stream means connected-but-silent — the old code called `record_success` and returned an empty answer, so the failure count never grew); a mid-stream break cools the node (risk-class breaks are left to the server-side ledger so one interception is never counted twice), while the slot is still returned on normal completion and on client disconnect
.venv/bin/python tests/test_stream_lifecycle.py # [STREAM-AUDIT] Connection reclamation on every exit: Kimi and GLM called `client.send(..., stream=True)` and never `aclose()`d — not on success, not on 401/429/5xx — so each request leaked a slot in the shared pool (100 conns / 40 keep-alive), with the health probe leaking one more per node every five minutes; the symptom (random timeouts, pool exhaustion) is hours from the cause. Both now close in a `finally`, and non-object JSON frames (`data: []`, `null`, `42` — legal JSON, but `.get()` on them raises) are skipped instead of turning a single frame into a 502
.venv/bin/python tests/test_stream_preflight.py # [H4] A zero-upstream pre-dispatch rejection (credential outage / operator disable / quarantine lease / ring exhausted) must be visible on the **status line**, not as HTTP 200 with an in-band `data: {"error": ...}`: the OpenAI chat and Anthropic streaming paths left the `execute_chat` await inside the response generator, so the headers had already gone out as 200 and most SDKs treated the request as successful and dropped the error -- silent failure, while `/v1/responses` returned 503 for the same condition. Both now await only the call that precedes the first event (the moment the upstream stream is handed over), and the suite proves that this did **not** become buffering: a failure after the stream is open still emits an in-band error and `[DONE]` is never sent. The suite also asserts all three protocols agree on the same status for the same rejection. Two older cases in `tests/test_server.py` asserted the 200+in-band behaviour and were corrected, not worked around.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_state_write_elision.py # [H5] Cooldown-state persistence must write only when the state actually changes: `_persist_state` sits on the per-request `record_success`/`record_failure` paths and used to rewrite the whole file (temp + fsync + os.replace, inside the `asyncio.Lock`, on the event loop) even when the content was byte-identical -- measured 20 healthy successes = 20 atomic writes, ~0.6ms median / up to ~12ms. Now 50 healthy successes cost 0 writes while every genuine transition (cooldown created, failure count incremented, cooldown cleared) still writes exactly once and the on-disk content equals the in-memory snapshot; `record_success` pops the failure count instead of writing 0 so "healthy" and "absent" are the same state; the fingerprint is seeded after restore so a restart does not re-write identical bytes; `save_state` returns whether the write really happened so a failed write is retried rather than remembered as done; and the bare `except: pass` that hid a `NameError` (missing `json` import) and silently disabled the *entire* cross-restart avoidance chain now logs a warning -- best-effort is not the same as invisible.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_stream_start_reclaim.py # [C2] All three streaming paths hold upstream resources *before* the response body exists (`execute_chat` has already read the first chunk and holds the node semaphore; `/v1/responses` also calls `load_balancer.acquire()` before building the response), while every release lives in the body generator's `finally`. `aclose()` on a never-started async generator is a **no-op** (its `finally` never runs), so a client that vanishes before the response head leaked LB quota, the node semaphore and the upstream stream on all three protocols -- permanently, until restart. Fixed by `ManagedStream` (explicit, idempotent reclaim that advances one frame before closing, so a never-started source still runs its `finally`) plus `ReclaimingStreamingResponse.__call__`, whose `finally` reclaims the slot, closes the body, closes the upstream stream and releases the protocol-level quota -- the close is dispatched as an independent shielded task, because cleanup on a cancelled task has every `await` re-cancelled. Also proves a complete stream still releases exactly once and a mid-stream upstream failure still reports in-band.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_session_continuity.py # [SESSION-CONTINUITY] Upstream conversation ids are **node-private**, and transparent failover is the second path that moves a request to another vendor -- it never goes through `resolve_affinity`'s handoff branch, which is the only place that stripped the heterogeneous id (M3). `server.py` wrote `affinity_decision.upstream_conv_id` into `chat_args` once and `providers/failover.py` handed it verbatim to every candidate (`call_kwargs = dict(chat_args)`). Measured with glm failing over to doubao: doubao received `conversation_id="glm-conv"`, and the weight is not the string but the branch it triggers -- all five providers do `prompt = continuation_content(...) if conversation_id else flatten_messages(...)`, so the target sent an 18-char delta against an 83-char history and answered a conversation it could not continue: a wrong answer with no error. `FailoverRouter._kwargs_for_candidate` now drops the id when the candidate is not the primary (the primary stays value-for-value). The second case pins the poisoning that follows: `record_turn` recorded under the bind node with the *serving* node's id (measured registry `glm / doubao-conv`), and `SessionAffinityManager` only overwrites on truthy values and has no clear API -- so one failover turn fed glm a foreign id on every turn afterwards, permanently. All eight record sites now go through `_affinity_record` / `_serving_provider`: the binding is only updated with an id when the serving node *is* the bound node, otherwise it records `None` (meaning "no replayable upstream session", so the next turn opens one and flattens). Verified red on `7d47658` with both measured symptoms.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_affinity_cooling.py # [M3] Session affinity used to have exactly two outcomes: the bound node is "available" -> stay, or it is not -> **permanently** migrate to another vendor. The availability predicate answers False for *any* soft cooldown, including a 15-second 429 backoff, so every rate-limit hiccup permanently re-married an in-progress long-running agent session to a different vendor: upstream context rebuilt, prompt cache lost, tool contract relearned -- paying tens of times the cost to dodge a window worth seconds, when backoff is the normal case (adaptive pacing exists to slow down *before* risk control). Now "can it be used" and "is it worth abandoning the whole context for" are separate judgements (`_is_provider_healthy` vs `_is_provider_retainable`): hard blocks (quarantine/auto lease, `DISABLED_PROVIDERS`, long avoidance) still hand off and strip the heterogeneous upstream id, while a short cooldown reports the new `degraded` status, keeps the binding and the upstream conversation id, and never records the session under the other vendor -- so the moment the cooldown expires the same session returns to the original node and original upstream conversation with zero handoff. Telemetry: `x-session-affinity: degraded` + `x-session-affinity-degraded: <bound node>`, and `affinity_degraded` on diagnostics; `clear()` resetting a *second* copy of the stats dict is why the new counter first raised `KeyError` -- one definition now, again.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_request_timeouts.py # [M2] Request timeout boundaries and "a timeout is not risk control": waiting for the first chunk used to be a bare `await` bounded only by httpx's 600 s read timeout, and the streaming pull only by the same *per-socket-read* timeout (which resets on every byte) -- so an upstream that trickled one byte every 599 s, or only `: keepalive`, held the node semaphore, probation lock, LB quota and an HTTP/2 connection forever while `sse_keepalive` (unbounded) kept the client from ever giving up. `GATEWAY_FIRST_CHUNK_TIMEOUT` (180 s) and `GATEWAY_STREAM_IDLE_TIMEOUT` (180 s, implemented with `ensure_future` + `wait`, never `wait_for`, so expiry is distinguishable from caller cancellation and the upstream can be closed first) now turn a half-dead upstream into a `504/upstream_timeout` the failover loop can act on; `=0` restores the old behaviour exactly. The same suite pins that a timeout is *not* risk control: `record_failure(is_timeout=True)` is decided before branch selection because `classify_interception_severity` defaults any non-401/429 input to `waf`, which gave timeouts a <=45 s cooldown -- inside the "short cooling is worth one last try" band (so the node kept being dispatched) while labelling the cause `waf` (so the dossier told the operator to log in and complete a slider on a platform that never intercepted them). Timeouts never enter the risk ledger, never start recovery probation, and the dossier reports `timeout` -- `_LIVE_ONLY_CAUSES` lets only causes the ledger *cannot* express override its dominant severity, so a 15 s rate-limit backoff can never erase a whole risk history.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_handshake_budget.py # [BUDGET] A boundary attached to the wrong layer: `GATEWAY_FIRST_CHUNK_TIMEOUT` is enforced **per candidate**, and the dispatch loop walks every candidate in the ring, so the real wait is `candidates x per-attempt` -- measured `failed after 5.0s` with a 1s timer and a dead five-node ring, i.e. **900s** at the default 180s. During that window the client receives no bytes at all (the response head has not been sent yet, so `sse_keepalive` never runs) while the LB quota and node semaphore are held by a request guaranteed to fail. `GATEWAY_HANDSHAKE_BUDGET` (180s) caps total handshake time across all candidates and composes with the per-attempt timer via `min()`, so a healthy slow handshake is untouched. The expiry verdict deliberately fires **before** the dispatch loop's `try`, not inside it, so the `except` branch cannot record a failure and a cooldown against a node that was never called. The suite asserts the **total request time** (5.08s -> 1.21s), not any single attempt's. The same work **falsified** the keepalive docstring's premise: with the real binaries, `claude -p` survived 420s and `codex exec` 600s of complete silence (the harness cap fired, not the client), so an early response head plus handshake keepalive would have solved a non-problem while breaking H4; the first test written for it was deleted after measuring, and the correction is recorded in `providers/stream_keepalive.py`.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_handshake_budget_fairness.py # [BUDGET-2] The first budget fix was itself a regression: `min(per_attempt, remaining_budget)` handed the whole remaining budget to whichever candidate was being tried, so at the defaults the first slow candidate consumed all 180s and candidates 2-5 were never attempted -- measured with the real `claude -p` against a dead ring: `[握手预算] 总预算 15.0s 已耗尽（已尝试 1 个节点）`. That is *worse* availability than the pre-fix code and it breaks the most common real fault shape ("first node slow, second node fine"). The budget is now divided fairly among the untried candidates (`remaining / (later + 1)`), so the last candidate takes the whole remainder and the total-time bound is exactly preserved; the suite pins both halves (a late healthy candidate must still get its chance, and a dead five-node ring must still finish far below `5 x per-attempt`). Also pins the honest report: a sub-second share used to print `在 0s 内未返回首包`.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_ring_exhaustion_backoff.py # [H7] The saving the budget bought was being eaten by the client's own retries. With the budget working (15s per request, 504), one real `claude -p` run still burned the full 240s harness cap and issued **8 requests**, each walking all five nodes -- 40 upstream calls against a ring already known to be dead, i.e. exactly the automated-retry storm the avoidance line exists to prevent. Controlled experiment, same server and command, **only the header differs**: 504 alone -> 240s / 8 requests / no verdict; `+ x-should-retry: false` -> **19s / 1 request / 5 upstream calls**, `EXIT=1`, with the reason shown to the user. A ring walked to completion is a deterministic conclusion and now carries the suppression header plus a `Retry-After` from the shortest remaining node cooldown (the real window, not a constant); a single-node timeout with candidates left is still not suppressed, because a different node may genuinely work. The suite also pins that a healthy response never carries the header.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_singleflight_backpressure.py # [M4] Follower subscription queues were unbounded (`asyncio.Queue()` default `maxsize=0`) and the leader fed them with `put_nowait`, which never raises on an unbounded queue -- so the `except: pass` guarding it was dead code and "what happens when it fills up" had no answer: measured, a leader broadcasting 20 000 x 512-byte frames left 20 000 frames (~10 MB) sitting in one non-consuming follower's queue, with nothing erroring. The realistic trigger is an agent client on a slow link or a momentarily stalled SSE consumer. Now a follower whose backlog reaches `GATEWAY_SINGLEFLIGHT_BACKLOG` (default 256) is detached -- its queued frames drained, a `RuntimeError` placed in its queue so `stream_follower_gen` raises it to that client -- while the leader, the replay history and the other followers are untouched; blocking the leader instead would let one slow client stall the whole flight, and silently truncating would hand the client a half answer it reads as complete. Telemetry: `followers_detached` + `follower_backlog_limit` on `/v1/diagnostics`. The suite's first version was itself wrong: a leader that never awaits between frames legitimately detaches even a synchronously-draining follower, so it now models a *slow but consuming* follower and asserts a synchronously-draining follower on the same flight is not detached.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_credential_renewal_discrimination.py # [REFRESH-VERIFY] The evidence for "this credential was renewed" was a file write, not a content change. `_FINGERPRINT_FIELDS` contained `captured_at`, and `Session.save()` refreshes it on **every** save -- so a headless refresh that found the same stale token sitting in the browser profile and re-saved it changed the fingerprint while changing nothing upstream. Measured end-to-end with the real `CooldownTracker` + `CredentialRenewalTracker`: a 401 produced a 60s cooldown, one no-op re-save made `renewed_providers()` report the node renewed, and `release_renewed_credentials_now()` reset the cooldown to **0** -- i.e. a refresh that fixed nothing lifted the OPT-026 avoidance built for "keep retrying with the same dead credential" and put the node straight back in front of upstream. It also poisons OPT-027's discriminator: `note_auth_failure` reads "same credential + one empty re-save" as "a renewed credential was still rejected" and infers an account-level ban. The fingerprint is now the **credential content** (token / cookie / user_id / device_id); `captured_at` stays on `Session` where it serves `age` and freshness. Both directions are pinned -- a real token change and a cookie-only rotation (the common case when a re-login keeps the token) must still count as renewals -- because narrowing the hash until nothing is ever a renewal would disable the whole OPT-026/027/029 release chain. `tests/test_credential_renewal.py`'s original case asserted the defect verbatim ("重登必然刷新 captured_at，指纹必须随之改变") and was **corrected**, not worked around.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_probe_conversation_gc.py # [PROBE-GC] The health probe is traffic the gateway makes *about itself*, and it was the one producer of upstream conversations nobody reclaimed: `probe_provider` calls `provider.chat(..., conversation_id=None)`, so every upstream opens a temporary session, and it **discarded the returned `conversation_id`** -- all three `ConversationGC.schedule` call sites live in `server.py`'s request paths, none in `healing/monitor.py`. At the default `HEAL_CHECK_INTERVAL=300` across five nodes that is **1440 probe conversations per day**, each a real "hi" dialog left in the user's web sidebar: it buries the actual anonymous-call sessions, and "one 'hi' every 60 seconds" is itself the timing signature risk control scores on. The stream itself was never leaked (measured: the async generator's `finally` runs), so this is an upstream-accounting leak -- exactly the face the anti-bot system can see. Fixing it required consuming the stream to the end, because deepseek/doubao/glm only report `conversation_id` in the **trailing** metadata frame while the probe returned after the first frame; that in turn let the probe use `stream_completion.observe()` for a real truncation verdict (absent key = unknown, so the three marker-less protocols are untouched). Two guards were required by that change and are pinned here: a handshake that succeeds but whose answer is long enough to hit the 15s cap must stay **healthy** (all five upstreams ignore `max_tokens`, so a long answer is normal -- measured: the naive version reported `ok=False`, indistinguishable from a dead node), and a protocol with no completion marker must never be judged truncated.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_login_sweep.py # [LOGIN-SWEEP] `login.py all` is the command the gateway's own refusals tell the operator to run (`credential_watch.py:230`, `failover.py:2766`) and the only manual entry point for lifting the OPT-026/027/029 avoidances -- yet it expanded "all" into five fixed names and called each unconditionally, never asking whether that node should be touched. Measured: with a 72h operator lease on deepseek and `DISABLED_PROVIDERS=kimi`, the touch order was `['qwen','deepseek','doubao','kimi','glm']`. That contradicts the gateway's own dossier, whose `ban` answer is verbatim "cannot be bypassed, re-login is useless -- the ban binds the account, not the token; any attempt only extends it; the only correct strategy is zero touches inside the window": the gateway was instructing the operator to run a command that touches an account it had itself judged "every touch makes it worse" -- the OPT-035 defect class (advice drifting from code), on the advice side. The verdict must distinguish causes rather than skip anything avoiding: `quarantined`/`disabled` are skipped (re-login repairs neither), while `cooldown` still logs in, because a cooldown is exactly what re-login repairs -- OPT-029's long human-verification avoidance is lifted only by a human completing the slider, so skipping coolings would close the one repair path (the self-lock OPT-026 already fixed once). An explicitly named provider always runs: naming one is the human overriding the gateway. It reuses `CooldownTracker.touch_avoidance_reason()` instead of writing a second predicate (one declaration, one reading place), is read-only with zero upstream calls, and falls back to "log in normally" on any query failure so a broken diagnostic can never turn `all` into a no-op.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_provider_frame_robustness.py # [STREAM-AUDIT] Five upstream adapters, one frame-shape rule: `data: []` / `data: null` / `data: 42` / `data: "text"` are **legal JSON** and parse fine, then `.get()` on them raises `AttributeError` -- one odd frame turns the whole turn into a 502 with no cause the client can read. Four of the five adapters already had the `isinstance(obj, dict)` guard ([C3] fixed Kimi and GLM for exactly this); **DeepSeek did not** -- `_parse_sse` went straight from `json.loads` to `obj.get("v")`, measured raising `AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'get'` at `providers/deepseek/provider.py:269` on a single `data: []` frame. The behavioural case injects non-object frames *around* a real content frame and asserts the content still arrives (the guard must skip, not abort). The second case is structural and deliberately so: a behavioural test only covers the shapes I thought of, while "every adapter declares the guard" is the property that makes the *next* adapter's omission visible at review time; verified non-vacuous by removing doubao's guard and watching it go red. Note the file-resolution trap the first version hit: `providers/<name>_provider.py` is a 182-byte compatibility shim, so the real source is `providers/<name>/provider.py` -- reading the shim made all five report missing.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_singleflight_constraints.py # [S3] A defect *introduced* by the fixes that made client constraints effective. SingleFlight hands every follower the leader's answer, so an identical fingerprint asserts the two requests' correct answers are interchangeable -- yet `compute_key` omitted `tool_choice`, `stop`/`stop_sequences`, `response_format`/`text.format`, `parallel_tool_calls` and `max_tokens`. That was harmless when those fields were parsed and never read (they truly did not change the output); sections 34b/35 then made each of them change the output, and the key was never updated. So a request declaring `tool_choice:"none"` can be handed a tool call, and one declaring `stop` can be handed uncut text -- a wrong answer with no error anywhere. Adding fields is the only safe direction (a more specific key reduces sharing, it can never cause a wrong share); `None` == `{}` and `sort_keys=True` keep every existing call site value-for-value, and a case asserts genuinely identical requests still dedup, since a key that never dedups would be the worse regression. The suite also pins all three protocol call sites, because widening the signature alone is a fix that passes every direct-call unit test while the real request path still drops the constraints.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_singleflight_session_identity.py # [SINGLEFLIGHT] The dedup key's one correctness rule -- identical fingerprint means the two requests' correct answers are interchangeable -- applied to **session identity**. `compute_key` covered provider/model/messages/tools/temperature/thinking/stream/constraints but not *which conversation this is*, and that is what decides whether the prefix lives upstream or in the prompt: a bound session continues (delta only), a fresh one flattens the whole history. Their `messages` can be byte-identical -- a Codex/Claude Code sub-agent sends the same instruction text and declares the same tools as its parent -- so the fingerprints matched and the child was handed the parent's continuation answer while its own upstream conversation was never created (measured: one upstream call, B's registry entry empty, yet B's response said `x-session-affinity: bound`). A wrong answer with no error. `session_key` / `upstream_conv_id` now enter the key; anonymous callers pass `None` for both, so they stay value-for-value and still dedup -- asserted by a second case, because a key so specific that nothing ever dedups is the worse regression (the [S3] rule).
.venv/bin/python tests/test_budget_resource_reclaim.py # [BUDGET-3] Two misjudgements on the new budget path, both the same shape as the rest of this file: a decision of *ours* recorded as a fault of the upstream. (a) A timeout **capped by our own budget** was booked as a node failure -- the share is `remaining / untried`, so at the defaults the first candidate gets 36s, while a reasoning model legitimately thinking for tens of seconds is healthy (`_first_chunk_timeout`'s own docstring says so); measured share 0.1s against a node answering in 0.4s -> `qwen 冷却 60.0s / 成因 timeout / failure_count 1`, and every later request routed around an innocent node. `BudgetCappedTimeout` marks the attempt as ceiling-cut-by-the-budget: failover continues, but no cooldown and no failure count. (b) `max(share, 0.05)` read as a safety net but converted "not enough budget" into "spend 50ms" -- measured budget 0.12s over five candidates -> five upstream calls each cut at 0.05s, five false 60s cooldowns, and an outcome identical to a zero-upstream refusal; against these five upstreams a 50ms call can never succeed, so it buys nothing but a credentialed touch. `share < _MIN_HANDSHAKE_ATTEMPT_SECONDS` now raises `BudgetExhaustedRefusal` (`zero_upstream`) -- measured 0 upstream calls, 0 cooldowns. The same suite audits the resource side of the whole path (opened == closed per node, semaphores value-for-value, `active_concurrency == 0` on all three protocols) and records a test bug worth remembering: the ASGI case took its semaphore baseline *after* driving the request, i.e. compared the result with itself, so it would have stayed green through a real leak.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_budget_capped_evidence.py # [BUDGET-4] The regression the previous budget fix introduced, found by my own probe rather than by a user report: after `[BUDGET-3]` (correctly) stopped convicting a node for a timeout *our own budget* capped, it kept **no record at all**. Measured with a five-node ring, qwen hung, the rest instant, budget 5s -> first share 1.0s, five turns: `[1.06, 1.02, 1.03, 1.06, 1.02]`, `qwen_calls` 1->5, `cooldown=0.0s`, `failures=0` on every turn -- every client request pays a full share for a node already known not to answer, forever, and each of those waits issues a real credentialed upstream call ("every request is one second slower", with no convergence). The correct semantics sit between the two extremes and the discriminator is a **different fact**, not a looser threshold: one capped timeout is not evidence of a node fault, but "N times in a row, within the entire share we gave it, it produced no first chunk" is evidence that **this candidate is slower than its share**. `note_budget_capped()` keeps that streak (persisted; a restartable counter is no counter) and at `GATEWAY_BUDGET_CAPPED_LIMIT` (default 3) books one real `timeout` cooldown -- no ledger entry, no probation, no `waf`/`auth` cause, so the dossier never sends the operator to complete a captcha on a platform that never intercepted them; `record_success` clears the streak. Measured after: `[1.06, 1.02, 1.02, 0.01, 0.01]`, qwen touched 3 times instead of 5. **The measurement had to move out of the router, and that is the transferable lesson**: the first version called `execute_chat` directly and stayed red even after a correct fix, because `execute_chat` still dispatches to a primary in a *short* soft cooldown (OPT-021's deliberate last-resort) -- the layer that stops paying for the hung node is the load balancer above it, so the suite now drives the real HTTP path and measures per-turn client latency. `GATEWAY_BUDGET_CAPPED_LIMIT=0` restores the old behaviour value-for-value (verified `[1.06, 1.02, 1.02, 1.18, 1.02]`, `qwen_calls=5`, cooldown 0), and `budget_capped` on `/v1/diagnostics` makes "is this mechanism doing anything" observable rather than inferred from logs. **[BUDGET-5]** then closed the hole that fix left: clearing the streak on escalation regranted `GATEWAY_BUDGET_CAPPED_LIMIT` free full-share attempts every cooldown cycle, so a request gap longer than the cooldown collapsed back to "every request pays a full share" — measured 12/40 stalled turns vs. 7/40 once the streak saturates at the threshold and the first post-cooldown attempt becomes the canary (it is also the only recovery probe: one success clears the streak entirely).
.venv/bin/python tests/test_stream_completion.py # [S2] A stream that ends without its protocol's completion marker was indistinguishable from a normal finish: `record_success` is recorded at first chunk and never revisited at exhaustion (failure count stays 0, so the node stays primary and the user sees "the reply stops halfway" repeatedly), all five providers emit an unconditional trailing metadata frame, and the protocol handler then appends `finish_reason: "stop"` + `[DONE]` -- a self-declared-complete half answer, and for an agent a half ```tool_call fence that parses to nothing with no error anywhere. The investigation contradicted the hypothesis: only qwen (`status == complete`) and kimi (`all_done`) have a marker that is read; doubao's `is_finish` is an *outbound request* field and glm's `finish` does not exist -- so the verdict is tri-state (seen / not-seen / **no marker = unknown**), and "absent means unknown, not truncated" is the assertion that keeps the three marker-less providers working. The suite pins the reporting set as exactly {qwen, kimi} in **both** directions (a scripted-upstream test would stay green while production never sets the key; and nobody may add a fabricated marker for a protocol that has none), and asserts a truncation is not filed as a risk interception -- the first working version recorded it as `waf`, because the severity classifier defaults any non-401/429 to waf, which would send the operator to complete a captcha that never happened ([M2]'s defect, one cause over).
.venv/bin/python tests/test_pacing_race.py # [M1] The per-node `min_call_interval` (qwen 0.3s / deepseek 0.4s / kimi 0.35s) was enforced by **two** mechanisms and therefore by neither: `server.py` held a wait-then-immediately-release `traffic_pacer.acquire(...)` that exited before `execute_chat` made the real upstream call (delaying only its own request, constraining no concurrent caller, yet reporting its wait as `x-risk-pacing-delay-ms` -- telemetry asserting a throttle that never happened), while `apply_pacing_and_acquire` read `_last_call_time`, computed `elapsed`, slept and wrote back **all outside any lock**, so concurrent callers read one `last` and were released together. Measured: three concurrent dispatches at 0.30s produced upstream call stamps `[0.0, 0.301, 0.301]` -- two in the same millisecond on one account, the automation signature the four unflagged vendors must not emit. Pacing is now a single authority adjacent to the upstream call, and the correctness is in **two** stages, not one: a locked slot reservation (so concurrent callers do not share a gap) plus a locked re-check at the release moment against the real clock -- a reservation alone measured `[0.0, 0.398, 0.601]` (min gap 0.203s < 0.240s) because a caller that oversleeps eats the next slot, and a slot is a prediction while risk control measures the real interval. The suite asserts the staircase on *upstream call start times*, that 4 callers x 0.12s take >= 0.36s rather than 0.12s, and (by source assertion) that no second clock can be reintroduced into `server.py`.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_responses_history_tools.py # [TOOL-LOOP] Responses history replay of tool calls in the two shapes real clients use -- `message.tool_calls` and `role:"tool"` message items -- both of which were silently discarded: the model saw `[Tool Result for c1]` with no record of having made call c1, breaking the agent loop with no error anywhere. Both now replay as an assistant call with the id and arguments preserved and a result tagged with that id; a case asserts `message.tool_calls` and `function_call` render **byte-identically**, so one call cannot grow two rendering rules; parallel calls in a single message all survive; and an end-to-end case checks the call reached the prompt the upstream actually received.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_content_normalization.py # [PROTOCOL] Content a client may legally send that the gateway mishandled: attachments were **silently dropped** on all three protocols (an OpenAI `image_url` / `input_file`, a Responses `input_image` / `input_file`, an Anthropic `image` reduced to a bare `[Image: png]` type label) so the model answered confidently about an attachment it never received — they now reach the prompt as explicit `[Image omitted: ...]` / `[File omitted: <name>]` placeholders, which is the difference between "I cannot see images" and a hallucination; `{"type": "text", "text": null}` crashed with `TypeError: sequence item 0: expected str instance` in **two** copies of the extractor (`base.text_of` and `prompt_optimizer._extract_text_robust`, the latter running before routing, so a legal request was a 500) and must never be echoed as the string `None`; and an OpenAI `system` parts list was f-string'd into a Python repr (`[{'type': 'text', ...}]`) ahead of the tool contract — the model read client speech as noise. The image/file placeholders are asserted end-to-end against the prompt the upstream actually receives, not just at the helper.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_responses_custom_tools.py # [TOOL-LOOP] Responses freeform/custom tools (Codex CLI's `apply_patch`): the declaration gets a real `{"input": "<raw text>"}` contract instead of `Args: {}` (the model was told the tool takes no arguments, so it never called it), a replayed `custom_tool_call` stays an **assistant** tool call with the patch preserved character-for-character instead of becoming a raw-JSON **user** message the model cannot recognise as its own, a replayed `custom_tool_call_output` is a `[Tool Result for <id>]` and not a user turn, the non-streaming completion emits `custom_tool_call` (not a `function_call` the client silently drops) — including the JSON-string `arguments` shape that would have handed `apply_patch` a quoted `\n`-escaped wrapper — and the streaming path emits `response.custom_tool_call_input.delta`/`.done` while a plain function tool still gets `function_call_arguments.*`
.venv/bin/python tests/test_parallel_tool_calls.py # [TOOL-LOOP] Two tool calls written **back to back** (zero separator — the exact byte shape real codex 0.145.0 produces) must both reach the client. The shared fence regex closed greedily (`\n?```+`), so the first block's close ate the second block's *opening* backticks and `finditer` resumed past them: the second call vanished with no error on all three protocols. The close is now lazy (`\`{3,}?`) plus a negative lookahead so it cannot stop at the start of a new block, with `(?![A-Za-z0-9_])` keeping `tool_calling`/`json5x` out of the language family; the alternation was extracted to `_TOOL_FENCE_LANG` so opener and lookahead cannot drift. Asserted at the protocol boundary (the client really receives two `tool_calls` / two `tool_use` with distinct ids / two `function_call`s with distinct `call_id`s for all three separators), on the streaming accumulation path, on three adjacent blocks, and that `parallel_tool_calls:false` still trims to the first. Zero regression proved over a shape matrix (four backticks, every language alias, unclosed fence, inline prose backticks): only the back-to-back case changes.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_report_read_only.py # [PROTOCOL] A **read** endpoint that wrote the repository: `GET /v1/reports/latest` (what the frontend calls when the report modal opens) re-wrote three git-tracked files under `reports/` on every call — content byte-identical, `generated_at` refreshed, so `git status` was permanently dirty and every `reports/` diff was noise. Root cause was structural, not a stray write: the path was registered **twice**, and the first registration (`get_tasks_evaluation`, an *action* that aggregates tasks and persists) shadowed the second (`get_latest_optimization_report`, whose "read the snapshot, generate only on miss" contract is exactly the read-only behaviour wanted) — dead code that looked alive. Measured: 4.11 ms median of synchronous dict assembly plus three file writes **on the event loop**, for a handler that is a pure read. Now each path has exactly one handler, the read path serves the stored snapshot verbatim and generates with `persist=False` **in a worker thread** (`anyio.to_thread`), `TaskEvaluator`'s `project_root` actually decides where it writes (it was decorative — the save path used the module-global `REPORTS_DIR`, so isolation depended on the caller happening to run under `patch()`), and `TESTING=1` routes the store to a temp dir per the OPT-019 convention (the dispatcher integration case wrote into the real checkout on every run). The duplicate-registration assertion is deliberately **ring-wide** (`test_no_path_is_registered_twice`, all 56 routes) rather than scoped to these two paths: a second registration never errors or warns, it just quietly replaces a handler one day, and the only symptom is "I changed it and nothing happened". All six cases verified red on the pre-fix code, including one that first passed **vacuously** — it asserted "nothing was written to the temp dir" while the old code wrote to the real repo instead, so it was tightened to assert the repo was untouched too.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_responses_previous_id.py # [PROTOCOL] The Responses field the gateway accepted and then ignored: `previous_response_id` (client sends only the delta, the server is supposed to hold the history) was undeclared on `ResponsesRequest` and survived only through `extra="allow"`, read by nothing. Measured: `{"previous_response_id":"resp_abc123","input":"what did I ask before?"}` -> HTTP 200 and the upstream prompt was exactly `[{'role': 'user', 'content': 'what did I ask before?'}]` — the model answered a question with no antecedent, confidently, with no error anywhere, while the client's contract is that the server supplies the history. The gateway persists no response objects, so *any* value of this field is provably unresolvable; "silently start a fresh session" is precisely the measured behaviour. The fix refuses with `404` + `code: previous_response_not_found`, which is the code real SDKs and gateways already have recovery logic for (resend with full history) — silently ignoring it means they never reach that path. Zero upstream (H4): the refusal sits before any provider is constructed, and the suite asserts the provider factory was never called. The second case pins zero regression for requests that omit the field.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_anthropic_stream.py # [ANTHROPIC-STREAM] The Claude Code wire contract: block indices must start once, in order, and stop exactly once — the tools path used to emit start(1,text) immediately followed by start(1,thinking) when the model alternated text and reasoning (the normal shape of an agent turn), an illegal stream the SDK either rejects or misattributes; also thinking/text exclusivity, empty-response skeleton, tool_use + input_json_delta with stop_reason=tool_use, message_start first / message_stop last, and usage on the terminal delta
.venv/bin/python tests/test_health_truthfulness.py # [HEALTH-TRUTH] `/health` was the third place claiming a capability the dispatcher does not have: it returned `"status": "ok"` unconditionally, so a fresh clone (`session/` empty, all five nodes `offline`/unconfigured) advertised a gateway that cannot serve a single request. Measured: HTTP 200 / `status: ok` / `qwen_configured: false … glm_configured: false` / all five `offline`. It is the only entry point operators, monitoring probes and agent setup scripts use to decide "is it up", so `ok` tells everyone to start sending requests that all end in 401 with nothing in the response pointing at the cause. Now `status` reflects **whether anything can really be served**, with `status_reason` naming the remedy (`login.py all`), `dispatchable_providers`, and a per-node `dispatchable` — and that field is derived from the **same** `evaluate_dispatch_gate()` the dispatcher asks (`configured and gate.dispatchable`), not from a second definition. This is OPT-032/033/035's defect class (a preview/dossier saying dispatchable while the boundary refuses) moved onto the readiness surface. The suite asserts the agreement as a **property over every node × state** (healthy / no-credentials / disabled) rather than enumerating scenarios, because each past drift was one missed cell; short soft coolings stay `ok` (demote-only, OPT-021's last-resort availability preserved). Pre-existing `test_healing.py:187` asserted exactly the lie (`status == "ok"` on this machine's empty `session/`) and was corrected to the new contract, not worked around.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_concurrency_soak.py # [SOAK] The five suites above each prove one mechanism against one crafted case; this one asks whether they hold **together under load**, through the real ASGI stack with scripted upstreams and zero network. Measured: a 14-request ring soak peaks per node at qwen 3 / doubao 5 / glm 4 / deepseek 1 / kimi 1 — all ≤ their configured `PROVIDER_RISK_SPECS` limits, spread across all five (a single-node soak with qwen alone peaks at **exactly 3**, its limit; below 3 would mean no concurrency happened at all, so the assertion is two-sided). A 3×12 disconnect storm returns every resource every round — 36 upstream streams opened, node semaphores value-for-value, `LB=0`, `inflight=0`, **no accumulation across rounds**. Keepalive at 0.35 s of upstream silence emits 12 `: keepalive` frames and the payload `['a','b','c']` survives intact and ordered. Singleflight fanout with one slow follower detaches it while the leader stays unstalled (0.62 s). **Two harness requirements worth recording, both of which produced a false reading first**: requests must be **staggered**, because `select_route` reads LB `active_concurrency` acquired *after* `execute_chat`, so same-tick requests all see 0 and pile onto one node (the first version put all 14 on qwen); and each case needs its **own event loop**, because `asyncio.Semaphore` binds on first use — the global router's semaphores bind in case 1 and case 2 then raises `bound to a different event loop`, surfaced as a 502 that cools the node and poisons later measurements.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_unconfigured_credentials.py # [DAY-ZERO] The state every new user hits first: after `git clone`, `session/` is empty but the gateway starts, and `_make_provider` raises a **local** `ProviderError(status=401, err_type="auth_error")` ("未检测到 Qwen 令牌…"). `execute_chat` only reads `exc.status`, so a conclusion that never left the machine was handed to `record_failure` as an upstream HTTP 401 — the heaviest, most patient tier there is (`auth`: 1800s × 2ⁿ, capped 24h). Measured: one agent-CLI retry (real `claude -p` issues 7 in 40s) puts all five nodes into 30-minute auth avoidance with **zero upstream calls**. The worse half is a self-lock: `note_auth_failure()` writes no fingerprint record when the credential file is *absent*, so OPT-026's renewal release (`renewed_providers()`, which only scans recorded nodes) can never find the node — the operator runs the `login.py` the gateway itself told them to run, and nothing is released. The suite pins the four things that must hold together: no ledger entry / no probation episode / no ring-wide outage trip; the node is still **not dispatchable** (a guard against fixing it into "unconfigured = healthy"); the avoidance is **not** the `auth` tier (`< 600s` — a threshold deliberately in the vacuum band, since 1800 reads back as 1799.9 and `< 1800` would pass with the defect present, the OPT-031 trap); and adding a credential file immediately restores the node. The E2E case drives the real ASGI stack and asserts **state** (ledger count, worst cooldown), not the tripwire's construction count — construction is purely local, so counting it would be [M1]'s "an assertion about a window that is not the window the code protects". Measured RED pre-fix: one request → 3 ledger entries (qwen/glm/doubao) + ring-wide outage tripped.
.venv/bin/python tests/test_e2e_verdict.py # [E2E-VERDICT] The e2e runner's three verdict blocks (single / parallel / N sessions) each built their own field list, so the rule "at one round no per-round marker exists" had been learned in one block and not the others: a green `--write` run printed `tool_markers=0/1` beside `PASS` while its per-client data read `written='e2e-ok', content_ok=True` — a failure-shaped number on a run with nothing wrong, and the run's strongest evidence missing from the only line most runs are read through. The parallel and sessions lines also dropped `exit` and `conversation_reused` (the §66/67 session-reuse proof), and the sessions line reported a shared body count as if it were per-session. One formatter, `_verdict_line`, is now used by all three, and the suite asserts the structural rule (exactly one definition, three call sites, no hand-built field list anywhere else) — because a behaviour test only covers the modes I thought to construct, which is how this survived.

### Official Claude Code CLI & Codex CLI Integration

#### 1. Official Claude Code CLI Integration
Point the official `claude` CLI (or any Anthropic SDK application) to the local gateway:
```bash
ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000 \
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=test-key \
ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL=claude-3-5-haiku-20241022 \
CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC=1 \
claude -p "Hello" --model claude-3-7-sonnet
```

#### 2. Codex CLI Integration (OpenAI Responses Protocol)
Point the official `codex` CLI to the local gateway's `/v1` endpoint.

**`OPENAI_BASE_URL` alone does NOT work** — `codex` 0.145.0 ignores it (measured: `ModelProviderInfo { base_url: None }`, dials `wss://api.openai.com/v1/responses`; the string does not occur in the binary). A custom provider must be declared in `$CODEX_HOME/config.toml`:

```toml
model_provider = "unipivot"
model = "gpt-5.5"
approval_policy = "never"
sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"

[model_providers.unipivot]
name = "unipivot"
base_url = "http://127.0.0.1:8000/v1"
wire_api = "responses"
requires_openai_auth = false
```

```bash
CODEX_HOME=/path/to/that/dir codex exec "Implement a binary search function in python"
```

#### 3. Real-Binary End-to-End Acceptance (the only test that answers "do agents actually work?")
```bash
.venv/bin/python scripts/e2e/run_agent_loop.py                  # both binaries, if installed
.venv/bin/python scripts/e2e/run_agent_loop.py --client codex   # one arm
.venv/bin/python scripts/e2e/run_agent_loop.py --rounds 10      # a long agent session
.venv/bin/python scripts/e2e/run_agent_loop.py --parallel --rounds 10  # both agents, ONE gateway
.venv/bin/python scripts/e2e/run_agent_loop.py --parallel --client claude --sessions 3 --rounds 8  # N sessions, ONE account
.venv/bin/python scripts/e2e/run_agent_loop.py --keep           # keep artifacts to inspect
```
A pass requires **four** independent proofs, not the client's stdout: a marker file really created on disk by the real binary, **one marker per round** (so "round *n* executed" is proven, not inferred), at least two upstream bodies captured (a tool result came back), and the captured prompt carrying both the tool contract and `[Tool Result for <id>]`. A missing binary is SKIPPED, never counted as a pass. This is not part of the offline suite — it needs the real `claude` / `codex` binaries and takes ~30s per arm.

**`--parallel` is the setup developers actually run** — two agents in two terminals on one machine — and nothing else in the repository exercises it end to end. Both binaries drive the *same* gateway process concurrently for N rounds each, so their requests interleave through the same connection pool, the same per-node concurrency semaphores, the same singleflight manager and the same session-affinity registry. Measured (`--parallel --rounds 10`): `claude` 11 bodies / 10/10 round markers, `codex` 11 bodies / 10/10, **22 upstream bodies total**, both `EXIT=0`, no round lost, no cross-client contamination.

**`--parallel --client <name> --sessions N` is the harder shape, and the one the risk line exists for**: N concurrent sessions of the *same* binary, all routed to the **same upstream account**. Two different vendors sharing one gateway is easy; "one account, several parallel sessions" is the automation signature per-node semaphores and pacing exist to bound. Measured: `--client claude --sessions 3 --rounds 8` → all three sessions 8/8 round markers, **27 upstream bodies**, all `EXIT=0`.

Attribution cannot use the tool name here — both sessions declare `Bash` — so it uses the **filesystem**: each session runs in its own working directory and the scripted upstream emits a *relative* marker path (`e2e_marks/ROUND_n`), which resolves per session with no coordination and cannot be forged by the model. Verified structurally rather than assumed: the three sessions' `ROUND_1` files are **three distinct inodes**, so a single shared directory cannot be passing for all three. Because the bodies genuinely cannot be split per session, the result reports `shared_bodies` + `attributable_by: "marker-directory"` instead of inventing a per-session count — printing `bodies=12` for each of two 6-request sessions would be a number that is really the sum.

**A green verdict alone does not prove concurrency, so it is measured.** Two *sequential* runs produce a byte-identical verdict — same markers, same body counts — which means the marker/body assertions cannot distinguish interleaving from serialization. Worse, an early version of `--parallel` did not launch both binaries concurrently at all and every check still passed. The scripted upstream therefore logs a wall-clock `t` per upstream call, and the runner derives two numbers from them: the **overlap window** (ms during which both clients had a call in flight) and the **interleaving sequence** (`C`/`X` per call in time order, plus switch count). Measured on real runs: **1317–1461 ms of overlap** and **15–21 client switches across 22 bodies** (one run came out exactly `XCXCXC…`, 21 alternations — every call switched client). Either check failing is a run failure, because "0 ms of overlap" means the feature under test was never exercised. This is the same discipline the rest of the file applies to product code: *make the claim measurable, or the green light means nothing.*

Attribution is the design problem, and it is the trap worth remembering: once both clients share one upstream log, *"which client ran this round"* cannot be read from the client itself. It is read from the **tool each request declared** (`Bash` ⇒ claude, `exec_command` ⇒ codex) inside `scripted_upstream._client_tag()`, and the marker paths are derived the same way. Without that the two clients write into one directory and "claude ran 10 rounds" is satisfiable by codex's rounds — a green run measuring nothing. Each client also gets its own working directory for the same reason.

---

## Native Models & Alias Mapping Matrix

Incoming model requests are parsed via `server.py:resolve_model()`. The gateway routes models to one of the five backend providers according to specific rules and environment variables (`CLAUDE_BACKEND`, `OPENAI_BACKEND`, default `qwen`):

### 1. Native Provider Models

| Provider | Supported Models / Aliases | Target Web Model / Characteristic |
|---|---|---|
| **通义千问 (Qwen)** | `qwen`, `qwen-plus`, `qwen3.7`, `qwen-max`, `qwen3.7-max`, `qwen-flash`, `qwen3.6-flash`, `qwen3.6` | `Qwen` (default), `Qwen3.7-Max` (reasoning/flagship), `Qwen3.6-Flash` (speed) |
| **DeepSeek** | `deepseek`, `deepseek-chat`, `deepseek-instant`, `deepseek-reasoner`, `deepseek-r1`, `deepseek-expert` | `deepseek-chat` (flagship/general), `deepseek-reasoner` (R1 reasoning) |
| **豆包 (Doubao)** | `doubao`, `doubao-pro`, `doubao-pro-chat`, `doubao-lite`, `doubao-think`, `doubao-deep-think`, `doubao-expert` | `doubao-pro` (default), `doubao-think` (thinking), `doubao-lite` (speed) |
| **月之暗面 (Kimi)** | `kimi`, `kimi-chat`, `kimi-latest`, `kimi-explore`, `kimi-think`, `kimi-math`, `kimi-k1` | `kimi` (standard), `kimi-explore` (deep search/thinking), `kimi-math` |
| **智谱清言 (GLM)** | `glm`, `chatglm`, `glm-4`, `glm-4-plus`, `glm-4-air`, `glm-4-flash`, `glm-4-long`, `glm-zero-preview`, `glm-think` | `glm-4-plus` (flagship), `glm-4-flash` (speed), `glm-zero-preview` (thinking) |

| Client Model Name | Qwen (Default) | DeepSeek (`CLAUDE_BACKEND=deepseek`) | 豆包 (`CLAUDE_BACKEND=doubao`) | Kimi (`CLAUDE_BACKEND=kimi`) | GLM (`CLAUDE_BACKEND=glm`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `claude-3-7-sonnet` / `claude-3-opus` | `Qwen3.7-Max` | `deepseek-reasoner` | `doubao-think` | `kimi-explore` | `glm-zero-preview` |
| `claude-3-5-sonnet` | `Qwen` | `deepseek-chat` | `doubao-pro` | `kimi` | `glm-4-plus` |

## Architecture & Code Structure

The repository is a local multi-protocol API gateway (`server.py`) supporting three standard protocols:
- **OpenAI Chat Completions** (`/v1/chat/completions`, `/chat/completions`)
- **Anthropic Messages** (`/v1/messages`, `/messages`, `/v1/messages/count_tokens`)
- **OpenAI Responses** (`/v1/responses`, `/responses` for Codex CLI)

### Core Subsystems
1. **Peer Capability Rings & Failover Grid (`providers/failover.py`)**:
   - Manages three capability rings: `REASONING`, `FLAGSHIP`, and `SPEED`.
   - Ensures transparent failover before the first token chunk is emitted, preventing half-packet pollution.
   - Dynamic cooldown avoidance for WAF (e.g., RGV587) and 429 rate limits, with exponential backoff (30s~120s).
   - Adaptive pacing, jitter injection, and per-provider concurrency limiting via semaphores.

2. **Self-Healing & Auto-Hotfix Brain (`healing/`)**:
   - `HealthMonitor` (`healing/monitor.py`): Performs non-blocking background probes (interval: `HEAL_CHECK_INTERVAL`, default 300s). Transitions state across `healthy`, `degraded`, `recovering`, and `offline`.
   - `HealingEngine` (`healing/healer.py`): Upon upstream protocol failure, elects the highest-performing healthy model to craft repair prompts and dispatches local CLI repair agents.
   - `AgentRunner` (`healing/agent_runner.py`): Discovers and bootstraps local `claude` or `codex` CLI environments, launching isolated subagents to update code and run tests.

3. **HTTP/2 Connection Pool Management (`providers/http_client.py`)**:
   - Global singleton `AsyncClient` configured with connection limits (max 100 conns, 40 keep-alive, 30s expiry).
   - Multiplexing enabled via `http2=True`.

4. **Atomic Credential Store (`session_store.py`)**:
   - Resolves credentials with priority: `Environment Variables > session/<provider>.json > None`.
   - Thread-safe POSIX atomic write pattern (`os.replace` + `os.fsync`) with strict `0o700` directory and `0o600` file permissions.

5. **Tool / Function Calling & Token Counting**:
   - `anthropic_compat.py`: Balanced bracket parser supporting deeply nested JSON tool calls and XML tool syntax.
   - `responses_compat.py`: Standard OpenAI Responses protocol serializer with `function_call_arguments.delta` / `done`.
   - `base.py`: Token estimation (`_est_tokens`) supporting bilingual CJK weighting.

6. **Smart Session Affinity & State Handoff Engine (`providers/session_affinity.py`)**:
   - Manages LRU and TTL-based conversation tracking to pin multi-turn agent sessions to the same upstream provider.
   - Detects provider degradation, WAF cooldown, or failure and performs seamless cross-model state handoff with heterogeneous upstream conversation ID stripping.
   - Emits `x-session-affinity`, `x-session-handoff-from`, and `x-session-turns` telemetry headers across all protocols.

7. **SingleFlight & Stream Fanout Engine (`providers/singleflight.py`)**:
   - Computes deterministic cross-protocol SHA-256 fingerprints across wire parameters and payload semantics.
   - Leader/Follower flight groups with streaming multi-subscriber fanout and historical replay buffers.
   - Non-streaming response coalescing with `x-singleflight: leader | follower` telemetry headers and zero duplicate upstream calls.

8. **Disconnect Guard & Upstream Reclamation (`providers/disconnect_guard.py`)**:
   - Propagates client disconnects to the upstream async generator and explicitly closes it, releasing the HTTP/2 connection slot and upstream concurrency quota immediately instead of letting an abandoned stream drain quota.
   - Guarantees a terminal state for the SingleFlight flight group on every exit path, including `CancelledError`, and reclaims flight groups whose leader vanished so followers can never hang.
   - Emits `x-disconnect-guard` telemetry headers across all protocols.

9. **Token Count Cache (`providers/token_counter_cache.py`)**:
   - Memoizes the deterministic `/v1/messages/count_tokens` result behind a bounded LRU, eliminating repeated O(n) estimation on long conversations.

10. **Request Lifecycle Finalization & Quota Reclamation (`providers/singleflight.py:terminate_aborted_group`)**:
    - Guarantees the non-streaming SingleFlight leader path terminates its flight group on every exit, including client aborts (`CancelledError` is a `BaseException`, so `except Exception` never fires) — without it the group stays non-terminal forever, hanging every follower.
    - Makes the `load_balancer` concurrency slot deterministically idempotent-released on abort; the Responses endpoint previously had no `finally`, so an aborted request leaked its slot permanently and the balancer would shed away from that provider until restart.
    - See `tests/test_lifecycle.py`.

11. **Risk-Control Ledger & Durable Cooldown (`providers/risk_ledger.py`)**:
    - Persists each provider's cooldown deadline and consecutive-failure count with a POSIX atomic write (`os.replace` + `os.fsync`, dir `0o700` / file `0o600`), so a restart no longer wipes avoidance and immediately re-pokes a node that upstream has already risk-banned (the ban window is only lengthened by retries).
    - Appends every risk/WAF-captcha/auth interception to an auditable JSONL ledger (time, node, HTTP status, severity tier, reason, evidence excerpt), auto-pruned at `GATEWAY_RISK_LEDGER_MAX_LINES`.
    - Severity tiers `auth > ratelimit > waf`: repeated (≥3) auth failures escalate to a long cooldown (`GATEWAY_AUTH_COOLDOWN_BASE` × 2ⁿ, capped by `GATEWAY_AUTH_COOLDOWN_MAX`), while a first auth failure keeps the short legacy cooldown so a re-login never locks a healthy node for an hour.
    - Exposes `risk_ledger` diagnostics via `/v1/diagnostics` and a 24h summary via `/health`; `GATEWAY_DISABLE_RISK_LEDGER=1` degrades to pure in-memory cooldowns. See `tests/test_risk_ledger.py`.

12. **Cooldown-Aware Health Probe Gate (`healing/monitor.py`)**:
    - `HealthMonitor` consults `CooldownTracker` before every probe: a node in a risk-avoidance cooldown is marked `cooling` and the real network probe is skipped entirely. Without this the 300s audit loop re-poked an upstream-banned node every interval, lengthening the ban window — exactly the failure mode the durable ledger guards against, one layer up.
    - Cooling probes neither increment `consecutive_failures` nor trigger self-healing, so a risk ban is never misread as a node fault; `GATEWAY_DISABLE_PROBE_COOLDOWN_GATE=1` disables the gate and `/v1/diagnostics` exposes `healing_status.cooldown_probe_skips`. See `tests/test_probe_gate.py`.
    - **[OPT-018] Read-time cooldown overlay + healer election avoidance**: `ProviderHealth.status` defaults to `healthy`, so before the first probe cycle (or right after a restart) a node sitting in a risk-avoidance cooldown still *reports* healthy — and `pick_healer_model` would elect it as the self-healing brain, dispatching a real upstream completion through a banned account. `get_status()`/`get_all_statuses()` now overlay live cooldown state ("cooling") at read time without persisting it, `pick_healer_model` excludes cooling candidates via `monitor.get_cooldown_remaining()` and records `cooldown_healer_skips`, and when no non-cooling candidate remains it returns `None` so healing is deferred instead of touching the banned node. The single master switch for all cooldown avoidance is `GATEWAY_DISABLE_COOLDOWN_GATE=1` (`GATEWAY_DISABLE_PROBE_COOLDOWN_GATE` remains as a back-compatible alias).

13. **Cooldown-Aware Token Refresh Gate (`server.py:_auto_refresh_stale_sessions`)**:
    - The startup silent token-refresh pass consults the cooldown tracker: a node in a risk-avoidance cooldown is not refreshed (a login/refresh call to an upstream-banned account would only deepen the ban). Deferred nodes are retried by a bounded background thread (`GATEWAY_REFRESH_DEFER_POLL`, `GATEWAY_REFRESH_DEFER_MAX_WAIT`, default 24h) that refreshes once the cooldown clears and otherwise gives up cleanly. See `tests/test_refresh_gate.py`.

14. **Evidence-Driven Adaptive Pacing (`providers/failover.py:compute_pacing_multiplier`)**:
    - The traffic pacer widens each provider's call interval proportionally to its recent risk-control interceptions (weighted `waf > auth > ratelimit`), so a node that just tripped a captcha or 429 is called more slowly until its 6h evidence window drains. This is the "avoidance" half of the ban problem: when a ban cannot be bypassed, slow down before it happens. Cached per provider (`GATEWAY_PACING_EVIDENCE_TTL`, default 60s); `GATEWAY_DISABLE_ADAPTIVE_PACING=1` reverts to the fixed interval. See `tests/test_adaptive_pacing.py`.

15. **Graceful Shutdown Drain (`server.py:_drain_inflight_requests`)**:
    - A pure-ASGI `InflightTrackerMiddleware` counts in-flight requests across the *entire* response (its `await self.app(...)` only returns after a streaming body finishes, unlike `BaseHTTPMiddleware`). On shutdown the lifespan finally-block waits — bounded by `GATEWAY_SHUTDOWN_DRAIN_TIMEOUT` (default 30s) — for that count to reach zero before stopping the health monitor and closing the HTTP client, so a restart no longer severs a half-sent stream (which would make clients retry and re-load upstream). `inflight_requests` is exposed on `/health` and `/v1/diagnostics`. See `tests/test_graceful_drain.py`.

16. **Durable Conversation Deletion Queue (`providers/conversation_gc.py`)**:
    - Anonymous calls (no client `conversation_id`) leave a temporary session in the user's upstream web sidebar, so the gateway deletes it afterwards. The old `asyncio.create_task(provider.delete_conversation(cid))` was fire-and-forget: a failed delete or a process restart silently leaked the session forever. `ConversationGC` now enqueues each deletion in a POSIX-atomic JSON queue (dir `0o700`, file `0o600`) that survives restarts, retries via a background drain (bounded by `GATEWAY_CONVERSATION_GC_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, default 5, then drops with a warning), and drains once at startup to clean up the previous process's leftovers. `GATEWAY_DISABLE_CONVERSATION_GC=1` degrades to the legacy fire-and-forget behavior. Exposed as `conversation_gc` in `/v1/diagnostics`. See `tests/test_conversation_gc.py`.
    - **[OPT-017] Cooldown-aware deletion gate**: a delete is itself a real upstream request, so firing one at a node in a risk-avoidance cooldown is just another channel that keeps touching a banned account. Deletions for a cooling provider are deferred — the entry stays queued and does **not** consume a retry attempt — and are performed by a later drain once the cooldown clears. Mirrors the probe gate (OPT-012) and the refresh gate (OPT-013). `GATEWAY_DISABLE_CONVERSATION_GC_COOLDOWN_GATE=1` reverts; deferrals are exposed as `deferred_by_cooldown` in `/v1/diagnostics`. A gate-query failure never blocks deletion (zero-regression default).

17. **Test Isolation of Durable Stores (`tests/*`)**:
    - The durable stores (risk ledger cooldowns, interception JSONL, conversation GC queue, device identities) resolve to a `tempfile.mkdtemp()` directory under `TESTING=1`, but several legacy suites set fake tokens *without* setting `TESTING`, so a test run wrote fake cooldowns into the developer's real `session/` directory — and, because every `CooldownTracker` restores from the shared ledger singleton, later cases in the same process inherited cooldowns recorded by earlier ones, making results order-dependent (`tests/test_failover.py` was red for this reason). Those suites now set `TESTING=1` (isolated temp dir, zero writes to `session/`), and `tests/test_failover.py` additionally sets `GATEWAY_DISABLE_RISK_LEDGER=1` so it tests pure in-memory cooldown semantics as designed — cross-restart durability has its own suite, `tests/test_risk_ledger.py`. `tests/test_providers.py` was found writing device identities into the real `session/` for the same reason and now sets `TESTING=1` too (see OPT-030).
