    - **Verified**: full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 81`; `tests/test_credential_readiness_agreement.py` (7 cases) is RED on a pristine `HEAD` worktree with `派发边界仍然构造了 Provider: ['qwen','glm','doubao','kimi','deepseek']`. Nine pre-existing suites failed **only** because they drive dispatch with in-memory fake upstreams and no credentials — the gate was right and the fixtures were describing a deployment that cannot exist — so each gained a fake-token prelude (`for _pk in (...)`) rather than `GATEWAY_DISABLE_UNCONFIGURED_GATE=1`, which would have tested a non-default configuration. `tests/test_risk_dossier.py`'s pinned posture set gained `POSTURE_UNCONFIGURED`, the assertion that exists precisely to make a vocabulary change impossible to slip through. Note the direction of the correction: the **product** changed and the **tests** were corrected to it only where they asserted the old lie — the eight fixture preludes were not asserting anything about credentials, they were simply silent about them.

79. **`developer` Is `system`'s Official Replacement Name, and the Gateway Treated It as an Unknown Role (`providers/base.py:is_contract_role`)**:
    - **[SESSION-CONTINUITY] The same fact as §76, wearing a different role name.** OpenAI introduced `role: "developer"` as the documented replacement for `system` on the o-series models (the SDK's `EasyInputMessage` accepts it directly); semantically it is still "the instruction that outranks the user and applies to this turn". The gateway recognised only the literal `"system"`, so one string change turned the standing contract into a third kind of thing — a speaker label no upstream has ever seen. Measured on the real HTTP path (scripted provider, `TESTING=1`, zero upstream calls):

      ```
      turn 1   prompt = 'Developer: STANDING-RULE-42: always answer in French.\n\nUser: hello\n\nAssistant:'
      turn 2   continuation_content(...) = 'and now?'      ← the whole standing rule is gone
      ```

    - **Three consequences, none of them an error**: (a) `Developer:` is a label web-chat models have no prior for, so the instruction loses its priority over the user turn; (b) `continuation_content` hoisted only `role == "system"` ones, so from turn two the client's own rule never reached the model — §76's defect exactly, one role name over; (c) the prompt folder's head protection compared against `"system"` too.
    - **The fix is one predicate, four read points** — the recurring "one fact, one definition" rule: `is_contract_role(role)` in `providers/base.py` returns `True` for `system` and `developer`, and it is read by `_ROLE_LABELS` (`developer` → `System`, the honest rendering, since that is what it means), `continuation_content`'s hoist filter, `prompt_optimizer`'s head-protection loop, and the tool-contract merge site in `server.py` (a leading `developer` message now receives the injected `## Tools Available` block instead of a second `system` message being pushed in front of it).
    - **One case was measured green on the pre-fix tree, and it is recorded as a guard rather than dressed up as a bug fix.** Head protection is the *positional* contiguous prefix `messages[:first_user_idx + 1]`, so a leading `developer` message was already inside it — the folding path did **not** carry an independent `developer` defect (probe: folded output preserved `developer 'RULE'` and `user 'do it'`). `prompt_optimizer.py`'s switch to `is_contract_role()` is therefore predicate alignment, value-for-value identical for this shape, and `test_prompt_folding_protects_a_leading_developer_message` says so in its docstring. The same discipline as §59 (a test deleted after measuring) and §69 (a case that passed pre-fix documented as a guard, not as RED-first).
    - **Verified per case on a pristine `HEAD` worktree** (`git worktree add /tmp/dev_base HEAD`), not on a first-case failure: 4 RED with the measured lines above (`developer` rendered as `Developer:`; `continuation_content` = `'and now?'`; the two-round bound session losing the rule at turn two; the Responses `developer` item rendered as an unknown role), 2 GREEN by design (the trailing-`developer` control, and the folding guard). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 82`. Both real binaries re-run green afterwards:
      ```
      claude: PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=Bash,         tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      codex:  PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=exec_command, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      ```
    - Pinned by `tests/test_developer_role.py` (7 cases).

80. **`response_format` Is a Wire Contract, and the Parser Owns It (`providers/response_format.py`, `server.py`)**:
    - **[PROTOCOL] §35 made the request visible and left the promise unkept.** `response_format` (OpenAI/Responses; `text.format` is the same field spelled differently) was translated into one sentence of upstream prompt text and nothing else, so the parser had no opinion about whether the answer *was* JSON. Measured on the real HTTP path (scripted provider, `TESTING=1`, zero upstream calls, model answering the way these upstreams actually answer — prose + a fenced payload + a sign-off):

      ```
      model: 'Sure! Here is the data you asked for:\n\n```json\n{"name": "Ada", "age": 36}\n```\nHope this helps!'

      /v1/chat/completions   (non-stream)  200  content     = that prose
      /v1/chat/completions   (stream)      200  content     = that prose, in two deltas
      /v1/responses          (non-stream)  200  output_text = that prose
      ```

      The client's contract is that `content` is itself a JSON object — every SDK does `JSON.parse` on it immediately — so the turn dies on the line after the response, with no error the operator can act on. Same discipline as `tool_choice` / `stop` / `parallel_tool_calls`: **the prompt lowers the incidence, the parser guarantees the outcome.**
    - **Two steps, and the order is load-bearing**: *recover*, then *be honest*. `extract_json_payload()` tries the raw text, the fence-stripped text, and every balanced value in it — **all** of them strictly first, and only if every strict parse fails does the non-truncating self-healing pipeline get a turn. Otherwise a strictly-valid payload could lose to an earlier "repaired" candidate and the gateway would hand the client a *rewritten* answer the model never gave. A strict hit is returned **verbatim** (byte-for-byte, so a client that hashes content keeps working); a repaired hit is re-serialised because the pipeline's output need not be strict JSON.
    - **A definite failure is reported, never disguised** — 502 `invalid_output` carrying an excerpt of the model's own words. Handing prose to a client that asked for JSON is not a degraded success, it is a guaranteed client-side exception; and silence is worse than an error, because the operator cannot tell a model that refused from a gateway that mangled the response. In-flight, the rule from §45/§57 applies unchanged: on the OpenAI path an in-band error frame and **never** `[DONE]`; on the Responses path `response.failed` as the terminal event (Codex 0.145.0 discards a bare `event: error`, §57). `invalid_output` is deliberately **not** in `_DETERMINISTIC_REFUSAL_ERR_TYPES`: a model-quality miss is worth one client retry, so neither `x-should-retry: false` nor `Retry-After` is attached (§54's boundary).
    - **Truncation repair is switched off for structured output, and that asymmetry is the point.** `repair_truncated_json` appends the missing closers so a token-limited *tool call* still yields usable arguments — the right trade there, because the alternative is losing the whole call. For structured output the same repair fills absent fields with `null`, i.e. **invents data the model never produced**, and the client has no way to distinguish it from a real answer (§52: a truncated answer must not impersonate a complete one). Measured: `'{"name": "Ada", "age":'` stays a 502 whose message preserves the truncated tail, rather than becoming `{"name": "Ada", "age": null}` with status 200. `json_repair.repair_json_string` / `safe_loads_with_repair` grew `allow_truncation_repair` (default `True`, so the tool path is value-for-value unchanged).
    - **Streaming must buffer, and buffering is the contract, not a limitation.** A JSON guarantee cannot coexist with token-at-a-time delivery: the first bytes of an object are not valid JSON, and bytes already sent cannot be recalled. So in a declared JSON mode the content deltas are accumulated and emitted as **one** chunk after enforcement; reasoning deltas still stream (they are not content, and §58's thinking-suppression rule is untouched). Three separate streaming paths needed this — chat/no-tools, chat/with-tools, and Responses — and the tools branch had a trap worth recording: it normally delivers the final text through `text_delta` frames and emits the `init_chunk` only `if not first_frame_emitted`, so a naive "emit the buffered payload in place of `init_chunk`" fix sends **nothing** whenever a reasoning delta had already set the flag. The JSON branch therefore emits unconditionally, and `rem_text` is suppressed so a partial payload cannot escape through the tool streamer's un-emitted prefix.
    - **Two defects the suite found in the feature it was written to test, both measured before being fixed.** (a) `format_response_format_prompt` hardcoded `"Respond with a single valid JSON object"` even for a `json_schema` declaring `type: "array"` — the prompt contradicted the `Schema:` line printed immediately below it (`'Respond with a single valid JSON object matching the "items" schema … Schema: {"type":"array"}'`). The prompt is the *only* contract these web-chat upstreams ever see, so telling the model the wrong shape is a guaranteed violation. (b) Recovery could not lift a top-level **array** out of prose at all: `extract_balanced_json_objects` only starts scanning at `{`, so `Here:\n```json\n[1, 2, 3]\n```'` produced **no candidate** (`payload is None`) and a *correct* answer was reported as a contract violation. The scanner that fixes it lives in `response_format.py` and is value-only (`_balanced_json_values`, both `{}` and `[]`, outermost only); the tool path's object-only extractor is deliberately left untouched. And the declared top-level type now has **one** definition — `response_format_top_level_type()` in `anthropic_compat`, read by both the prompt builder and the validator — so "the prompt says object, the parser accepts array" cannot happen again.
    - **Scope stated honestly**: only `json_object` / `json_schema` are enforced; `{"type": "text"}`, `{}`, and any unrecognised shape return the text **unchanged** (fail-open, zero regression). Only the **top level** is validated (`schema.type`); nested schema and `required` are not checked — a half-implemented JSON Schema validation would create the *impression* of validation while providing none, which this file's defect catalogue is largely a list of.
    - **Verified per case on a pristine `HEAD` worktree** (`git worktree add /tmp/rf_base HEAD`), each with its own measured symptom rather than a shared first-case failure: 11 RED (`JSONDecodeError` where the client parses the prose; `[DONE]` present on a failed stream; `response.failed` absent; the array-schema prompt saying `JSON object`; the array schema accepting an object; two deltas in JSON mode; the truncated payload returned as 200), 2 GREEN by design — the two zero-regression guards (absent/`text` passes prose through byte-for-byte; a strict payload is delivered byte-for-byte), which must be green on both trees.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_response_format.py` (13 cases), with the `_make_provider` tripwire and a zero-hit counter asserted at the end (§35's lesson: a case that forgets the scripted provider issues a **real** credentialed upstream request). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 83`.
81. **The Readiness Surface Must Hand Out the Gate's Verdict, Not a Boolean (`server.py:health` / `server.py:diagnostics` / `static/index.html:providerVerdict`)**:
    - **[HEALTH-SURFACE] §63 fixed the readiness *endpoint* and left its *consumers* re-deriving the answer.** `/health` now reports `degraded` when nothing can be served, and §78 made an unconfigured node a hard block at the dispatch boundary. But both readiness surfaces published only a boolean `dispatchable` per node, so every consumer — the web UI is the one a human actually stares at — had to reconstruct *why* from `status` / `in_cooldown` / `*_configured`, and a reconstruction drifts. `static/index.html` was four heuristic branches whose catch-all was:

      ```js
      } else if (rawStatus === "healthy" || isConfigured) {
        statusClass = "healthy"; statusLabel = "健康在线";
      ```

      `isConfigured` answers "does this machine hold a credential for this node", which is not "will this request be dispatched to it".
    - **Measured on real responses, both surfaces, five states** (the HEAD verdict extracted verbatim and executed under node against payloads captured from a live `TestClient`):

      | state | `/v1/diagnostics` (**the UI's first source**) | `/health` (fallback) |
      |---|---|---|
      | operator-disabled | `离线 / 待配置`, summary `4 健康 · 0 熔断避让` | **`健康在线`**, summary `5 健康 · 0 熔断避让` |
      | quarantine lease 72h | `已熔断避让` | `已熔断避让` |
      | long avoidance 30m | `已熔断避让` | `已熔断避让` |
      | short cooldown 45s | `已熔断避让` | `已熔断避让` |

      The surfaces failed in **opposite directions**, and the first row is why the entry exists: `loadDiagnosticsData()` fetches `/v1/diagnostics` first and only falls back to `/health`, so the primary path told the operator to run `login.py` for a node the operator had *deliberately excluded* — re-login cannot repair a configuration declaration (the OPT-026/029/034 misleading-the-operator class) — and the summary dropped the node out of **both** counters (`4 + 0 ≠ 5`). The fallback path called the same node healthy. Fixing only `/health`, which is what §63–§80's neighbouring work would have left you with, repairs the half nobody sees.
    - **The second failure direction is the opposite mistake, and it exists on both surfaces**: a *short* soft cooldown is deliberately demote-only (§OPT-021/031 — the dispatch boundary does **not** block it), yet the UI counted it as `熔断避让`, rendering "five nodes can serve" as "4 usable · 1 dead". So the old verdict was wrong about nodes it should have passed *and* nodes it should have stopped.
    - **Same root cause as OPT-032/033/035, one surface further out: a fact was published in a form that cannot be acted on.** The gate already knows `blocked_by` / `cause` / `remaining_seconds`; publishing a boolean forces every consumer to re-derive, and re-derivation is where the drift lives. Both surfaces now emit the gate's own three fields verbatim — `p_info["blocked_by"]`, `["blocked_cause"]`, `["blocked_remaining_seconds"]` — computed from the **same** `evaluate_dispatch_gate()` the dispatcher asks, with `dispatchable = configured and gate.dispatchable` unchanged from §63/§78.
    - **The frontend is now a pure function between markers, and the suite executes *that* function rather than a copy.** `formatSpan()` / `providerVerdict()` / `summarizeMesh()` sit between `MESH-VERDICT-BEGIN` / `MESH-VERDICT-END` in `static/index.html`; `tests/test_health_surface.py` slices the block out verbatim and runs it under `node`. A Python re-implementation of the JS would only prove the re-implementation is right. The block was also lifted out of `renderDiagnosticsCards` for exactly this reason — an assertion can only be pointed at a function that exists.
    - **Each block cause gets its own actionable label, and the number is real**: `credential_outage` → run `login.py all`; `disabled` → "改配置才生效，重登无用"; `quarantine` → `封禁租约中 · 剩 3 天`; `long_avoidance` → `长效避让中 · 剩 30 分钟` (`blocked_cause` decides the advice: `ban` never suggests re-login); `unconfigured` → `login.py <provider>`. The old single label `已熔断避让` collapsed "wait 3 days", "wait 30 seconds" and "you disabled this" into one word.
    - **The summary counts what the gate allows** (`N 可派发 · M 不可派发`), so the two historical lies are both structural impossibilities: a non-dispatchable node can never be counted as usable, and a dispatchable one can never be counted as broken.
    - **Verified**: 5 cases in `tests/test_health_surface.py`. On a pristine `0dce977` worktree (`git worktree add /tmp/hs_base HEAD`) case 1 is red with `[healthy] qwen: /health 报 blocked_by=None，派发闸门为 ''` and the new case 1b is red on its own subject with `[healthy] qwen: /v1/diagnostics 报 blocked_by=None，派发闸门为 ''。前端的第一数据源就在这里，它缺字段，浏览器只能自己猜`; cases 3–5 fail structurally (the markers do not exist on HEAD). The two-surface agreement is asserted as a **property over every node × every state** (`/health` verdict ≡ `/v1/diagnostics` verdict, including the summary string), because each past drift in this file was one missed cell. Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 84`, working tree clean afterwards.

82. **The Verdict Line Must State What the Run Established, Not a Per-Mode Paraphrase (`scripts/e2e/run_agent_loop.py:_verdict_line`, `tests/test_e2e_verdict.py`)**:
    - **[E2E-VERDICT] The instrument that judges every other claim kept three copies of one field list.** The runner has three verdict blocks — one client, two clients in parallel, N sessions of one binary — and each formatted its own parenthesised summary. The rule *"at one round no per-round marker exists by construction, so `0/1` reads as a failure on a green run"* had been learned in the **sequential** block (`markers_txt = f"{markers}/{want_rounds}" if want_rounds > 1 else "n/a"`), and never in the other two. Measured on a green `--parallel --write` run:
      ```
      claude: PASS (bodies=2, tool_markers=0/1, tool=Write)
      codex:  PASS (bodies=2, tool_markers=0/1, tool=apply_patch)
      ```
      against per-client data `"written": "e2e-ok", "content_ok": true`. Two independent lies in one line: a failure-shaped number on a run with nothing wrong, and the run's strongest evidence — the file's **real content**, the only thing `--write` exists to measure, since `touch` and a real `Write` are indistinguishable by existence alone — absent from the line most runs are ever read through.
    - **The same two blocks also dropped `exit` and `conversation_reused`.** §66/§67 added session-reuse to the verdict because "the loop closed, the tools really executed, and the run was silently 4× more expensive" printed `PASS`; the sequential line carried the field, the parallel and sessions lines never did. A proof added to one of three surfaces is a proof two thirds of runs cannot see — the OPT-035/§81 defect class, in the harness.
    - **A per-session `bodies=` is a number that cannot exist there.** In N-sessions mode the two sessions share one upstream log (`res.pop("bodies")`, `attributable_by: "marker-directory"`), yet the verdict printed `rounds=m/n` with nothing saying the requests were shared. The honest form is `shared_bodies=`, taken from the same field the mode already publishes.
    - **The fix is one definition, and the structural assertion is the load-bearing case.** `_verdict_line(res, ok)` is now the only place a body is formatted, and all three blocks call it. A behaviour test can only cover the modes I thought to construct — which is exactly how one of three kept the defect for this long — so `test_every_verdict_block_uses_the_one_definition` asserts the source: exactly one `def _verdict_line(`, exactly three call sites, and **no** line outside that function may contain `tool_markers=` / `content_ok=` / `conversation_reused=`. The next mode has to go through the shared formatter or turn the suite red.
    - **Verified against the real binaries, all four shapes** (this is the harness, so the proof is its own output rather than a scripted assertion):
      ```
      --parallel --write          claude: PASS (exit=0, bodies=2, tool_ran=True, tool=Write,        written='e2e-ok',   content_ok=True, conversation_reused=True)
                                  codex:  PASS (exit=0, bodies=2, tool_ran=True, tool=apply_patch, written='e2e-ok\n', content_ok=True, conversation_reused=True)
      --parallel --rounds 2       both:   PASS (…, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)   ← the count is not erased
      --client claude (1 round)   claude: PASS (…, tool_markers=n/a, conversation_reused=True)  ← no phantom 0/1
      --sessions 2 --rounds 2     codex-1/2: PASS (exit=0, shared_bodies=6, …, tool_markers=2/2)
      ```
      Both directions matter: the fix must not delete the real `m/n` on multi-round runs (that count is the per-round execution proof of §60/§61), and it must not leak `written=` outside write mode (pinned by the sessions run above, whose `written` field is `""` while its verdict correctly says nothing about content).
    - **Verified per case**: case 1 is red on the pre-fix tree with `e2e 运行器没有共享的结论行格式函数…`, and the structural case is red on its own subject with `结论行格式函数有 0 份定义，应当只有一份` — the two independent halves of the defect, each with its own message. The suite imports the runner by path via `importlib` (`__main__`-guarded, no binaries needed), so it stays in the offline glob. Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 85`, working tree clean afterwards.

83. **A Fence Inside JSON Arguments Is Data, Not a Delimiter — and a Size Cap Is Not a Boundary Either (`providers/anthropic_compat.py:iter_tool_blocks`, `providers/tool_streamer.py:_should_unlatch`)**:
    - **[TOOL-FENCE] The failure only appears when an agent writes a file that contains code, which is most of what an agent writes.** A tool call travels as an injected ```` ```tool_call ```` fence (none of the five upstreams has native tool calling), and the fence's close was "any backtick run not followed by a language alias". When the arguments themselves contain a fenced block — a README, a config, any document — the pair of backticks **inside the JSON string literal** matches that close. Measured with the real binaries on a `Write` whose content holds a ```` ```bash ```` block: `claude` exit 0, file created, content `'before fence\n'` — everything after the first fence gone; `codex` exit 0, the `apply_patch` payload truncated the same way, the file never created. Both report success.
    - **The healing path made it worse rather than catching it.** With the arguments cut mid-string, `safe_loads_with_repair` reconstructed valid JSON and returned `was_healed=True` — a *truncated file body* delivered as a success, with the repair flag asserting the opposite of what happened. This is §52's rule (a truncated answer must not impersonate a complete one) one layer down: a *repaired* answer is the most convincing truncated one there is.
    - **The rule, stated once**: after a fence opener, the block ends where the **balanced JSON object** ends, never at a byte shape. `iter_tool_blocks(text)` yields `(start, end, body, closed)` and is the single implementation shared by `parse_tool_calls`, `parse_tool_calls_with_healing` and the streaming state machine — the same "one fact, one definition" discipline as §53/§74/§75, because a fix applied to the parse function alone leaves the streaming path (the one real clients use) truncating.
    - **The new code repeated §68's defect before the suite caught it.** The first `_TRAILING_FENCE` was greedy (`\s*`{3,}`): with two calls written back to back, the first block's close extension consumed the second block's *opening* backticks, and `test_two_calls_where_the_first_contains_a_fence` measured `只解析出 [('Write', …)]`. Fixed by making the close lazy (`{3,}?`) plus the existing `(?!\s*LANG(?![A-Za-z0-9_]))` lookahead — the fix for a defect reproducing the defect it was written next to, which is why the suite pins the two-call shape as well as the one-call shape.
    - **Second face, in the streaming predicate: the 64 KB cap called a legitimate call "text".** `_should_unlatch` released the latched buffer as ordinary text once it exceeded `MAX_TOOL_BUFFER`. A `Write` of a large file crosses that threshold *while* the arguments are still being written, so the client received **80 056 characters of raw JSON as prose**, and `finalize()` then returned the same call — one call delivered twice, once as garbage. The cap is now chosen per buffer (`_latch_size_limit`): a latched buffer whose first block body starts with `{` is a call being written and is bounded only by `MAX_TOOL_BUFFER_HARD` (4 MB, the memory ceiling); anything else keeps the old 64 KB. Measured after: 80 KB → 0 leaked chars, call preserved; 600 KB → same.
    - **The structural scan was O(n²) and the fix is a 2-line gate.** `iter_tool_blocks` is O(len) and `_should_unlatch` is asked on **every chunk**; scanning the whole 64 KB+ buffer per chunk measured 1.71 s for just the 16 KB above the threshold. The scan now runs only when the buffer's tail is a backtick or `>` (the only shapes a real close can take), and `_latch_size_limit` probes a 256-char prefix. Measured: 80 KB → 0.10 s, 600 KB → 0.83 s.
    - **Both directions are pinned, because the fix's obvious over-reach is worse than the defect.** A truncated JSON object (the model really stopped mid-answer) must still heal — `test_a_truncated_json_object_still_heals`; a plain, non-JSON latched block must still be released at 64 KB with **every character** preserved (measured: `in=120013 out=120013 equal=True`, both at 512-byte and 37-byte chunk sizes); and a prose ```` ```json ```` example must still stream through unchanged (`"".join(emitted) + tail == prose`).
    - Verified RED on a pristine `e4834d8` worktree in five cases with measured messages (`anthropic_compat.parse_tool_calls 在第一个代码围栏处截断了参数: {'file_path': 'README.md', 'content': '# README\n\nRun this:\n\n'}`), with the three control cases green on both trees — then GREEN with both real binaries writing the complete file (`exact: True`). `tests/test_tool_fence_json.py` (8 cases; tripwire: the provider factory raises and a zero-hit counter is asserted at the end, per §35).

84. **Suppressed Thinking Is Still Content the Turn Produced (`server.py` — non-streaming `/v1/messages` block assembly, tools-branch streaming usage)**:
    - **[THINKING-TURN] `display:"omitted"` says "do not show me the reasoning", not "produce nothing".** Section 58 established that every real Claude Code request carries `{"type":"adaptive","display":"omitted"}` (6/6 captured bodies) and that suppression must happen at the *emission* boundary, never at accumulation — the reasoning still drives tool recovery and still counts as tokens. That decision left two branches deriving the turn from **text alone**, and a reasoning model that writes its whole turn into the thinking channel (no answer text at all — a normal shape for these upstreams, measured in the tool-loop work) then produced:
      - **an empty assistant turn reported as a clean finish.** Non-streaming `/v1/messages`, `display:"omitted"`, reasoning-only: `content: []`, `stop_reason: "end_turn"`, `usage: {'input_tokens': 1, 'output_tokens': 4}` — HTTP 200, nothing to read, nothing to continue, no error anywhere. The same upstream output on the **streaming** path is legal (`content_block_start(0,text)`, `content_block_stop(0)`), so one fact had two interpretations across two transports — the defect class this file records over and over. The guard was `if full_output or not full_thinking:` and its premise was "a thinking block was already emitted, so an empty body is fine"; suppressing the thinking block falsified the premise without changing the condition.
      - **the tools branch under-reporting every agent turn's cost.** `output_tokens` on the tools streaming path was `_est_tokens("".join(streamer.accumulated_text))`, while the no-tools streaming path and the non-streaming path both use text **and** thinking. Measured on one identical upstream output (100 reasoning chars + `"ab"`): tools streaming `{'output_tokens': 1}`, no-tools streaming `{'output_tokens': 150}`, non-streaming `{'output_tokens': 150}`. The tools path is the one agent clients use every turn, so the path that matters most was the one that disagreed with the other two.
    - **One fact, one predicate.** The block assembly now computes `thinking_block_emitted = bool(full_thinking) and not suppress_thinking` once and reads it for **both** the thinking-block append and the text-block guard, so "was a thinking block actually emitted" cannot be answered two ways. The text block is appended unless a thinking block was emitted, which is exactly the old behaviour for a displayed thinking block and fixes the suppressed case.
    - **The empty text block is the correct shape, not a shrug.** Anthropic clients require at least one content block; the streaming path already emits this exact block; and re-emitting the reasoning as *text* would override the client's explicit `display:"omitted"` and feed the history-replay blow-up §58 measured (310 bodies against 4). Silence about the reasoning is the client's choice; a legal, content-addressed empty turn is ours.
    - **The usage fix reads `accumulated_reasoning`, not `accumulated_text`** — the streamer deliberately keeps reasoning out of the text ledger (it must not take part in tool parsing), so the tokens have to be added from the field that holds them. The comment records why the two ledgers exist, so the next reader does not "simplify" them back together.
    - **The load-bearing assertion is agreement between transports, not two shape checks.** `test_the_two_transports_agree_on_the_same_upstream_output` drives the *same* scripted upstream through streaming and non-streaming, with and without tools, and requires the folded block skeleton to be byte-identical. A fix applied to one branch would pass a shape test on that branch and leave the other red; this property is what makes "one fact, two interpretations" unable to come back. The usage case is the same idea one field over: the tools streaming `output_tokens` must equal both the non-streaming value and the no-tools streaming value for the same upstream output.
    - Verified RED on the pre-fix tree, per case with its own measured message: `只有思考内容的回合被返回成空 content，stop_reason='end_turn'…`, `带 tools 的空回合仍然是 content=[]…`, `[no-tools] 两个传输对同一份上游输出给出了不同的骨架：stream=[('text', '')] body=[]`, `工具流式的 output_tokens=1 与非流式的 5 不一致…`; the two controls (`display` unset ⇒ the thinking block *is* the turn, with no empty text block appended; a plain text turn unchanged) are green on both trees by design. Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 87`, and both real binaries re-run green afterwards (`--parallel --rounds 2`: `claude` and `codex` each `PASS`, `tool_markers=2/2`, `conversation_reused=True`, 1096 ms overlap). `tests/test_thinking_only_turn.py` (6 cases; `_make_provider` tripwire with a zero-hit counter, per §35).

85. **A Lite Client Declares Its Tools Inside `input`, and the Gateway Read Only `tools` (`providers/responses_compat.py:hoist_additional_tools`, `scripts/e2e/`)**:
    - **[RESPONSES-LITE] The newest Codex models moved the declarations, and the gateway answered every turn with a cheerful empty loop.** Three entries in Codex 0.145.0's catalog carry `use_responses_lite: true` (`gpt-5.6-sol`, `gpt-5.6-terra`, `gpt-5.6-luna`; measured with `codex debug models`). They send a different request shape: top-level `tools` is `null`, `instructions` is `""`, and **every** declaration lives inside `input[0]`:
      ```json
      {"type": "additional_tools", "role": "developer",
       "tools": [{"type": "custom",   "name": "exec"},                       // freeform, 10,495-char JS orchestrator
                 {"type": "function", "name": "wait"},
                 {"type": "namespace","name": "collaboration", "tools": [ …6 sub-tools… ]},
                 {"type": "function", "name": "request_user_input"}]}
      ```
      Captured verbatim into `tests/fixtures/codex_responses_lite_additional_tools.json`. The gateway read only `req.tools`, so the item fell into the converter's `elif "role" in item` branch and became a `developer` message with `content: ""`. Measured against the real binary (`codex exec -m gpt-5.6-sol`, scripted upstream): `has_tools_section: False`, `declared_tools: []`, `prompt_len: 23955`, client exit **0**, `E2E_MARKER` never created. The model was never told a tool existed, so every turn of every session was a silent no-op — HTTP 200, a normal-looking answer, nothing to look at.
    - **The hoist belongs at the model boundary, because four readers consume `req.tools`.** Prompt rendering (`format_tools_system_prompt`), the parse allowlist (`resolve_allowed_tools`), freeform dispatch (`freeform_tool_names`) and the namespace map (`namespace_of_tools`) each read the field independently, across both streaming and non-streaming paths — fixing the prompt alone would make the model write `wait`/`exec` and have it discarded as undeclared, i.e. the identical symptom one layer harder to find (§62's lesson). `hoist_additional_tools()` runs once in a `model_validator(mode="after")`, then feeds the merged list through the **same** `flatten_declared_tools` the top-level path uses, so a hoisted `collaboration` container gets §62's flattening with no second rule. Merge policy: top-level `tools` is the client's explicit channel and wins on a name collision, duplicates are dropped, order is preserved; and the consumed item is removed from `input`, because leaving it there produces an empty `developer` utterance — a declaration replayed as a statement that says nothing.
    - **Measured after the product fix, same binary, same script**: `has_tools_section: true`, nine callable names (`exec`, `wait`, `request_user_input`, and the six `collaboration` sub-tools), `payload_len: 44004`, three upstream bodies, `served_conv: [None, "upstream_qwen_1", "upstream_qwen_1"]` (session reuse from turn two, §66), and `payload_has_tool_result: true` on turns 2–3.
    - **Then the instrument had four defects, and each one produced a confident false reading.** All four are the shape §60/§68/§82 record — the tool ran, the command did not, and the verdict read exactly like "the gateway dropped the call":
      1. `bodies=0` on a run whose captured log held three bodies: the runner attributed Codex by a *single* tool name (`exec_command`), and a lite model declares `exec`. `_OWN_TOOLS = {"claude": ("Bash",), "codex": ("exec_command", "exec")}` plus an `any()` filter — one client, two legitimate shapes, because "which client is this" must not depend on which model the operator selected.
      2. `_client_tag("exec")` returned `"exec"`, so in `--parallel` mode the marker path would resolve outside `{want_dir}/codex` and "codex ran this round" would be unattributable — silent mixing of the two clients' evidence.
      3. The emitted `input` was `return await tools.exec_command(…)` and the client answered `Script error:\nSyntaxError: Illegal return statement`. Codex evaluates `input` as a **script body**, so a top-level `return` is a parse error and a trailing expression is the script's value: `await tools.exec_command(…)`.
      4. With `return` gone the client answered `Script error:\ntool \`exec_command\` expects a JSON object for arguments`. `exec`'s own description says nested methods "take either a string or an object"; the implementation accepts only the object. The emitted form is now `await tools.exec_command({"cmd": …})`, the shape the flat models' own `exec_command` declaration uses.
    - **Verified per case on a pristine `HEAD` worktree** (`git worktree add /tmp/lite_base HEAD`), each with its own measured message rather than a shared first-case failure: 7 RED (`上游提示词里没有工具声明段…`, `` `additional_tools` 条目留下了内容为空的消息: [{'role': 'developer', 'content': ''}] ``, `` `wait` 调用没有作为 function_call 回给客户端 ``, `` `exec` 没有被赋予 `{"input": ...}` 参数契约 ``, `namespace 子工具的调用没有回给客户端`, `流式路径没有把 exec 报成 custom_tool_call`, `合并同名声明时把其余声明一起丢了`), and 1 GREEN by design — the flat-`tools` control (`test_a_flat_tools_array_is_unchanged`), which must be green on both trees because it is the zero-regression assertion.
    - **All four real-binary arms green, with the flat shapes untouched:**
      ```
      lite  (gpt-5.6-sol)    codex: PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=exec,         tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      flat  (gpt-5.5)        codex: PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=exec_command, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      claude                 claude: PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=Bash,        tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      --parallel --rounds 2  both clients PASS with the same fields
      ```
      The two Codex shapes must both be exercised by any change to the harness, because the model a user selects is not knowable from the request: the operator picks `-m gpt-5.6-sol` or nothing at all, and the gateway has to work either way. `UNIPIVOT_E2E_CODEX_MODEL=<id>` selects the lite arm; the default arm keeps pinning the flat one. Pinned offline by `tests/test_responses_lite_tools.py` (8 cases, `_make_provider` tripwire with a zero-hit counter per §35). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 88`.
86. **The Gateway's Own Zero-Upstream Refusals Must Not Be Booked as Platform Interceptions (`providers/base.py:LOCAL_REFUSAL_ERR_TYPES`, `providers/failover.py:is_risk_interception`, `server.py` ×9)**:
    - **[RISK-TEXT] Every link in the avoidance line rests on one premise, and five gates violate it.** `record_failure` treats every `waf` it is handed as *the platform really intercepted us once*: the ledger escalates the cooldown, the dossier counts it, and the operator is told to complete a slider. The five zero-upstream gates — `credential_outage` (OPT-025), `disabled` (OPT-033), `quarantined` (OPT-021), `ring_exhausted` (OPT-031), `unconfigured` (READINESS) — are conclusions the gateway reached **locally**, with `provider.chat()` never called. Their messages are written for an operator, so they necessarily quote platform wording *and* name the nodes: `处置: 成因 auth/waf 表示凭证失效或需完成人机验证，请执行 .venv/bin/python login.py <provider>` plus `qwen(waf，剩余3.0h), glm(waf，…), …`. `is_risk_interception`'s rule 1 is `"人机" in msg and any(k in msg for k in ("qwen","千问","ali","rgv"))` — that self-description satisfies both halves.
    - **Measured on the pristine `4446a03` worktree** (real dispatch path, `TESTING=1`, whole ring seeded into long avoidance, zero upstream calls):
      ```
      POST /v1/chat/completions -> 503 ring_exhausted   touched=[]
      before: {qwen: (3599.9s, waf), …, deepseek: (3599.9s, waf)}
      after : {qwen: (3599.9s, waf), …, deepseek: (7200.0s, waf)}   ledger 15 -> 16
      [风控拦截避让] 节点 [deepseek] 人机验证重复出现 (第 4 次)：… 已升级为长效避让 7200.0s。请执行 login.py deepseek 由人工完成验证
      ```
      Three of this file's recurring defects happen at once: the cooldown **doubles** for a node nobody touched; the ledger's "all-time interception count / most recent cause" now describes something that never happened; and the dossier sends the operator to complete a verification on a platform that never intercepted them (the OPT-026/029/031/035 misleading-the-operator class).
    - **The discriminator must be the fact the raise site owns, not the prose.** `LOCAL_REFUSAL_ERR_TYPES` declares the five types, `ProviderError.is_local_refusal` reports membership — and **also** accepts any error carrying its own `zero_upstream` marker, because `BudgetExhaustedRefusal` deliberately shares `upstream_timeout` with a genuine timeout and can only be told apart by its class (BUDGET-3). The common fact is "we never made a `chat()` call", so that is what the predicate asks; a type list alone would miss the next zero-upstream refusal.
    - **Half the fix lives at the call sites, and the other half alone is a no-op.** All nine `server.py` risk-classification sites passed `e.message`, i.e. a **string**, so `is_local_refusal` could never be seen; they now pass `e`. This is §53/§75's "signature widened, call sites unchanged — passes every unit test, does nothing on the real path" shape, which is why one case asserts the source structurally rather than trusting the behavioural ones.
    - **The reverse control is part of the suite, not an afterthought.** The *same message text* as a plain string must still classify as risk (the classifier may not be blunted into "record nothing"), and genuine upstream interceptions (`403 RGV587 … qwen`, `rate_limit_exceeded`) and 401s must classify exactly as before.
    - **Verified per case on the pristine `HEAD` worktree**, each with its own measured message rather than a shared first-case failure: 3 RED (`网关自己的零上游拒绝被写进了风控账本（15 -> 16 条）…`, `网关没有声明「本地拒绝」的 err_type 集合…`, `仍有调用点把异常文案当成判据——本地拒绝标记在那里不可见`) and 1 GREEN by design (the upstream-interception control, which must be green on both trees). The suite imports `LOCAL_REFUSAL_ERR_TYPES` *inside* case 2 so an `ImportError` on HEAD is one honest per-case failure instead of a module-level crash that would hide the other three.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_local_refusal_not_risk.py` (4 cases, `_make_provider` tripwire with `TOUCHED == []` asserted per gate). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 89`; real-binary E2E re-run green on both arms afterwards:
      ```
      claude: PASS (exit=0, bodies=2, tool_ran=True, tool=Bash,         conversation_reused=True)
      codex:  PASS (exit=0, bodies=2, tool_ran=True, tool=exec_command, conversation_reused=True)
      ```

87. **推理强度是「要思考」的声明，不是四个白名单词 (`server.py:_effort_wants_thinking`, `scripts/e2e/`)**
    - **[AGENT-LOOP] 越想要模型想清楚，越会把思考整个关掉。** `/v1/responses` 判定 thinking 意图时，对 `reasoning.effort` 只认一个字面量集合：`if isinstance(effort, str) and effort.lower() in ("high", "medium", "low", "minimal")`。而 Codex CLI 的**真实**目录（`codex debug models`，0.145.0）声明的档位是 `gpt-5.6-sol/-terra: low…ultra`、`gpt-5.6-luna: low…max`、`gpt-5.5/-5.4/codex-auto-review: low…xhigh`。于是 `-c model_reasoning_effort=xhigh`（以及 `max` / `ultra`）落在白名单外，`enable_thinking` 保持 `False`，上游照常 200、日志什么都没有，模型以**非推理模式**作答。实测（真实 `/v1/responses` 路径）：`low`/`medium`/`high` 为 `True`，`xhigh`/`max`/`ultra` 全部为 `False`。qwen 把它直接映射成 `chat_mode: think|quick`，这是推理模型与普通模型的全部区别。§34 对模型名兜底写过同一句话——「固定表永远追不上厂商发布」；`xhigh`/`max`/`ultra` 就是这样长出来的，而这里的固定表恰好把「想得更多」读成「不认识」。
    - **同一字段在 `/v1/chat/completions` 上更彻底**：请求模型根本没有声明 `reasoning_effort`，它躺在 `extra="allow"` 里没有任何代码读过（§35 的「解析了然后没人用」）。实测同一取值两条路径的结论相反：`effort='low'` → `/v1/responses=True` / `/v1/chat/completions=False`。
    - **判据改成语义而不是词表**：`_THINKING_OFF_EFFORTS = {none, off, disabled, false, no, 0}` 是唯一的关闭声明，其余任何非空强度一律开启（失败开放）。厂商再加一档时，网关只会「多想一点」，不会「静默不想」。两个协议共用 `_effort_wants_thinking()`，chat 路径的 `ChatCompletionRequest.reasoning_effort` 现在被声明并读取，`thinking` 字段默认 `None`，`is_thinking` 只裁决一次后复用到 `chat_args`——同一事实两份定义正是 §53/§75 反复记录的缺陷。
    - **验收仪器此前看不见这个缺陷，这才是它存活至今的原因**：`scripts/e2e/run_agent_loop.py` 把 `model_reasoning_effort = "low"` 写死了，而 `low` 恰好是旧白名单里仍然工作的四个值之一。任何一次真实二进制验收都不可能失败。现在：`UNIPIVOT_E2E_CODEX_EFFORT`（默认 `low`，空值表示「不声明」）写进 `config.toml`；`scripted_upstream` 的上游调用记录新增 `"thinking": bool(kw.get("thinking"))`（`kw_keys` 只能证明键存在，不能证明取值）；`_effort_failure(res, name)` 由三种运行模式共用（与 `_write_failure` 同一理由：每个模式各写一份，就是「一个分支还在检查、另一个已经悄悄不查」的由来）；结论行（§82 的唯一格式器）新增 `effort=…,thinking=…`。
    - **受控实验，同一台机器、同一条命令、只改一个变量（哪一份 `server.py`）**：
      ```
      修复前:  codex: FAIL (… effort=xhigh,thinking=False)
               !! the client declared reasoning effort 'xhigh' but no upstream request
                  carried thinking=True — the gateway silently downgraded the model
      修复后:  codex: PASS (… effort=xhigh,thinking=True)
      ```
      两次都是 `bodies=3`、`tool_markers=2/2`、`conversation_reused=True`——即「回路闭合、工具真的执行」在两种情况下都成立，唯一的差别是模型的推理模式，这也正是该缺陷为什么从其他任何指标上都看不出来。
    - 逐例在 pristine `HEAD` 工作树上验证：第 1 例 RED（`['xhigh','max','ultra']` 全为 False）、第 3 例 RED（`'low'` 处两协议不一致）、第 4 例 RED（`'turbo'` 被静默关闭）、第 5 例 RED（HEAD 的 `run_agent_loop.py` 里 `_codex_effort` / `_effort_failure` 都不存在，`"thinking"` 在上游记录里出现 0 次）；第 2 例按设计 GREEN——它是明确关闭取值的零回归守卫，必须在新旧两棵树上都绿。全量 glob `failures: 0 / 90`。
88. **工具结果的两种历史形状必须渲染成一份文本 (`providers/responses_compat.py:render_tool_output`)**:
    - **[PROTOCOL] §43 只统一了「调用」一侧，把「结果」一侧留成了两条渲染路径。** Responses 客户端回传工具执行结果有两种合法形状，§43 已经确认过它们指**同一件事**：`{"type":"function_call_output","call_id":"c1","output":…}` 与 `{"type":"message","role":"tool","tool_call_id":"c1","content":…}`。两条都渲染成 `[Tool Result for c1]:` 开头的一段用户消息——但**内容**走了两个不同的转换：`role:"tool"` 分支调用 `extract_responses_content`（认得 `input_text` / `input_image` / `input_file` 块，附件变成 §41 的 `[Image omitted: …]` / `[File omitted: …]` 占位符），而 `function_call_output` / `custom_tool_call_output` / `local_shell_call_output` 三条分支对 dict/list 一律 `json.dumps`，把结构**原样倒进提示词**。
    - **实测（真实转换函数，`TESTING=1`，同一份逻辑结果：一段文本 + 一个 20 KB base64 的 `input_image`）**:
      ```
      role:"tool"            101 字符  'exit code 0\n[Image omitted: this gateway's upstream providers accept text only]'
      function_call_output 20133 字符  '[{"type": "input_text", "text": "exit code 0"}, {"type": "input_image",
                                        "image_url": "data:image/png;base64,AAAA…"}]'
      ```
      两个后果，都是本项目反复修正的缺陷形状。(a) **成本**：整张附件的 base64 进了提示词，而这段历史在后续**每一轮**都会被重新发送（§66 的 continuation 只省掉答案侧），模型为一个它根本看不到的附件持续付费——一次 `view_image` 就是 20 KB × 剩余轮次。(b) **可读性**：模型收到的是一段它无法解释的 JSON，而不是一句「附件已省略」。§41 已经论证过这个区别：占位符让模型能说「我看不到图片」，原始 JSON 只会让它对着一个从未收到的东西自信作答。
    - **判据是 §43 / §62 的同一条纪律：一个事实，一份渲染。** 新增 `render_tool_output(output)` 是工具结果的**唯一**渲染入口，三条 `*_call_output` 分支和 `extract_responses_content` 内部的 `tool_result` 块全部改为问它。
    - **「不要倒 JSON」不能做成「不要任何结构」，这是本修复最容易犯的错。** 一个返回 `{"stdout": "on branch main", "exit": 0}` 的工具，它的字段**就是**结果本身，模型要读的就是这些键——序列化它是对的。真正的分界线是 Responses 的**内容块词表**（`_CONTENT_BLOCK_TYPES`：`input_text` / `input_image` / `input_file` / `thinking` / …）：带这些 `type` 的是内容，必须走附件策略；不带的是一份普通数据，原样序列化。`test_plain_and_structural_outputs_survive_verbatim` 把这条守则的两侧都钉住了（字符串结果、普通 dict 结果、多段 `input_text`）。
    - **第二个同源缺陷藏在 `extract_responses_content` 自己的 dict 分支里。** 它对单个 dict 的处理是「有 `type` 且是 text 家族 → 取 text；否则 `json.dumps`」——所以一个**单独的** `{"type":"input_image","image_url":"data:…"}` 仍然会被原样倒出去，与列表形状 `[…, {...}]` 得到两个不同答案。现在单个内容块被当作长度为 1 的内容数组，走同一条列表渲染，所以「一个块」和「一个块放进数组」不可能再分叉。
    - **验证**：`tests/test_responses_tool_output_rendering.py`（3 例）。在 pristine `c042d1a` worktree（`git worktree add /tmp/rto_base HEAD`）上逐例验证：case 1 以 `工具结果里的 base64 附件被逐字写进了提示词…实测 20133 字符` 变红，case 2 以 `同一个工具结果由两条分支渲染成了两份不同的文本` 变红，case 3（零回归守则）**按设计为绿**——它守的是「不该改变的行为」，必须在修复前的树上也成立。全量 glob 扫描 `failures: 0 / 91`。
    - **真实二进制验收**（这是本项目唯一能回答「智能体到底能不能用」的仪器）：`--rounds 2` 双臂全绿，且会话复用未被破坏：
      ```
      claude: PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=Bash,         tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True, effort=-,thinking=False)
      codex:  PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=exec_command, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True, effort=low,thinking=True)
      ```

89. **A Tool Result Must Carry the Id of the Call It Answers (`providers/base.py:_message_line`)**:
    - **[TOOL-LOOP] The contract told the model to look for a shape its own history never contained.** The injected tool prompt's rule 5 reads, verbatim: *"When you receive a `[Tool Result for <id>]` block, continue the task using that result."* (§103 later found that this one sentence named only one of the three shapes the gateway writes, and rewrote it as a three-bullet list — the quote above is the text this section was written against.) Two of the three history renderers honour it — Anthropic emits `[Tool Result for toolu_1]:` / `[Tool Error for toolu_2]:`, Responses emits `[Tool Result for c1]:`. The **OpenAI chat** path emitted `Tool (Read): ok`: no id, and a shape that appears nowhere in the contract. Measured on the real `/v1/chat/completions` path (scripted provider, `TESTING=1`, zero upstream calls) with one turn calling `Read` twice in parallel and returning two results whose **content is identical** (the ordinary shape — two empty files, two commands that both print `ok`):

      ```
      Assistant: Reading both.
      Tool (Read): ok
      Tool (Read): ok
      ```

    - **This is not a wording preference, it is the only pairing information there is.** §68 made two calls in one turn reach the client intact; when the client sends both results back, the assistant side is keyed by `call_a` / `call_b` and the result side carried nothing, so the two result lines were **byte-identical**. The model cannot tell which output answers which call — and the gateway also gave it a directive (`[Tool Result for <id>]`) that matched nothing it could see. The third of three renderers was the only one that disagreed, which is this file's recurring defect class (§43/§62/§88: one fact, one rendering) applied to the one field that closes the agent loop.
    - **The fix is the id when there is one, and the honest fallback when there is not**: `call_id` present ⇒ `[Tool Result for <id>]`; absent ⇒ `[Tool Result] (name)`, and never a fabricated id — inventing one would send the model looking for a call it cannot see. The name is only available on the id-less path when the client itself supplied `name`, because the reverse lookup (`_tool_call_id_map`) is keyed by id; that asymmetry is now stated in the test rather than left as an unwritten assumption.
    - **Three pre-existing assertions pinned the old shape and were corrected to the new contract, not weakened around it.** `tests/test_tool_roundtrip.py::test_tool_message_carries_its_name` had `assert "Tool (Bash):" in prompt`; its docstring's actual subject — *"the upstream must not see a nameless output"* — is now satisfied more strongly by the id, so the case asserts both branches (id present ⇒ `[Tool Result for call_abc123]`; id absent + client-supplied name ⇒ `[Tool Result] (Bash)`). `test_empty_tool_result_is_still_reported` and `tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_openai_two_round_loop` asserted `"Tool (Bash)"` / `"Tool (Read)"` and now assert the contract marker. This is the same treatment §45 gave the two stale `test_server.py` cases and §63 gave `test_healing.py`.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_tool_result_pairing.py` (4 cases, the 92nd file in the glob), verified **per case** on a pristine `HEAD` worktree (`git worktree add /tmp/pair_base HEAD`), each with its own measured message rather than a shared first-case failure: case 1 `实测结果行仍是 'Tool (Read): ...' 形状`, case 2 `同一个工具的两条并行结果在提示词里完全相同，模型无法区分它们来自哪次调用: ['Tool (Read): ok', 'Tool (Read): ok']`, case 3 `工具契约要求模型识别 '[Tool Result for <id>]'，但它回传的历史里从来没有这个形状`, and case 4 GREEN by design (the zero-regression guard: `(empty result)` and an id-less `"loose output"` must both survive). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 92`; both real binaries re-run green afterwards:
      ```
      claude: PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=Bash,         tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      codex:  PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=exec_command, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      ```

90. **`search` Changes the Upstream Request, So It Belongs in the Dedup Key (`providers/singleflight.py:compute_key`)**:
    - **[SINGLEFLIGHT] The same one-line rule as §53 and §75, for the third time, on a field nobody had counted as an output.** A dedup key's only correctness criterion is *identical fingerprint ⇒ the two requests' correct answers are interchangeable*. `compute_key` had grown to cover provider / model / messages / tools / temperature / thinking / stream / constraints / `session_key` / `upstream_conv_id` — and not `search`, which four of the five providers write straight into their outbound body: qwen `"deep_search": "normal" if search else None`, deepseek `"search_enabled": search`, glm `"is_networking": search`, kimi `"use_search": search`. Whether the gateway asks upstream to run a web search plainly changes the answer to the same question.
    - **Measured on the real ASGI path** (scripted provider, `TESTING=1`, zero upstream calls), two requests identical except `search`, issued concurrently:
      ```
      statuses: 200 200
      upstream calls: 1 [{'search': True}]
      A search=True  -> SEARCH-ON-ANSWER | sf: leader
      B search=False -> SEARCH-ON-ANSWER | sf: follower
      ```
      The client that explicitly declined search was handed the search answer, and its declaration never reached any upstream. A wrong answer with no error anywhere — the same shape as [S3]'s `tool_choice` and §75's session identity, and found by sweeping for fields that change the wire but are absent from the key rather than by a bug report.
    - **Adding the field is the only safe direction, and the opposite error is pinned too.** `search=False` (the default) reproduces the previous payload value-for-value, so the Anthropic and Responses call sites — neither of which has this field — are untouched, and genuinely identical requests still collapse: asserted by a case that drives two byte-identical `search=True` requests and requires **one** upstream call with `leader` / `follower` roles, because a key so specific that nothing ever dedups is the worse regression (§53's rule, applied a third time).
    - **The call site is asserted structurally, not just the signature.** Widening `compute_key` while leaving `server.py` alone is a fix that passes every direct-call unit test and changes nothing on the real request path — the trap §53 recorded for `constraints` and §75 for `session_key`. The suite reads `server.py` and requires **exactly one** `search=req.search,` argument, which is correct because only the OpenAI chat protocol carries the field; requiring it at all three sites would pin the other two to a field they do not have.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_singleflight_search.py` (4 cases, the 93rd file in the glob), verified **per case**: case 1 red structurally on the pre-fix tree (`compute_key() got an unexpected keyword argument 'search'`), case 2 red behaviourally with the measured line above (`两条只差 \`search\` 的请求被合并成了一次上游调用（实测 1 次，上游收到 [{'search': True}]）`), case 3 green by design on both trees (the zero-regression guard), case 4 red structurally (the parameter does not exist). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 93`.

91. **`stream_options.include_usage` Is a Per-Request Declaration, Not a Per-Flight One (`providers/singleflight.py:compute_key`)**:
    - **[SINGLEFLIGHT] The `usage` frame was produced at the wrong layer, and both directions of the resulting asymmetry are wrong.** `compute_key` covered provider / model / messages / tools / temperature / thinking / stream / constraints / session identity / `search`, but not `stream_options`. And `include_usage` is read in exactly two places, **both inside the Leader's stream generator** — `server.py:2807` (no tools) and `server.py:3079` (tools) — each reading `req.`, i.e. the Leader's own declaration. A follower short-circuits at `server.py:2617` to `return StreamingResponse(sse_keepalive(stream_follower_gen(flight_decision.group)))`, and `stream_follower_gen` replays the group's broadcast frames byte-for-byte with no knowledge of `stream_options` at all. So a per-request promise was being delivered as a property of the dedup group.
    - **Measured on the real ASGI path** (scripted provider, `TESTING=1`, zero real upstream calls), both directions, symmetric and both wrong:
      ```
      A include_usage=False | sf: leader   | usage frame: False
      B include_usage=True  | sf: follower | usage frame: False      ← asked, got nothing
      upstream calls: 1

      --- leader=ON, follower=OFF
        first  sf=leader   usage_frame=True
        second sf=follower usage_frame=True                          ← did not ask, got one
      --- leader=OFF, follower=ON
        first  sf=leader   usage_frame=False
        second sf=follower usage_frame=False                         ← asked, got nothing
      ```
      The first shape is the damaging one: a client that declares `include_usage=True` to do token budgeting receives nothing and keeps a counter at zero, with no error anywhere. The second hands a client a `choices: []` frame it never requested, which a strict SDK attributes to its own request.
    - **The fix is the same one-liner rule as §53 / §75 / §90, applied a fourth time**: *identical fingerprint ⇒ the two requests' correct answers are interchangeable*, and "does this response carry a usage frame" is plainly part of the answer. `stream_options` joined the key. Adding fields only ever makes the key **more** specific, so this can never merge two requests that should stay apart; `None` and `{}` produce the same payload, so the Anthropic and Responses call sites — neither of which has this field — are value-for-value unchanged.
    - **The cost is stated honestly in the test, not hidden**: two requests differing only in `stream_options` now make **two** upstream calls instead of one. That is the correct trade — collapsing them means imposing one request's declaration on another, which is precisely the defect — and it is asserted so a future "optimisation" that narrows the key back would turn red.
    - **The zero-regression guard is the load-bearing case.** Two requests that both declare `include_usage=True` must still collapse into **one** upstream call with `leader` / `follower` roles, and both must receive the *same* usage numbers (identical fingerprint ⇒ interchangeable answers). A key so specific that nothing ever dedups is the worse regression, and §53's rule has now been applied four times for exactly this reason.
    - **The call site is asserted structurally**: widening `compute_key` while leaving `server.py` alone passes every direct-call unit test and changes nothing on the real path — the trap §53 recorded for `constraints`, §75 for `session_key`, §90 for `search`. The suite requires **exactly one** `stream_options=req.stream_options,` line, which is correct because only the OpenAI chat protocol carries the field.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_singleflight_stream_usage.py` (5 cases, the 94th file in the glob), verified **per case** on a pristine `HEAD` worktree (`git worktree add /tmp/sfu_base HEAD`): case 1 red structurally (`compute_key() got an unexpected keyword argument 'stream_options'`), case 2 red behaviourally (`第 1 条请求没有声明 include_usage，却收到了 usage 帧（实测修复前 follower 的 usage 帧 = True）`), case 3 red behaviourally on the opposite direction (`第 1 条请求声明了 include_usage=True，却没有收到 usage 帧（实测修复前 follower 的 usage 帧 = False）——它自己的声明从未被兑现`), case 4 green by design on both trees (the zero-regression guard), case 5 red structurally. Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 94`, and both real binaries re-run green afterwards:
      ```
      claude: PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=Bash,         tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      codex:  PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=exec_command, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      ```

92. **The Legacy Function API Was Parsed and Then Dropped on the Floor (`server.py:ChatCompletionRequest` / `ChatMessage`)**
    - **[TOOL-LOOP] The same "declared, parsed, read by nobody" shape as §34b/§35/§73, one API generation earlier.** OpenAI's function calling has two spellings. The current one — `tools` / `tool_choice` / `role:"tool"` / `assistant.tool_calls` — is fully supported. The previous one is `functions` (a bare `{name, description, parameters}` list, with no `{"type":"function","function":…}` wrapper), `function_call` (the force/forbid directive), `role:"function"` (the result message), and `assistant.function_call` (the call itself). Both request models carry `extra="allow"`, so all four landed in `model_extra` and **no code ever read them**. Measured on the real ASGI path (scripted provider, `TESTING=1`, zero upstream calls):
      ```
      1 functions+function_call      status=200 promptlen=2
          tools_section: False | mentions Bash: False
          prompt: 'go'
      2 role:function history        status=200 promptlen=41
          tools_section: False | mentions Bash: False
          prompt: 'User: go\n\nFunction: a.py b.py\n\nAssistant:'
      3 role:function only           status=200 promptlen=41
          tools_section: False | mentions Bash: False
          prompt: 'User: go\n\nFunction: a.py b.py\n\nAssistant:'
      ```
    - **Three things break at once, and none of them raises an error.** (a) The declaration never reaches the prompt, so the model is never told it has a tool called `Bash` and can therefore never call it — the most total form of "the tools did not fire". (b) The result falls through `_ROLE_LABELS`'s catch-all `role.capitalize()` and renders as `Function: a.py b.py` — a third-person monologue with the tool **name** discarded and the `[Tool Result for <id>]` marker the tool contract promises never emitted once. (c) The assistant's `function_call` vanishes entirely (`_serialize_tool_calls` only reads `tool_calls`), leaving an ownerless output — §43/§45's "the assistant turn disappeared from history" defect, wearing the older field name.
    - **The confirmation case measured something worse than the prompt probes.** Driving a scripted model that *does* write a ```tool_call fence against a legacy `functions` declaration: the client received `content: 'I will list the files.\n\n```tool_call\n{"name": "Bash", …}\n```'` — the fence leaked to the client verbatim as prose, because the declaration never existed so the anti-fabrication allowlist correctly refused the call (§32). A client following its own contract then does nothing at all, with `finish_reason: "stop"`.
    - **Normalize, do not re-implement.** The validators translate the three legacy fields into the current ones — `functions` → `tools` (adding the wrapper), `function_call` → `tool_choice`, `assistant.function_call` → `tool_calls` with a synthesized `call_<name>` id, `role:"function"` → `role:"tool"` carrying that same id. Everything downstream (prompt injection, `resolve_allowed_tools`, `parallel_tool_calls` trimming, the dedup key, both parse paths) then reads one representation, which is why **the twenty `req.tools` consumption sites needed no change at all** — verified by grep before the fix was designed rather than after it was written.
    - **Current spelling wins, and the old one only fills a gap.** When a client sends both `tools` and `functions`, `tools` is authoritative: two generations of declaration appearing together is deliberate, and the newer one is the newer intent — merging would silently enlarge a modern client's toolset. This is the "only add, never subtract" direction that makes the change safe.
    - **A case I wrote and then deleted, which is the part worth recording.** A `namespace`-container-in-legacy-`functions` case looked like a real gap and I even measured a symptom for it — but the legacy shape has no `type` field, so `flatten_namespace_tools` is a pass-through for these entries by construction and the "symptom" was produced by the malformed fixture I had invented. A guard asserting a behaviour that cannot occur would have shipped as a measured finding. Deleted rather than explained away, and the corresponding flattening step was removed from the product code too, since it would have been a comment claiming to do work. §59 and §69's seventh case are the same discipline: a test that cannot go red for the right reason is not evidence.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_legacy_function_api.py` (6 cases, the 95th file in the glob), verified **per case** on a pristine `c07d8a1` worktree (`git worktree add /tmp/lf_base HEAD`): case 1 red on the prompt (`旧版 functions 声明没有进入提示词（实测修复前 promptlen=2、tools_section=False）`), case 2 red on the directive, case 3 red on the rendering (`Function: a.py b.py`), case 4 red on the vanished call, case 5 red end-to-end on the leaked fence, case 6 green by design on both trees (the zero-regression guard). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 95`, and both real binaries green afterwards:
      ```
      claude: PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=Bash,         tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      codex:  PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=exec_command, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      ```

93. **`text.verbosity` Is a Real Declaration from a Real Client, and Only Half the `text` Dictionary Was Read (`providers/responses_compat.py:responses_text_verbosity`)**
    - **[PROTOCOL] The field was found by capturing what the real binary sends, not by reading the spec.** Driving a real `codex` 0.145.0 through the gateway (a `$CODEX_HOME/config.toml` custom provider pointing at a capture wrapper around the E2E stub) recorded **2 of 2** request bodies carrying `text = {"verbosity": "low"}`. `grep -rn verbosity` over the entire Python source returned **zero** occurrences.
    - **The shape of the bug is "one key of a dict is read"**, which is why it survived every previous audit: `responses_text_format` does read `req.text` — it reads `text.format`, and `text.format` works perfectly (measured: `{"format":{"type":"json_object"}}` → a 186-character prompt carrying the JSON directive). So nothing looked unread. The sibling key `verbosity` — the output **length** declaration, next to the output **shape** declaration — had no reader at all. Measured on the real ASGI path (scripted provider, `TESTING=1`, zero upstream calls):
      ```
      verbosity=low      status=200 promptlen=2  prompt='hi'
      verbosity=high     status=200 promptlen=2  prompt='hi'
      verbosity=medium   status=200 promptlen=2  prompt='hi'
      absent             status=200 promptlen=2  prompt='hi'
      format=json_object status=502 promptlen=186   ← the sibling key works
      ```
      Three different declarations produced a **byte-identical** prompt, and so did declaring nothing.
    - **Why this is a product defect and not a nicety**: all five upstreams are web chat models whose default style is "summarise, then expand, then offer further help" — exactly what `"low"` exists to suppress. Codex sends `"low"` on **every** turn, i.e. it plans its context budget on concise answers; the gateway dropping the declaration means every turn ships more tokens than the client budgeted for, with HTTP 200 and normal-looking content and no error anywhere. This is the §34b/§35/§73 defect class with a real client as the witness.
    - **Not a §58 violation, and the distinction matters**: `verbosity` is not a sampling parameter this gateway could fabricate — it is a **prompt-level instruction** ("be concise" / "be thorough"), the same kind as `response_format` and `thinking`, both of which already work. Translating it into an upstream `verbosity` field that none of the five web endpoints has would have been manufacturing a capability; injecting it as prompt text is honouring one.
    - **One pure function, one reading site.** `responses_text_verbosity(req)` returns the directive for exactly `low` / `medium` / `high` (case-insensitive, whitespace-trimmed) and `""` otherwise, and is appended to `system_parts` immediately after `fmt_prompt` in `convert_responses_to_gateway_messages` — so the two sibling constraints sit side by side and neither can overwrite the other. Fail-open on shape: an absent `text`, a non-dict `text`, a non-string `verbosity`, or an unrecognised value all return `""`, because a future OpenAI enum value must not be silently mapped onto a wrong length instruction.
    - **The dedup key needed no change, and that fact is asserted rather than assumed.** The Responses call site already puts the whole `req.text` into `constraints` (`server.py:4424`), so `verbosity` is already in the fingerprint — the §53 criterion ("identical fingerprint ⇒ the two requests' correct answers are interchangeable") was satisfied before this field started changing the output, which is the *lucky* case rather than the norm (§53's own defect was a field that started mattering after it was already in the key). `test_verbosity_is_covered_by_the_dedup_key` pins `"text": req.text,` structurally so a later refactor narrowing it to `text.format` turns the suite red instead of silently letting a `low` request receive a `high` answer.
    - **Verified with the real binary against the real gateway, before and after, same command**: `prompt_full` (the prompt the upstream stub actually received) carried `## Output Verbosity` in **0 of 4** bodies before the fix (prompt 37,944 chars) and **2 of 2** after (38,108 chars). The offline suite is the guard; the real binary is the proof.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_text_verbosity.py` (5 cases, the 95th file in the glob). Cases 1–3 are RED on a pristine `HEAD` worktree (`git worktree add /tmp/verb_base HEAD`) with the measured lines above; cases 4 and 5 are **green on both trees by design** and are labelled as guards in their own docstrings, per the §59/§69/§79 discipline — case 4 is the fail-open guard (the tempting wrong fix is `if verbosity: inject`, which would let a future value change model behaviour), case 5 is the dedup-key guard (already correct, but a documented defect shape from §53).

94. **`n` Is the One Output Constraint This Gateway Provably Cannot Honour, and It Was Silently Collapsed to One Choice (`server.py:ChatCompletionRequest`, `server.py:chat_completions`)**
    - **[PROTOCOL] Measured on the real ASGI path** (scripted provider, `TESTING=1`, zero upstream calls), `POST /v1/chat/completions`:

      | request | status | `choices` | `indices` | upstream calls |
      |---|---|---|---|---|
      | no `n` | 200 | 1 | `[0]` | 1 |
      | `n=1` | 200 | 1 | `[0]` | 1 |
      | `n=0` | 200 | 1 | `[0]` | 1 |
      | `n=-1` | 200 | 1 | `[0]` | 1 |
      | `n=3` | 200 | 1 | `[0]` | 1 |
      | `stream=true, n=3` | 200 | `text/event-stream` | `[0]` | 1 |

      `n` was not declared on `ChatCompletionRequest` at all (`extra="allow"` swept it into `model_extra`, read by nothing), and all 17 `choices`-emission sites hardcode `index: 0`. So the field was neither rejected nor honoured: a client planning a best-of-3 rerank received one candidate under HTTP 200, with nothing anywhere to show that two were missing. One illegal value (`n=0`) was treated as the default.
    - **The discriminator is "can this contract be honoured at all", and here it provably cannot.** All five upstreams are web chat endpoints that return exactly one completion per call. Producing `choices: [0,1,2]` leaves two options, and both are fabrication: duplicating the same answer three times hands a reranking client three copies of one sample (the exact use of `n` is to score *independent* samples); issuing n upstream calls multiplies the risk footprint by n, directly against the standing constraint that no other vendor's risk score may be raised. This is §73's rule (`previous_response_id`) applied to a field whose remedy exists rather than a missing feature: **a declared contract the gateway cannot honour is refused, not silently degraded.**
    - **The refusal shape is the one SDKs already recover on**: `400 / invalid_request_error` with **`param: "n"`** and a message naming both the reason ("upstreams return one answer per call") and the remedy ("omit `n`, or send `n=1` and repeat the request yourself"). `param` is what tells a `BadRequestError` handler which field to drop. `n == 1` is not refused because it is value-for-value identical to omitting the field — the tempting wrong fix is `if req.n is not None: refuse`, which would break every client that merely writes out the default.
    - **Zero upstream, on both transports**: the check sits at the top of `chat_completions`, before any provider is constructed, and the streaming request is refused **on the status line** as `application/json` rather than as a 200 carrying an in-band SSE error — the H4 rule, measured above as a 200 + `text/event-stream` before the fix. It deliberately does not enter `_DETERMINISTIC_REFUSAL_ERR_TYPES`: `_is_deterministic_refusal` answers `False` for anything below 500, and a 400 is not retried by any SDK, so no `x-should-retry` / `Retry-After` is attached.
    - **The dedup key needs no `n`, and that is a consequence rather than an omission.** §53's criterion is "identical fingerprint ⇒ interchangeable correct answers". After the refusal, `n` can only be absent or 1, which produce the same answer by construction, so the field cannot change the output and adding it to `compute_key` would assert a distinction that does not exist. (This is the opposite of §53's own case, where a field started changing the output *after* it was already in the key; here the refusal prevents the field from ever mattering.)
    - The field is now **declared** on the model with the reasoning inline, so the next reader sees it rather than finding an unknown key in `model_extra`. Scope is deliberately the OpenAI chat path only: `n` is not a parameter of the Responses or Anthropic protocols (Codex sends none — captured bodies carry no `n`), so refusing it there would be inventing a constraint rather than honouring a declared one.
    - **Pydantic's lax coercion was checked, because it decides where the refusal lands**: `n=3.0` → `3` → refused, `n="3"` → `3` → refused, `n=1.5` → `422` (a rejection, different shape), `n=1.0` → `1` → accepted. The last one matters: JSON has no int/float distinction, so a JS client legitimately encodes `n=1` as `1.0`, and a `strict=True` field would have rejected a valid request.
    - **Both real binaries re-verified green after the gate** (`--rounds 2`): `claude: PASS (bodies=3, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)` and `codex: PASS (bodies=3, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)` — neither sends `n`, so the refusal costs the priority clients nothing, which is why the change is safe to land on the same day as an agent-loop fix.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_n_parameter.py` (6 cases, the 97th file in the glob). Four cases are RED on a pristine `5851b03` worktree (`git worktree add /tmp/n_base HEAD`), each with its own measured line: `n=3` → 200, `n=0` → 200, `stream=true, n=3` → 200 + `text/event-stream`, and `n` absent from `model_fields`. Two cases (`n=1` and absent) are **green on both trees by design** and labelled as guards in their own docstrings, per the §59/§69/§79 discipline.

95. **An Unrecognised Content Part Is Not "No Content" (`providers/base.py:CONTENT_OMITTED_TEMPLATE`, `providers/responses_compat.py:extract_responses_content`)**
    - **[PROTOCOL] The same rule §71 established, missing from the one layer it had not been applied to.** §71 fixed the Responses **item** layer (six server-side item types were rendered as raw-JSON *user* messages) and found the Anthropic **block** extractor already announcing unknown blocks. The OpenAI **content-part** extractors were the remaining pair whose fallback was "drop it": `base.text_of` ended its loop with no `else`, and `extract_responses_content`'s list loop ended at `elif "text" in block and block["text"]`. Measured on the real ASGI path (scripted provider, `TESTING=1`, zero upstream calls), `prompt` being the text `flatten_messages` really hands the upstream:

      ```
      chat  audio only      200  prompt=''
      chat  text+audio      200  prompt='transcribe this'
      chat  unknown type    200  prompt=''
      chat  refusal part    200  prompt='User: go\n\nUser: why\n\nAssistant:'
      chat  image ctrl      200  prompt="[Image omitted: this gateway's upstream ...]"
      resp  audio only      200  prompt=''
      resp  text+audio      200  prompt='transcribe this'
      resp  unknown type    200  prompt=''
      anth  audio block     200  prompt='transcribe this\n[Content omitted: audio — …]'
      anth  unknown ctrl    200  prompt='[Content omitted: container_upload — …]'
      ```

      The last two rows are the control: one protocol already did this correctly, which is what makes rows 2/8 a defect rather than a design. **One fact, two default values** — the defect shape §43/§62/§68 keep recording.
    - **`input_audio` is real on both OpenAI protocols, so this is not a hypothetical shape.** Chat Completions and Responses both define `{"type":"input_audio","input_audio":{"data":…,"format":…}}`. A user sending `[text "transcribe this", audio]` had the instruction delivered and the audio silently dropped, so the model answers a request to transcribe something it never received — §41's criterion verbatim ("an explicit placeholder is the difference between 'I cannot see it' and a hallucination"). `AUDIO_BLOCK_TYPES` is defined once in `base.py` and read by all three extractors; the Anthropic extractor deliberately does **not** consult it, because the Messages API has no audio block type and recognising one there would be inventing a capability (§58).
    - **The `refusal` part was the worst of the three.** `[{"type":"refusal","refusal":"I cannot help with that."}]` is how an assistant's refusal is replayed, and it produced `User: go\n\nUser: why` — two consecutive user turns. The model sees that it never refused; the user sees it reverse a position it never took. A refusal's `refusal` field *is* that turn's prose (not an attachment, not metadata), so it is replayed as assistant text: `_refusal_text()` accepts `refusal` and falls back to `text` because some clients spell it that way.
    - **Unknown types now carry a typed announcement instead of vanishing**, via `content_omitted(kind)` — `[Content omitted: <type> — this gateway cannot render it]`. A part that does carry a real `text` still has that text rendered, which is the rule the Anthropic extractor already used (`elif "text" in block`) and is better than replacing real prose with an announcement. The single-**dict** path of `extract_responses_content` deliberately keeps `json.dumps`: it is also the outlet for tool **structured results** (`render_tool_output`), and announcing `{"stdout": …}` as "cannot render" would lose the result the model asked for. Message content is a list, and the announcement lives in the list branch; the asymmetry is commented at the branch.
    - **The wording has exactly one definition, and the suite asserts it structurally.** `CONTENT_OMITTED_TEMPLATE` lives in `providers/base.py`; `anthropic_compat`'s inline f-string was replaced by `content_omitted()`, and `prompt_optimizer._extract_text_robust` — the token-estimation copy that §55 proved must describe the prompt really sent — now counts the placeholder too. A behavioural test can only cover the part types I thought of, so `test_the_omission_wording_has_exactly_one_definition` scans every non-test `.py` for the literal `"[Content omitted:"` and requires exactly one hit, the same discipline §68 applied to `_TOOL_FENCE_LANG`. This is what makes the **next** extractor unable to regress into silent dropping.
    - **A second, smaller instance found while writing the guard case**: `_file_placeholder` read only the block's top level, but Chat Completions nests the name (`{"type":"file","file":{"filename":"a.py"}}`), so a client that **did** name its attachment got `[File omitted: unnamed]`. The whole reason that placeholder exists is to tell the model *which* file to re-send (§41), so the name is the payload. The lookup now checks the nested `file` object and `extract_responses_content` calls the same helper instead of keeping its own copy — measured pre-fix: `'[File omitted: unnamed]'`.
    - **Zero regression, asserted value-for-value**: image placeholders, text passthrough, and `{"type":"text","text":""}` still render exactly as before — the tempting wrong fix is to place the fallback **before** the known branches, which would replace two already-correct renderings with announcements. That case is **green on both trees by design** and labelled a guard in its docstring, per §59/§69/§79.
