    - **Verified per case on a pristine `8dcfa7b` worktree** (`git worktree add /tmp/parts_base HEAD`), each with its own measured message rather than a shared first-case failure: 6 RED (`音频没有留下任何痕迹… prompt='transcribe this'`; `Responses 侧的音频被静默丢弃`; `未知块被静默丢弃（实测修复前 prompt=''）`; `助手的拒绝内容块消失了… prompt='User: go\n\nUser: why\n\nAssistant:'`; `文件名丢了… '[File omitted: unnamed]'`; `公告措辞…实测出现在 ['providers/anthropic_compat.py']`), 1 GREEN by design (the guard). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 98`. Both real binaries re-verified green afterwards: `claude: PASS (bodies=3, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)` and `codex: PASS (bodies=3, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)`.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_content_part_omission.py` (7 cases, the 98th file in the glob).

96. **A Declared Output Budget the Gateway Accepted and Never Enforced (`providers/stop_sequences.py:TokenBudget`, `server.py:_declared_token_budget` / `_output_end_cause`)**
    - **[PROTOCOL] The field was required by the protocol, sent by the real client, and executed by nobody.** `max_tokens` (Anthropic) and `max_output_tokens` (Responses) were declared on the request models, folded into `chat_args`, and accepted by all five providers' `chat()` signatures — then dropped: the payload qwen builds carries neither `temperature` nor `max_tokens`, and the gateway enforced nothing of its own. Measured on the real ASGI path (scripted provider emitting `"word " * 400` once, `TESTING=1`, zero upstream calls):

      ```
      chat  max_tokens=5         -> 200  content_len=2000  finish_reason=stop
      anth  max_tokens=5         -> 200  text_len=2000     stop_reason=end_turn
      resp  max_output_tokens=5  -> 200  output_text_len=2000
                                         status=completed  incomplete_details=None
      ```

      `max_tokens` is a **required** field on the Anthropic Messages API, so Claude Code sends it on **every** request to budget its context: a 5-token classification came back as 2000 characters and, worse, a **self-declared-complete** answer — §52's rule ("a truncated answer must not impersonate a complete one") inverted, with the client itself having declared the truncation point.
    - **Enforce at the gateway, not in the prompt** — the same discipline as `tool_choice` (§34b), `stop` (§35) and `response_format` (§80): the prompt lowers the incidence, the boundary guarantees the outcome. These five upstreams cannot count tokens, so a sentence asking them to stop at N would be noise that never binds. `TokenBudget` (in `stop_sequences.py`, beside the stop scanner, because both are "the client declared where output ends") estimates with the project's existing `_est_tokens` — CJK ≈ 1.5 token/char, other ≈ 3.5 chars/token — and cuts at the longest prefix within the budget by binary search, sound because `_est_tokens` is monotone non-decreasing over a prefix.
    - **Only when the client really wrote the key — `model_fields_set`, not the field value.** `AnthropicMessagesRequest.max_tokens: int = 4096` has a default, so "omitted" and "explicitly 4096" are indistinguishable by value while behaving oppositely (never truncate vs truncate). `_declared_token_budget(req)` returns the int only when the key appears in `model_fields_set` and the value is a positive int; enforcing an implicit default would cut an answer nobody ever limited. This is the same judgement `_wants_thinking` makes, for the same reason.
    - **Three obligations in `apply_token_budget`, each with its own silent failure if missed.** (1) Metadata frames (`delta == ""` carrying `{"conversation_id": …}`) pass through untouched — all five upstreams report their session id in the trailing frame, and swallowing it kills affinity and anonymous-conversation GC. (2) The reasoning channel is **exempt**: thinking does not consume the client's output allowance, and §58's tool rescue and `output_tokens` accounting both depend on it arriving whole. (3) After the cut the upstream is still **drained to the end** — the stream's `finally` is where the concurrency semaphore and the connection are returned (§47), and the trailing frame has not arrived yet; breaking early would leak both.
    - **The wrapper is a separate variable on purpose**: every one of the nine stream iterations consumes `budgeted_stream`, while `stream` stays the raw `ManagedStream` that §47's `ReclaimingStreamingResponse` reclaims. Rebinding `stream` to the wrapper would put the budget layer between the reclaimer and the object whose `finally` releases the resources.
    - **The terminal enum is each protocol's own, and the reason is one definition.** `_output_end_cause(stop_matched, budget)` answers `stop` / `length` / `None` once (client terminator > budget > natural end), and the three call sites translate it into their own vocabulary — OpenAI `finish_reason: "length"`, Anthropic `stop_reason: "max_tokens"`, Responses `status: "incomplete"` + `incomplete_details.reason: "max_output_tokens"`. All three are real enum values in their specs (§35's rule: never invent one in the middle). **Stop wins over budget**, for observability: if the body stops exactly at the client's own terminator, that string *is* the answer to "why did it stop"; saying `max_tokens` is only truthful when the budget cut mid-text.
    - **Truncation happens before tool parsing** (§35): `max_tokens: 3` against an answer of `"Look here. "` followed by a ```` ```tool_call ```` fence returns **no** `tool_calls` and `finish_reason: "length"`. Anything past the cut does not exist for the client, so executing a call hidden there would be a call the client never asked for and cannot relate to its own conversation.
    - **The fix created a §53 hole and closed it in the same change.** `compute_key`'s correctness rule is "identical fingerprint ⇒ the two requests' correct answers are interchangeable". Before this section `max_tokens` changed no output, so keying on the raw field value was harmless — exactly §53's story for `tool_choice`/`stop`. The moment the field began to change the output, the three `constraints=` dicts keying `req.max_tokens` would merge "omitted" with "explicitly 4096": **same fingerprint, opposite correct answers**, and the follower would receive a body cut (or not cut) against its own declaration. All three sites now pass `_declared_token_budget(req)`, and `test_the_dedup_key_distinguishes_absent_from_declared_default` pins both halves — the parsed-model behaviour (`None` vs `4096` from two requests whose field values are both 4096) and the source shape of all three call sites, because a signature-level fix passes every unit test while the real path still drops the field.
    - **Two cases are guards, and say so.** `test_an_undeclared_budget_is_never_truncated` (a 20 000-character answer through Anthropic with `max_tokens` omitted must arrive whole — the 4096 default must never be applied) and `test_a_budget_larger_than_the_answer_never_truncates` are **green on the pre-fix tree by construction**: the field did nothing there. They are labelled as guards in their own docstrings rather than dressed up as RED-first proofs (§59/§69/§79).
    - **Verified per case on a pristine `HEAD` worktree** (`git worktree add /tmp/mt_base HEAD`), each run individually so failures are behavioural rather than a shared `ImportError`: 8 RED with their own measured messages (the three rows above, both streaming forms, and the tool-call-after-the-cut case), 2 GREEN by design, plus the structural case (`输出预算判定应当只定义一次，实测出现在 []`). The dedup-key case is red on the old tree only because the mechanism it guards did not exist, which its docstring states; it was then proved **able to fail** by tampering a single call site back to `req.max_tokens`, which produced `第 1 个协议的指纹没有用声明判据` naming the offending block. Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 99`.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_max_tokens.py` (12 cases, the 99th file in the glob; the twelfth is §97's).

97. **An Instrument That Reported a Number Nobody Measured (`server.py` output-budget plumbing, `scripts/e2e/`)**
    - **[E2E-FIDELITY] The first real run of §96's new evidence field printed a lie, and the lie was about to become a product conclusion.** §96 added `declared_budget` to the e2e verdict line so every run states the client's declared output budget — the number the gateway now *cuts* at. The first `--rounds 2` run read:

      ```
      claude: PASS (…, conversation_reused=True, effort=-,thinking=False, budget=32000)
      codex:  PASS (…, conversation_reused=True, effort=low,thinking=True, budget=4096)
      ```

      The Claude figure is real (Anthropic's `max_tokens` is a required field and Claude Code sends 32000). The Codex figure was not: **Codex 0.145.0 sends no output budget at all.** Captured directly from the real binary against a recording endpoint (6/6 `/v1/responses` bodies, zero upstream, no credentials touched): the key set is `client_metadata / include / input / instructions / model / parallel_tool_calls / prompt_cache_key / reasoning / store / stream / text / tool_choice / tools` — neither `max_output_tokens` nor `max_tokens` appears once. The 4096 was `max_tokens = req.max_output_tokens or req.max_tokens or 4096`, the gateway's own fallback, which the stub reads as the provider kwarg. The instrument measured a fabrication and printed it as the client's words.
    - **Why this was one run away from poisoning the record**: the next sentence I was about to write was "Codex declares 4096 ≈ 14 336 characters, which a large `apply_patch` turn could now hit" — a *product risk assessment* derived from a number that never existed. This is §67/§69's class ("the instrument must measure the wire, not the bookkeeping") reaching the newest field, and it is worse than the earlier instances because the fabricated value was produced by **product code**, so no amount of harness care could have caught it at the harness layer.
    - **The same request already had the fact three times, and the third copy was invented.** `/v1/responses` reads the client's budget three ways: the cut (`TokenBudget(_declared_token_budget(req))`), the dedup key (`constraints={"max_output_tokens": _declared_token_budget(req)}`), and the provider argument (`or 4096`). The first two use the declaration predicate (absent ≠ declared-default, §96); the third fabricated a value. One fact, three definitions — and the fabricated one was the only one that got recorded, read and cited. All three endpoints (`/v1/chat/completions` `or 2048`, `/v1/messages` `or 4096`, `/v1/responses` `or 4096`) now pass `_declared_token_budget(req)`, so the provider argument *is* the declaration and "the client said nothing" is representable.
    - **Behaviour is unchanged, and that is checkable rather than assumed**: all five providers accept `max_tokens` only for OpenAI-protocol compatibility (`deepseek/provider.py` says so verbatim) and none of them reads it — `grep -n max_tokens providers/*/provider.py` finds the parameter in four signatures and no use in any body. So the only effect of the fabricated value was on whoever read the request record; there is exactly one such reader today, and it was misled on its first run.
    - **Measured table** (scripted provider recording `kw.get("max_tokens")`, `TESTING=1`, zero upstream calls; pristine `HEAD` worktree vs. fixed tree):

      | request | pre-fix | post-fix |
      |---|---|---|
      | chat, no `max_tokens` | **2048** | `None` |
      | chat, `max_tokens=512` | 512 | 512 |
      | messages, no `max_tokens` | **4096** | `None` |
      | messages, `max_tokens=512` | 512 | 512 |
      | responses, no budget | **4096** | `None` |
      | responses, `max_output_tokens=512` | 512 | 512 |

      The three bold cells are the defect; the other three prove the fix did not turn a real declaration into an absence.
    - **Both directions are pinned, because a field that is always empty is as useless as one that always lies.** `tests/test_max_tokens.py::test_the_provider_is_told_the_declared_budget_not_a_fabricated_one` drives all six rows through the real ASGI paths and asserts the recorded list is exactly `[want]`; on a pristine `HEAD` worktree it fails with `chat/未声明: 网关交给上游的 max_tokens 是 [2048]，应当是 None`. `tests/test_e2e_verdict.py` keeps the complementary direction (`declared_budget=[]` ⇒ no `budget=` in the line), and its docstring now carries the measured Codex fact rather than a hypothesis.
    - **Re-run with the real binaries, same commands, after the fix** — the Codex line is now silent about a budget because there is nothing to report, and both arms still close the loop:
      ```
      claude: PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=Bash,         tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True, effort=-,thinking=False, budget=32000)
      codex:  PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=exec_command, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True, effort=low,thinking=True)
      ```
      The transferable rule is the one this file keeps re-learning from a new angle: **an instrument may only print a number the product actually carries as that fact** — and when a product layer fabricates a value, the instrument that reads it inherits the lie, so the fix belongs in the product, not in the formatter. Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 99`, working tree clean afterwards.

98. **A Session Id Names a Session, Not a Dialogue Line (`providers/session_affinity.py:tool_contract_key` / `_line_key`, `server.py` affinity wiring)**
    - **[SESSION-IDENTITY] Claude Code's auto-mode classifier shares its parent's `session_id`, and the gateway handed it the parent's live conversation.** Claude Code 2.1.293 sends an extra `/v1/messages` for every tool-approval decision: non-streaming, `tools: []`, `max_tokens: 2112`, `stop_sequences: ["</block>"]`, its own 139 702-character "security monitor" system prompt demanding a `<block>yes|no</block>` verdict, one user message wrapping a `<transcript>` — and `metadata.user_id.session_id` **identical** to the parent conversation (three bodies captured from the real binary: parent turn 1, classifier, parent turn 2; system sha `0270404a12` / `2d052caeb7`, 47 tools vs 0). The affinity registry keyed only on that session id, so the classifier resolved `bound` and was handed `conversation_id='up1'`, and the parent's next turn continued the same, now-polluted, conversation. Measured with the real captured bodies through this gateway with a scripted provider (the wire prompt built by the real `flatten_messages` / `continuation_content`, never a re-implementation — §67(a)):

      ```
      pre-fix     parent#1 fresh  conv=None  44 763 chars
                  classifier bound conv='up1' 140 302 chars  <-- appended to the user's conversation
                  parent#2  bound conv='up1'  44 543 chars  <-- continues the polluted line
                  conv sequence: [None, 'up1', 'up1']

      post-fix    parent#1 fresh  conv=None  44 763 chars
                  classifier fresh conv=None 140 320 chars  <-- its own line
                  parent#2  bound conv='up1'  44 543 chars  <-- back on its own line
                  conv sequence: [None, None, 'up1']
      ```
      Two harms, neither of them an error: the user's upstream conversation permanently gains a 140 KB security-monitor turn (every later answer is generated on top of it), and the classifier is served as a *continuation* of a conversation whose history has nothing to do with the transcript it is grading.
    - **The client's session id cannot be narrowed — there is no finer identifier on the wire — so the fix widens the identity key with a second fact the request really carries.** `tool_contract_key(tools)` is the sha256 (16 hex) of the sorted declared tool **names**; `_line_key(session_id, tool_contract)` is `session_id` when there is no contract and `session_id\0<contract>` otherwise. Adding a discriminator is the conservative direction the dedup key already settled on (§53/§75): it can only *split* conversations, never merge two that should have stayed apart. Only names, not schemas — a parameter tweak must not split a dialogue, while 0 vs 47 tools must. `tool_names_of` is reused as the single extractor, because it already answers "which tools did the client declare" for all three protocol shapes including custom/freeform and namespace containers (§39/§62); a second extractor here would give the prompt and the line identity different answers.
    - **One key definition, read by all three consumers.** `resolve_affinity`, `record_turn` and `has_upstream_session` all go through `_line_key`, so the decision, the record and the "does this line hold an upstream session" question cannot disagree — the same reason `_affinity_record` (§74) exists. `AffinityDecision` carries `tool_contract` so `record_turn` writes back the line that was actually resolved instead of recomputing it. All **3** `resolve_affinity` sites and all **8** `record_turn` sites in `server.py` pass it; the structural case extracts each call by parenthesis balancing and fails if any site omits it, because widening a signature alone passes every direct-call unit test while the real request path keeps handing the parent's conversation to the classifier (the §53 `constraints` lesson, again).
    - **The cost is one context rebuild when the declared tool set changes**, and it is stated rather than hidden. §76's promise — "tools declared after turn one must reach the model" — is preserved, but by a different mechanism: the changed contract starts a *new* line, which flattens the full history and ships the current contract. `tests/test_continuation_contract.py`'s `test_bound_session_turn_two_carries_system_and_tools` used to construct that promise as "turn 1 no tools, turn 2 with tools" — i.e. it was asserting a continuation that the identity rule now correctly refuses — so it was **corrected** to declare the same tools on both turns (which is what real clients do: Claude Code 47/47 with digest `035695cc210236e5`, Codex stable per §62/§68), and the old shape gained its own case asserting the new correct behaviour (`cid is None`, full history + contract still on the wire). The neighbouring `test_tool_declared_only_after_turn_one_reaches_upstream` still passes for the same user-visible reason, and now also asserts the mechanism (`cid is None`) so it cannot pass by accident.
    - **Observable, not inferred**: the response carries `x-session-tool-contract` on any request that declared tools, and omits it when the contract is empty (so "no contract" and "empty contract" stay distinguishable); `/v1/diagnostics` now reports `active_sessions` (distinct session ids) **and** `active_lines` (registry entries) — reporting only the first hides "did the classifier really get its own line", reporting only the second reads a legitimate one-to-many as double counting.
    - **Verified per case** on a pristine `HEAD` worktree (`git worktree add /tmp/lines_base HEAD`): `tests/test_session_lines.py` (7 cases) is 6 RED with their own measured messages (the classifier joined the parent; the parent never returned to its own line; repeated classifier calls; the missing header; the un-widened call sites; the missing single definition) and 1 GREEN-by-design zero-regression guard (`test_a_session_with_a_stable_tool_set_still_continues`, which also asserts the opposite error — a key so specific that a stable session opens several conversations would be worse than the defect). The structural case was proven non-vacuous by deleting one `tool_contract=` argument and watching it go red (`record_turn( 有调用点没有传 tool_contract`). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 100`.
99. **The Model Is Part of the Line's Identity, and It Was Being Silently Substituted (`providers/session_affinity.py:_line_key` / `resolve_affinity`, `server.py` affinity wiring)**
    - **[MODEL-LINE] §98 split the dialogue line by tool contract and left the other half of the same question unasked.** `resolve_affinity`'s bound branch returns `entry.model_name`, and `server.py` then assigns `dispatched_model = affinity_decision.wire_model` — so a client that names a **different** model on a session that is already bound is dispatched to the *old* one. Measured on the real HTTP path (scripted provider, `TESTING=1`, zero upstream calls, same session id, same tool set, Anthropic path):

      | turn | client declares | `resolve_model` | provider received | affinity |
      |---|---|---|---|---|
      | 1 | `claude-3-5-haiku-20241022` | `Qwen3.6-Flash` | `Qwen3.6-Flash` | fresh, conv `None` |
      | 2 | `claude-3-7-sonnet` | `Qwen3.7-Max` | **`Qwen3.6-Flash`** | bound, conv `'up1'` |

      No error, no telemetry naming the substitution: the client's `/model` switch simply did not happen. The mirror image is the one that bites harder — Claude Code sends background small-model requests under the **same** `metadata.user_id.session_id`, so a background turn under a live parent session was not merely served the parent's model, it *hijacked the parent's line*: the next real parent turn then continued on the small model's line or vice versa. Both are the "declared instruction silently ignored" class this file keeps recording, arriving through the one mechanism that was built to keep a session *stable*.
    - **The fix is §98's rule, applied to the second component: widen the identity, never narrow it.** `_line_key(session_id, tool_contract, requested_model)` adds the model to the key; adding a discriminator can only *split* conversations, never merge two that should have stayed apart (§53/§75's direction rule, now applied three times). The component is the **resolved wire model before load balancing** (`wire_model` from `resolve_model`), not the client's raw alias and not the post-LB model, and both exclusions are load-bearing: two aliases resolving to one wire model (`claude-3-7-sonnet` and `claude-3-opus` → `Qwen3.7-Max`) produce interchangeable answers and must stay on one line, while a load-balancer downgrade is transient and splitting a conversation for it would repeat the exact regression §M3/OPT-021 exists to prevent.
    - **One key definition, four consumers.** `resolve_affinity`, `record_turn`, `has_upstream_session` (the chat path's flatten-vs-continuation decision) and the internal registry lookup all go through `_line_key`; `AffinityDecision` carries `requested_model` so `record_turn` writes back the line that was actually resolved rather than recomputing it — the same reason `tool_contract` rides the decision (§98). All **3** `resolve_affinity` sites, all **8** `record_turn` sites and the single `has_upstream_session` site in `server.py` are asserted by the structural case, which extracts each call by parenthesis balancing and now requires **both** identity components: widening a signature alone passes every direct-call unit test while the real request path keeps resolving the wrong line (§53's `constraints` lesson).
    - **A second honesty defect found while wiring it: the response headers named a target that was never dispatched.** `_make_lb_headers(decision)` builds `x-dispatched-provider` / `x-dispatched-model` from the load balancer's decision, and it runs *before* `resolve_affinity` — which can also change the destination (bound / degraded / handoff). Measured: header `x-dispatched-model: Qwen3.7-Max` while the provider received `Qwen3.6-Flash`. `_restate_dispatch_target()` is called once per protocol immediately after the affinity override, so the header names the model the provider really received. Failover *after* dispatch is reported separately by `x-failover-*` and deliberately not folded in here — one header must not claim two different facts.
    - **Two pre-existing suites were corrected, not worked around.** `tests/test_continuation_contract.py` and `tests/test_session_continuity.py` asked `has_upstream_session("sess-1")` and read `_registry.get("sess-2")` — the *old* key. Their subject (the line really holds an upstream session; the binding is not poisoned by a failover turn) is still true and still asserted; only the way they *address* the line was stale. Both now derive it through the same `_line_key` and the same `resolve_model` result the request path uses, so a future identity component cannot leave them asking about a line nobody writes.
    - **Verified per case** on a pristine `HEAD` worktree (`git worktree add /tmp/modelline_base HEAD`), each with its own measured message rather than a shared first-case failure: 4 RED (`会话中途换模型被静默忽略了：… 上游实际收到 'Qwen3.6-Flash'`; `换模型后仍续用旧模型的上游会话 'up1'`; `后台快模型请求并进了父会话的上游对话（'up1'）`; `响应头声明的模型与 provider 实收的模型不一致: 头 'Qwen3.7-Max' vs 线路 'Qwen3.6-Flash'`), 1 RED structural (`resolve_affinity( … 缺 requested_model`), and 1 GREEN by design — `test_two_aliases_resolving_to_one_wire_model_share_one_line`, the zero-regression guard that also pins the opposite error (a key so specific that two aliases for one model open two conversations would be worse than the defect, §59/§69/§79's guard-case rule). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 100`, working tree clean afterwards, and both real binaries re-run green afterwards:

      ```
      claude: PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=Bash,         tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      codex:  PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=exec_command, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      concurrent window: 959 ms of genuine overlap; interleaving XCXCXC (5 client switches)
      ```
    - Pinned by `tests/test_session_lines.py` (12 cases: 7 from §98 + 5 here).

100. **`tool_choice` Had Two Judges, and They Disagreed (`providers/tool_choice.py:interpret_tool_choice` / `providers/tool_streamer.py:resolve_allowed_tools` / `providers/anthropic_compat.py:_tool_choice_directive`)**
    - **[TOOL-LOOP] §34b's own rule, violated by §34b's own fix.** §34b split `tool_choice` into two consumers — the parse-time **allowlist** (the guarantee) and one sentence of **prompt directive** (the probability reducer), "提示词降低发生率，解析端保证结果". Both read the same client field, and they were two independent implementations, so they disagreed on two shapes, silently:
      - **A. `{"type": "none"}`** — Anthropic's *legal* spelling of "no tools this turn". The allowlist recognised it (`set()`), the directive did not (no `c_type == "none"` branch), so the model was never told to stop calling tools.
      - **B. a name that was never declared** (`{"type": "tool", "name": "Nope"}`). The allowlist failed open (unrecognisable ⇒ keep the declared set), while the directive still emitted ``You MUST call the tool `Nope` `` — ordering the model to make *exactly* the call the parser provably rejects. Three dialects hit it: `{"type":"tool","name"}`, `{"type":"function","function":{"name"}}`, and the Responses flat `{"type":"function","name"}`.
    - **Measured** on the real HTTP path (scripted provider, `TESTING=1`, zero upstream calls, `guard hits: []` — the §35 tripwire counter, not an assertion), a 12-shape matrix run against a pristine `HEAD` worktree:

      ```
      shape                  allowed   directive                                          verdict
      none                   none      Do not call any tool …                             ok
      {type:none}            none      （空）                                              CONTRADICTION: parser forbids everything, prompt never says so
      {type:tool,Nope}       all       You MUST call the tool `Nope` in this turn, …       CONTRADICTION: prompt demands 'Nope', allowlist ['Read'] forbids it
      {fn:{name:Nope}}       all       You MUST call the tool `Nope` in this turn, …       CONTRADICTION: …
      {flat:Nope}            all       You MUST call the tool `Nope` in this turn, …       CONTRADICTION: …
      ```
      End-to-end through both protocols: `[anthropic] tool_choice={type:none} directive in upstream prompt: False`, `[openai] tool_choice={type:tool,Nope} MUST-call-Nope: True`. After the fix the same matrix prints **zero** `CONTRADICTION` lines and `{type:none}` is `True` on both protocols.
    - **The fix is one judgement read twice, not a second branch.** Patching the directive for `{"type":"none"}` would have left two readings of one field — the defect class this file records dozens of times over. `providers/tool_choice.py` owns `tool_names_of` (moved verbatim, so `providers/tool_streamer.py`'s re-export keeps every existing import working) plus `interpret_tool_choice(tools, tool_choice) -> (allowed, directive)`, which computes **both from one reading**; `resolve_allowed_tools` returns `[0]`, `_tool_choice_directive(tools, tool_choice)` returns `[1]`. The two can no longer drift because there is nothing left to drift — the same move §80 made with `providers/response_format.py`, and it also breaks the `tool_streamer → anthropic_compat` import direction cleanly (the neutral module sits below both, instead of `anthropic_compat` reaching up).
    - **An undeclared name fails open on *both* sides, deliberately.** The direction rule from `resolve_allowed_tools` is preserved: only an explicit client declaration tightens. Narrowing the allowlist to a name the client never declared would turn a serviceable turn into an empty one, and demanding it in the prompt is worse than either — so a self-contradicting client now gets the default behaviour on both sides. `{"type": "any"}` / `"required"` still emit the must-call directive only when tools were actually declared: with no declaration upstream never received a tool contract, and "you must call a tool" would be the same contradiction one layer out.
    - **Asserted as a property, not as the two cells**: `test_the_two_judges_agree_over_every_shape` sweeps 17 shapes × both dialects and enforces four rules — allowlist empty ⇒ the prompt says so; prompt forbids ⇒ allowlist empty; prompt demands `X` ⇒ `X ∈ allowlist`; and a demand inside a forbidding turn is never emitted. Enumerating the two cells I had measured is precisely how this survived §34b in the first place.
    - **Verified per case** on a pristine `HEAD` worktree (`git worktree add /tmp/tc_base HEAD`): 3 RED behavioural (`tool_choice={"type":"none"} 没有追加禁调用指令…`; `[openai] 提示词要求模型调用一个它从未见过的工具 \`Nope\``) and 1 RED property (the same contradiction list, swept), plus 1 GREEN by design — `test_allowlist_semantics_are_unchanged`, the zero-regression guard that also pins that an unrecognised shape changes neither judge (the §59/§69/§79 guard-case rule: a case green on the pre-fix tree is labelled a guard, never dressed up as RED). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 101`, working tree clean afterwards.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_tool_choice_agreement.py` (5 cases).
101. **The Tool Contract Forbade What the Parser Delivers (`providers/anthropic_compat.py:format_tools_system_prompt`)**
    - **[TOOL-LOOP] Two features made parallel tool calls a supported shape, and the one document the model can read was never told.** §68 fixed the back-to-back `` ```tool_call `` parsing (the second block used to be swallowed by a greedy fence close), and §34b fixed `parallel_tool_calls` / `disable_parallel_tool_use` semantics: **only an explicit client declaration trims to the first call; the default on all three protocols allows parallel calls** (OpenAI/Responses `parallel_tool_calls` defaults true, Anthropic `disable_parallel_tool_use` defaults false). The injected contract still said:
      ```
      2. Only call ONE tool at a time (one tool per fenced block).
      4. After outputting a tool call, stop and wait for the tool execution result.
      ```
      None of the five upstreams has native tool calling, so this prompt is the **only** contract the model ever sees. Measured directly:
      ```
      parallel ALLOWED (default)     rule2_one-at-a-time=True  rule4_wait=True  disallow-directive=False
      parser behaviour               cap_parallel_tool_calls(TWO, False) -> 2 / 2
      ```
      A model that obeys the contract splits a turn it could have completed with N independent calls into N turns, and every turn re-pays the full history — the most expensive thing that happens in the agent workload this gateway exists to serve. And the contract was not merely conservative, it was **false about the gateway**: if the model emitted two blocks anyway (as models do), both were parsed and delivered to the client, so the prompt and the parser disagreed in a way no client can see.
    - **One judgement produces the wording, so "forbidden" has exactly one definition.** `parallel_allowed is not False` — the same predicate `cap_parallel_tool_calls` is fed — selects both rule 2's permission sentence ("emit their fenced blocks back to back rather than splitting the work across turns") and rule 4's plural form. The prohibition stays in the `## Parallel Tool Calls` section, which already existed and is appended only when `parallel_allowed is False`; the alternative (a permissive rule 2 plus a prohibitive section) would have been two statements about one fact that can contradict each other, the shape §100 had just removed from `tool_choice`.
    - **Asserted as a property, not as the one setting I measured.** `test_contract_and_parser_agree_over_every_setting` sweeps five settings (absent/true/false plus the two `tool_choice.disable_parallel_tool_use` values) and requires: parser keeps 2 ⇒ the prohibition phrase is absent **and** the permission phrase is present; parser trims to 1 ⇒ the reverse. A further case drives `/v1/chat/completions` through a scripted provider and asserts on the prompt the upstream **really receives** for both the default and `parallel_tool_calls:false` — the field being honoured in code is not the same claim as it reaching the model (§67(a)).
    - **Verified per case** on a pristine `HEAD` worktree (`git worktree add /tmp/pc_base HEAD`): 3 RED (`解析端会保留两个调用…契约却仍写着「一次只调一个、调完就停」`; the 3-row property list naming `{parallel_tool_calls: None}`, `True` and `{disable_parallel_tool_use: False}`; and the end-to-end default-request case), plus 1 GREEN by design — `test_contract_forbids_parallel_calls_when_the_client_disallows`, the guard that pins the prohibition still exists for a client that declares it (the §59/§69/§79 guard-case rule).
    - **A pre-existing assertion had to be corrected, and it was a proxy, not a fact.** §55's `test_count_tokens_sees_the_same_tools_prompt_as_messages` asserted `len(count_serial) > len(count_named)` — "the prompt with the prohibition is longer". That was only ever a proxy for "the constraint section reached the estimation path", and the legitimate new permission sentence made the *allowed* prompt longer, turning the case red with the behaviour fully correct. It now asserts the prohibition text itself is present in the serial prompt and absent in the named one, which is the claim in the case's own name and cannot be flipped by a wording change. Same lesson as [M1]: an assertion about a proxy is an assertion about something other than the behaviour.
    - **Re-verified on the real binaries** after the change (scripted upstream, zero real upstream calls): `--parallel --rounds 2` → `claude: PASS (bodies=3, tool=Bash, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)` and `codex: PASS (bodies=3, tool=exec_command, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)`, 925 ms of genuine overlap, interleaving `XCXCXC`. Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 102`.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_parallel_contract.py` (4 cases).
102. **A Refusal the Follower Cannot Read Is Not a Refusal (`providers/singleflight.py:wait_for_opening`, `server.py:_follower_opening_response` / `_follower_error_frames`)**
    - **[SINGLEFLIGHT] The same deterministic conclusion, two different outcomes, decided by an implementation detail the client cannot see.** SingleFlight collapses requests with an identical fingerprint into one upstream call; the leader really calls, every follower replays the leader's frames. When the leader is refused **before dispatch** — whole-ring credential outage (OPT-025), operator disable (OPT-033), a quarantine/auto lease (OPT-021), ring-wide long avoidance (OPT-031), or the day-zero `unconfigured` gate (§78) — `execute_chat` raises `ProviderError` with **zero upstream calls**, `server.py` finalises the group with `finish(error=e)`, and the history buffer is empty because not one frame was ever produced. The three streaming follower branches nevertheless returned `StreamingResponse(sse_keepalive(stream_follower_gen(group)))` **before** that conclusion existed: the status line went out as `200`, and only then did `stream_follower_gen` re-raise into a body that had not started. Measured on a real uvicorn over TCP with the only variable being leader/follower identity (a five-node ring all returning 429, follower arriving 0.15 s later):

      | | leader | follower (pre-fix) | follower (post-fix) |
      |---|---|---|---|
      | `/v1/chat/completions` | `429`, 1 frame, JSON body | **`200`, 0 frames, `body=[]`** | `429`, 1 frame, the leader's exact body |
      | `/v1/messages` | `429`, 1 frame | **`200`, 0 frames, `body=[]`** | `429`, 1 frame |
      | `/v1/responses` | `429`, 1 frame | **`200`, 0 frames, `body=[]`** | `429`, 1 frame |

      On `/v1/responses` that is §57's symptom verbatim — Codex reports `stream closed before response.completed` and the upstream's real reason (quota, context, a risk interception) is gone. And the H6 backoff signal never reached the follower: §54's `x-should-retry: false` + `Retry-After` were on the leader's status line only, so the client most likely to retry (the one that deduped onto a refused flight) was the one told nothing. §45's rule — *a deterministic zero-upstream refusal belongs on the status line* — was being honoured for one of two request identities.
    - **The window is real, not a test artefact.** `get_or_create` pops a group as soon as it errors, so a follower can only land in it while the leader is still inside `execute_chat`. In a healthy ring that is milliseconds; in a dead ring it is the whole `GATEWAY_HANDSHAKE_BUDGET` (180 s) because `execute_chat` walks every candidate — i.e. exactly the condition that produces the refusal is the condition that widens the window. The suite therefore widens it deterministically (`_slow_dispatch`, a 0.35 s delay wrapped around `execute_chat`) instead of racing the clock: the delay only moves the clock, the refusal itself is still the product's own zero-upstream decision.
    - **The contract is tri-state, and both orderings are load-bearing.** `FlightGroup.wait_for_opening(timeout) -> (opened, terminal_error)`: `opened=True` means a frame has been broadcast, so the status line is already meaningless and the follower replays as before; `opened=False, error=<exc>` means the leader ended **without producing a byte**, and only then may the follower turn that error into a status line; `opened=False, error=None` means the wait expired — fail-open, stream anyway, let the in-band terminal frame handle it. **`history` must be consulted before `leader_done`**, in both the fast path and after the wait: a group that broadcast a frame and *then* failed has already shown its client content, and turning that into a "pre-response 5xx" the leader's own client never saw would merge two completely different client experiences into one.
    - **The wait uses `ensure_future` + `wait`, never `wait_for`** — [M2]'s lesson, applied a third time: a timeout implemented by cancellation would make "the leader is slow" indistinguishable from "our request was cancelled", and would cancel the very waiters whose state is being inspected. The two waiters are cancelled explicitly in `finally` and their outcomes retrieved with `gather(return_exceptions=True)`, so no `Task exception was never retrieved` warning hides behind the real one.
    - **`follower_open_timeout()` defaults to `GATEWAY_HANDSHAKE_BUDGET`, and the default is a fact rather than a tunable.** The budget is the leader's own promise about how long `execute_chat` may take before it concludes deterministically; a follower never needs to wait past it, and waiting past it can only mean the leader died without the orphan reaper having run. `GATEWAY_FOLLOWER_OPEN_TIMEOUT=0` restores the old behaviour exactly (immediate `200`, in-band net only), which is also the switch the fail-open case drives.
    - **The net under the net: `render_error`, rendered by the protocol that owns the shape.** `stream_follower_gen(group, render_error=...)` no longer raises at its three failure exits (already-done-with-error, idle timeout, an exception item). It yields the frames the renderer produces and then returns — because by then the status line is gone, and §52's rule applies: a stream that cannot report its failure will impersonate a complete one. `_follower_error_frames(protocol)` supplies each protocol's **own** terminal event, and all three are required: OpenAI `data: {"error": ...}` with **no** `[DONE]`; Anthropic `event: error` with no `event: message_stop`; Responses `ResponsesEventStream.error(...)`, which emits both `error` and `response.failed` because Codex 0.145.0 discards a bare `error` (§57). The shared layer keeps `render_error=None` so it stays protocol-agnostic and directly drivable.
    - **One judgement, six call sites, and the non-streaming sibling that had the same hole.** `_follower_opening_response(group, sf_headers, make_body)` is the single decision (it takes the protocol's error-body builder as an argument rather than branching on protocol), called from all three streaming follower branches. While auditing it, the **non-streaming** `/v1/responses` follower turned out to already carry the leader's status but **not** §54's headers — the same §45 shape one transport over — so it now attaches `_refusal_headers(pe)` too, and the generic 502 branch carries `sf_headers`.
    - **A proxy assertion that was narrower than the semantics it protected, corrected here.** `tests/test_stream_keepalive.py::test_all_streaming_responses_are_wrapped` counted the literal substrings `sse_keepalive(stream_body)` and `sse_keepalive(stream_follower_gen(`, so reformatting the three follower outlets across lines turned it red with `存在未接入心跳的流式出口: 3/6` and the behaviour fully correct — §55's and §101's defect class (an assertion about a proxy). It now collapses whitespace and counts `return StreamingResponse( sse_keepalive(` / `return ReclaimingStreamingResponse( sse_keepalive(`, which is **stronger** than the original (it requires keepalive to be the first argument of the return, not merely present in the file) and cannot be broken by layout.
    - **Verified per case on a pristine `HEAD` worktree** (`git worktree add /tmp/ffv_base HEAD`), each with its own measured symptom rather than a shared first-case failure: 5 RED — three status-line cases with `跟随者连响应头都没拿到（实测异常 'RuntimeError: Caught handled exception, but response already started.'）`, the in-band case with `打开等待开关后跟随者应当立刻开流，实测 None`, and the mid-stream unit case with `AttributeError: module 'server' has no attribute '_follower_error_frames'` — plus 1 GREEN by design, `test_a_healthy_follower_still_receives_every_frame`, the zero-regression guard that the wait does not change a healthy flight (it needs fake tokens and `_slow_dispatch` for the same window reason, and it asserts `follower["frames"] == leader["frames"]` including `[DONE]`). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 103`, working tree clean afterwards. The suite keeps the §35 tripwire (`_make_provider` raises) and asserts a zero-hit counter at the end, because every case here drives the real HTTP path.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_follower_failure_visibility.py` (6 cases).
103. **A Marker the Contract Never Names Is a Marker the Model Cannot Read (`providers/anthropic_compat.py:format_tools_system_prompt` / `extract_anthropic_content`)**
    - **[TOOL-LOOP] The contract described one of the three shapes the gateway itself writes.** None of the five upstreams has native tool calling, so the injected `## Tools Available` text is the **only** document the model ever reads about tools. Rule 5 named exactly one marker:
      ```
      5. When you receive a `[Tool Result for <id>]` block, continue the task using that result.
      ```
      while the replay paths write three (`providers/base.py::_message_line`, `providers/anthropic_compat.py::extract_anthropic_content`, `providers/responses_compat.py::_tool_call_block`). Measured against the real conversion chain (each of the nine replay shapes rendered, then the contract searched for the stem):
      ```
      contract names [Tool Result for <id>]   -> True
      contract names [Tool Error for <id>]    -> False
      contract names [Tool Result] (no id)    -> False
      ```
      and the prompt the model really receives for one failed and one successful call:
      ```
      User: [Tool Error for toolu_1]:
            ls: cannot access '/nope'
            [Tool Result for toolu_2]:
            a.py b.py
      ```
    - **`[Tool Error for <id>]` is not an edge case — it is the normal shape of a working agent session.** `AnthropicToolResultBlock.is_error` is set on every non-zero exit code, every rejected `Edit`, every file that could not be read, i.e. on a large fraction of an agent's turns. The *only* sentence the contract had about results was "continue the task using that result", which is exactly the action §38 warns about: a marker is input to the model's next reasoning step, so presenting a failure as a result makes the model continue from a conclusion that never held. §38 fixed this for **folding** markers ("`状态正常` asserted over a failed tool run"); the same fact was still wrong in the **contract**, and the contract is the copy the model reads on every single turn. The second half is the retry pathology: told nothing about failure, the model re-issues the identical call, gets the identical error, and turns one failure into two — each of them a real upstream request.
    - **The third shape was unnamed too, and the same defect sat in a second place.** A chat `role:"tool"` message without `tool_call_id` renders as `[Tool Result] (Bash)` — byte-different from the `for <id>` form the contract promises, so the model has no reason to treat them as one thing. Auditing that path surfaced the mirror-image bug in `extract_anthropic_content`: a `tool_result` block **missing** `tool_use_id` produced the degenerate `'[Tool Result for ]:\na.py'` / `'[Tool Error for ]:\nboom'` (measured), while `base.py` and `responses_compat.py` both already degraded to `[Tool Result]` in the same situation. "A fact, one rendering" (§43/§62/§88) — the half-built marker matched neither the contract's shape nor the other two paths' answer to the same question.
    - **One rule now names every shape, and success wording is untouched.** Rule 5 became a three-bullet list — success (`[Tool Result for <id>]`), failure (`[Tool Error for <id>]`, with the explicit "do not repeat the identical call — it already ran and produced exactly this"), and the id-less pair — and the id-less marker in `extract_anthropic_content` degrades to `[Tool Error]` / `[Tool Result]` the way its two siblings always did. The success bullet keeps the original "continue the task using that result" verbatim, because the fix must not rewrite what the model understands about a *successful* round trip.
    - **The primary assertion is a property over the real conversion paths, plus a non-vacuity guard.** `test_every_marker_the_replay_emits_is_named_by_the_contract` renders all nine replay shapes (three chat, four Anthropic, two Responses), normalises each emitted marker to its stem, and requires every stem to appear in the contract — so the *next* result shape a client invents turns the suite red instead of waiting for "the model answers off-topic again". `_EXPECTED_STEMS <= set(emitted)` guards the universal statement, because "every emitted marker is named" is trivially true when nothing is emitted at all.
    - **The degenerate marker needed its own case, and the reason is a limitation of the property itself.** `[Tool Result for ]` normalises to `[Tool Result for <id>]`, which *is* in the contract, so the property is blind to it by construction. `test_an_idless_result_never_renders_a_half_built_marker` asserts the literal `"for ]"` never appears and that the output really fell back to the id-less form — an assertion that has to read the rendered text, not a normalisation of it. This is §55/[M1]/§101's rule arriving a third way: an assertion about a normalised form is an assertion about something other than what the model sees.
    - **End to end, on the prompt the upstream actually receives**: `test_end_to_end_the_upstream_prompt_explains_the_error_marker` POSTs a `/v1/messages` turn carrying a failed `tool_result` through the real ASGI stack (scripted provider, `TESTING=1`, zero real upstream calls) and requires **both** `[Tool Error for toolu_1]` and the contract's `[Tool Error for <id>]` in the captured prompt. Asserting on `format_tools_system_prompt` alone would pass even if the prompt never reached upstream (§67(a)).
    - **Verified per case on a pristine `HEAD` worktree** (`git worktree add /tmp/tec_base HEAD`), each with its own measured symptom rather than a shared first-case failure: 5 RED — `这些标记网关会写进历史，而契约一个字都没提：[Tool Error for <id>]（来自 anthropic / tool_result is_error）、[Tool Result]（来自 chat / role=tool 无 id 有名字）`; `契约没有点名 \`[Tool Error for <id>]\`——而这正是每一次失败的 Claude Code 工具调用被回放时的形状`; `契约没有点名无 id 的 \`[Tool Result]\` 形态（chat 的 role=tool 缺 tool_call_id 时网关就是这么写的）`; `缺少 tool_use_id 时写出了残缺标记（实测 [Tool Result for ]）`; and `上游收到的提示词里没有失败标记的契约说明——模型读到的是一段它从未被告知过含义的文本`. Plus 1 GREEN by design, `test_success_marker_rule_is_unchanged`, the zero-regression guard that pins the success sentence still says "continue" and does **not** pick up failure wording (the §59/§69/§79 guard-case rule). The suite keeps the §35 tripwire (`_make_provider` raises) with a zero-hit counter asserted in `run_all()`, because the end-to-end case drives the real HTTP path.
    - Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 104`, working tree clean afterwards.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_tool_error_contract.py` (6 cases). `tests/test_tool_result_pairing.py`'s docstring quoted the old rule 5 verbatim and was **corrected** to the new text rather than left to drift (the "documented behaviour drifts from real behaviour" defect class).
105. **A Session Id Names a Session, and a Tool Set Names a Contract — Neither Names a Dialogue Line (`providers/session_affinity.py:line_opening_key` / `_line_key`, `server.py` affinity wiring)**
    - **[SESSION-IDENTITY] §98's rule, applied to the second pair of requests that share every identity a session id can express.** §98 split a session into lines by the declared tool contract, because Claude Code's auto-mode classifier (`tools: []`) shares `metadata.user_id.session_id` with its 47-tool parent. The remaining cell is **sequentially spawned sibling sub-agents**: the parent spawns child A, then child B, and both carry the *same* `metadata.user_id.session_id`, the *same* 45-tool set, and the *same* parsed upstream model. Measured from the real Claude Code 2.1.293 capture (`/tmp/rc/bodies2.jsonl`, five `/v1/messages` bodies; `tool_count=45` on both children, `sub=True` on both, identical in-`messages` system-message sha `4cefbbbf130f`):
      ```
      n=1  cid=None      父会话第 1 轮
      n=2  cid=None      子 agent 1（正确：自己的线）
      n=3  cid='agent-1' 父会话第 2 轮
      n=4  cid='conv-2'  子 agent 2  <-- 拿到的正是子 agent 1 那次调用铸出的会话
      n=5  cid='agent-2' 父会话第 3 轮
      ```
      The two children's upstream conversations are therefore merged: the second child's answer is built on the first child's task and results, while the client is reading it for a different task. No error at any layer — the same face §66 recorded on the Responses path, now on the Anthropic path.
    - **The three rejected discriminators, and why each one is not evidence.** (a) The captured `cc_is_subagent` marker: **both** children carry it, so it cannot separate them — it separates a child from its parent, which §98's tool-contract rule already does. (b) The top-level system prompt: stable *in this capture* (`e582e033c10c` across the parent's three turns) but §76 measured Claude Code sending a **fresh** system block every turn (8662 chars vs 8594+119), so it is not a per-line constant and hashing it would thrash the parent's own binding. (c) Full-history prefix comparison: two interleaved siblings grow independently, so their prefixes diverge after turn one and a prefix rule degenerates into "everything is a new line" — the §53/§75 narrowing failure in the other direction.
    - **What *is* constant per line and distinct between siblings: the user turn the line opened with.** Measured over the same capture: the parent's three turns (`messages` 2 → 5 → 8) hash to the **same** opening (`3aced25b395c`), while the two children hash to `b64e375c72de` and `bf77a9b42571`. A conversation that started from a different opening is a different conversation — the same rule as §98, applied to the fact that actually varies here.
    - **`line_opening_key(messages)` is the single definition**, returning `""` for non-list input, for no user turn, and for a blank first user turn (so a pure prefill request keeps its previous identity byte-for-byte). `_line_key` gained it as a fourth component after `requested_model`; `AffinityDecision.line_opening` carries it so the decision's own identity components cannot disagree with what was looked up, and all three protocol handlers compute it once and pass it to `resolve_affinity` / `has_upstream_session` / all eight `record_turn` sites. Absent components still return the bare session id, so every pre-existing call site is unchanged.
    - **Direction is widening only (§53/§75).** Adding a component can only split lines that were wrongly merged; it can never merge two that were separate. Two honest residuals are recorded in the module docstring rather than papered over: (1) two requests with a byte-identical opening still merge — from the gateway's side they are indistinguishable from *one request sent twice*, and a retry **must** reuse the upstream session; (2) a client that rewrites its head mid-line (context compaction rewrites the whole history) starts a new upstream conversation and re-flattens — expensive, never wrong.
    - **Ten existing cases had modelled a request shape no real client sends, and they were corrected rather than worked around.** Once the head is part of the line identity, "each turn sends one *new, different* user message" stops being "turn 2 of the same conversation" — and the Anthropic and Responses protocols have no server-side history, so a real client re-sends the whole history every turn (`previous_response_id` is refused outright, §73). The affected turn shapes were corrected to carry a stable opening and a growing history, each with an inline note: `tests/test_affinity.py` (chat turn 3, the Anthropic streaming turn 3, the Responses turn 2 — which was *also* the exact §73 "a question with no antecedent" shape, and the Codex thread case whose `_post` helper now accumulates per-thread history), `tests/test_affinity_cooling.py` (the disabled-provider `handoff` control), `tests/test_anthropic_stream.py` (`metadata.user_id.session_id` reuse, previously `messages: [{"role":"user","content": f"turn {i}"}]` — i.e. a new line every round, so continuation could never happen and the case measured "fresh session" while claiming "session identity works"), `tests/test_continuation_contract.py` (two `has_upstream_session` calls now pass `line_opening`), and `tests/test_session_continuity.py` (`_line()` gained the component through the same `_line_key`, plus the `has_upstream_session` call). Each case still tests its own subject; only the request shape that had stopped denoting it changed. This is §45/§63/§78's pattern — the product changed, so the assertions that pinned the old lie were corrected, and the direction is stated so a later reader does not mistake it for weakening a test.
    - **Verified per case on a pristine worktree** (`git worktree add /tmp/sa_base 6b12d48`): case 1 is RED with the exact measured message `第二个子 agent 拿到了上游会话 id 'conv-2'——它与第一个子 agent 的 session_id、工具集、模型完全相同，于是被当成了续聊。实测（修复前）：n=4 cid='conv-2' 子 agent 2。` The suite's parent/child tool sets are deliberately different (`PARENT_TOOLS = TOOLS + [Agent]`, 3 vs 2 tools) so §98's rule is already doing its job and only the *sibling* collision is under test; an earlier version gave parent and children one tool set, and the base tree failed for the parent-merge reason instead of the sibling one. The import is `getattr(_affinity, "line_opening_key", None)` on purpose — a `from … import` would make the whole suite `ImportError` on the base tree and case 1, the behavioural one, would never run.
    - **One case is green on the base tree by design and says so**: `test_identical_opening_still_shares_the_conversation` pins the first honest residual (a genuine retry of the same opening **must** reuse the session), which is the zero-regression guard for a key that is otherwise only ever widened (§59/§69/§79 guard-case rule). Case 2 is structural — the function must exist, be stable as history grows, discriminate a different opening, return `""` for `[]` / assistant-first / `None`, and ignore a leading `system` message.
    - **Regression checked the way the defect would show up in production**: the full glob sweep is `failures: 0 / 105` after the corrected cases, and the real binaries still reuse their upstream conversation through the widened key —
      ```
      claude: PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=Bash,         tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      codex:  PASS (exit=0, bodies=3, tool_ran=True, tool=exec_command, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)
      concurrent window: 1031 ms of genuine overlap (6 upstream bodies total)
      ```
      `conversation_reused=True` on both arms is the load-bearing half: a key that split *every* request would also make the offline sibling case green while quietly turning every real agent session into "re-flatten the whole history every turn".
    - Pinned by `tests/test_subagent_affinity_isolation.py` (3 cases), which keeps the §35 tripwire (`_make_provider` raises) and asserts a zero-hit counter at the end.
106. **A Rejected Request Was Reported as "All Five Nodes Are Down" (`providers/base.py:classify_stream_status`, `providers/failover.py:execute_chat`, `server.py:_is_deterministic_refusal`)**
    - **[REQUEST-REJECT] The status classifier expressed two facts where there are three.** `classify_stream_status` knew 401 / 403 / 429 (risk control) and everything else, and every provider raised `ProviderError(status=resp.status_code if is_risk else 502, err_type=err_type)`. So an upstream **rejecting the request body** — 400 malformed, 413 payload too large (a long agent conversation's context limit), 422 semantically invalid or policy-rejected — became `502 upstream_error`: "the server is broken, try again", when the truth was "this request will be refused identically however many times you send it".
    - **Measured** (scripted provider raising exactly what the provider layer raises today, `TESTING=1`, zero real upstream calls, six protocol × transport paths):
      ```
      chat-nonstream        -> 502  calls=5  cooldowns={qwen, glm, doubao, kimi, deepseek}
      chat-stream           -> 502  calls=5  cooldowns={all five}
      anthropic-nonstream   -> 502  calls=5  cooldowns={all five}
      anthropic-stream      -> 502  calls=5  cooldowns={all five}
      responses-nonstream   -> 502  calls=5  cooldowns={all five}
      responses-stream      -> 502  calls=3  cooldowns={three}

      body: {"error":{"message":"对等能力环 [flagship] 内所有节点均已尝试且全部失效。…"}}
      ```
      Four consequences, each a defect shape this file already records elsewhere: **(a)** five credentialed upstream contacts for a request that is deterministically unacceptable — the automated-client signature [H7] and OPT-025 exist to eliminate, and for an agent session a context-limit rejection is a *guaranteed* event, not an edge case; **(b)** every healthy node booked with `record_failure` and a cooldown, so later requests route around nodes that did nothing wrong ([BUDGET-3]/[BUDGET-4]'s "our own judgement recorded as the upstream's fault"); **(c)** the client receives a **5xx**, which every SDK treats as retryable, so the same body is re-sent; **(d)** the operator is told "all nodes failed" when the fault is the request (the OPT-026/027/029/031/034/035 misleading-the-operator class).
    - **The status code is the whole fact, so no body sniffing is needed.** `_REQUEST_REJECT_STATUSES = {400, 413, 422}` → `("invalid_request", True)`. The tuple's second element was renamed `is_risk` → **`keep_status`** because that is all it ever did (`status=resp.status_code if X else 502` at all seven call sites); leaving the risk-only name would have invited the next reader to conclude that a non-risk class must not keep its status.
    - **No failover is a deliberate trade, and the reason is this project's own constraint.** Another vendor might accept what the first one rejected — but establishing that costs five credentialed touches, and the entire avoidance line exists because we do not spend accounts on a guess. The refusal message says so and names the remedy (fix the request: context length, field structure, content policy). An operator who wants a different vendor changes the model and re-sends.
    - **`_is_deterministic_refusal`'s `status < 500 → False` guard would have silently eaten the whole fix.** It was equivalent to set membership only while every member was 5xx; adding a 4xx member would leave the set correct and the behaviour unchanged — a test asserting the set would pass while nothing happened. The predicate is now membership-based with an explicit `_DETERMINISTIC_REFUSAL_4XX = {"invalid_request"}` (4xx admits exactly one class, others keep their own semantics).
    - **The one refusal that must *not* carry `Retry-After`.** Every other member of the set is waiting for a window that expires on its own (lease, cooldown, credential expiry), so a countdown is the honest signal; a rejected request's repairer **is the client**, who can fix and re-send immediately. `Retry-After: 600` would tell it to idle for ten minutes it does not need (§54's rule: the value must be the real window, and here the real window is zero). `x-should-retry: false` — the header §54 measured Codex actually obeys — is still sent.
    - **`_ERR_TYPE_MAP` gained `"invalid_request": "invalid_request_error"` explicitly** rather than relying on `_STATUS_ERROR_MAP[400]`: that map has no 413/422, so those would have fallen through to Anthropic's catch-all `api_error` — a client that asked for a readable, fixable error would get "unknown".
    - **A test bug of exactly the [M1] shape was caught by the suite's own zero-regression case.** `_restore()` cleared the risk state, so `assert not _cooldowns()` was true **by construction** and would have stayed green through any amount of wrongful cooling. State is now read inside the request scope, before teardown, and `_restore()`'s docstring says why it must not reset it.
    - **Verified per case on a pristine `HEAD` worktree** (`git worktree add /tmp/ir_base HEAD`), each with its own measured message: case 1 (`classify` contract) red with `实测被归成了 'upstream_error'`; case 2 (six paths) red with `chat-nonstream: 上游 400（请求被拒绝）被报成了 502 … 实测 body: {"error":{"message":"对等能力环 [flagship] 内所有节点均已尝试且全部失效。…`; case 3 (message honesty) red with `一条被拒绝的请求被报成了「整环失效」…`; case 4 is the zero-regression control (an upstream 5xx must still fail over and cool) and is **green on both trees by design**, per the §59/§69/§79 guard-case rule.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_invalid_request.py` (4 cases). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 106`.

107. **A Plain 5xx Is Not a Human-Verification Interception (`providers/failover.py:record_failure`, `providers/risk_dossier.py:AVOIDANCE_MATRIX`)**
    - **[COOLDOWN-CAUSE] The cause of the gateway's most common failure was a default value nobody chose.** `record_failure` opens by calling `classify_interception_severity`, and that classifier answers `SEVERITY_WAF` for **any** input that is not 401/429 — a fallback that reads as "if it is neither auth nor rate-limit, assume a captcha". Every branch in the chain then assigned its own cause explicitly — the timeout branch, the truncation branch, the unconfigured branch, the risk/auth branches — **except the last one, plain 5xx**, which fell through with `severity` still holding the classifier's default. Measured on the real `CooldownTracker` (credential in place, `TESTING=1`, zero upstream calls):
      ```
      [对等互备网络] 节点 [qwen] 失败 (HTTP 502)，进入冷却避让期 15.0s
      cooldown: 15.0 | cause: waf
      posture: cooling | cooldown_cause: waf
      avoidance.verdict: waf | recoverable_by_relogin: False | auto_recovers: True
      ```
    - **Four consumers misled at once, each a defect shape this file already records.** (a) **The dossier's advice**: `waf` in `AVOIDANCE_MATRIX` is the one tier whose remedy is "a human must complete the slider", so an operator reading `GET /v1/admin/risk` after a routine 502 was told to go log into a platform that had never intercepted them — the `[M2]` (timeout marked `waf`), `[S2]` (truncation marked `waf`) and `[DAY-ZERO]` (unconfigured marked `auth`) class, one cause over. (b) **The cause-scoped release path**: `release_auth_avoidance_locked` releases by cause, so a `waf` window waits for a credential fingerprint change while the window it actually needed was "fifteen seconds have passed" — the fix that ends the avoidance was unreachable. (c) **Long-avoidance classification** (§OPT-031) reads the cause for both its threshold reasoning and its operator-facing wording. (d) **The credential baseline that OPT-029's long avoidance is lifted by** was written on `severity == SEVERITY_WAF` **without** the shared "is this really an interception" gate that the ledger and the probation entry already used — the fourth hand-copied instance of one predicate, and the copy that decides whether the gateway believes a human intervened.
    - **The fix is three lines of assignment plus one hoisted predicate, not a new mechanism.** (1) The plain-5xx branch now writes `severity = "upstream_error"` — an explicit cause for the branch that previously inherited one. (2) `is_interception = not is_upstream_fault and (is_risk or is_waf_or_rate or is_auth_error)` is computed once, immediately after `is_upstream_fault`, and the probation entry, the ledger append and the WAF baseline all read it; the three sites that were already correct are now provably the same predicate as the one that was wrong. (3) `risk_dossier` learns the cause in all three of its tables — `AVOIDANCE_MATRIX` (label `上游 5xx (服务端错误，非风控拦截)`, `recoverable_by_relogin: False`, strategy ending "**重新登录无效**，也不应去完成任何验证码"), `_SEVERITY_TO_VERDICT` (without it the row falls through to `or "ratelimit"`), and `_LIVE_ONLY_CAUSES` (the cause never enters the ledger, so the verdict must come from the live cooldown).
    - **The new cause is safe against the risk cause by construction, and that is `[BUDGET-6]`'s asymmetry doing its job.** `_set_cooldown_cause` lets a **risk** cause overwrite freely (a later interception is newer evidence, and OPT-029's cause-scoped release depends on it) but lets a **non-risk** cause overwrite only when it actually extended the window. `upstream_error` is outside `_RISK_CAUSES` (`auth`/`ratelimit`/`waf`), so a 15-second 502 window can never relabel a 600-second rate-limit window that is still in force. The suite pins this direction explicitly, because the pre-fix behaviour (`waf`) *is* a risk cause and therefore *did* overwrite unconditionally — the defect and its absence of protection are the same fact.
    - **The terminal log line now names the cause.** The warning an operator actually reads printed `失败 (HTTP 502)` with no cause, so a mislabelled cause could only be discovered by going and asking the dossier. It now reads `失败 (HTTP 502, 成因 upstream_error)，进入冷却避让期 15.0s` — the cause is the fact that decides the next action (re-login / wait for a human / wait for the window / check upstream health), so it belongs in the line that is read at the moment of failure.
    - **Two test-harness traps were found while writing the suite, and both would have produced a green test measuring nothing.** (a) The credential-file patch covered **only `qwen`**, so case 4 (glm) hit `note_interception`'s early return for a `None` fingerprint and never exercised the branch at all — the helper now patches `credential_path` provider-agnostically and says why in its docstring. (b) The dossier case asserted `"验证码" not in strategy` and **failed after the fix**, because the corrected strategy text contains "也不应去完成任何验证码" — §62's trap (a substring judgement that fires on exactly the right wording). The judgement moved to the machine-readable field (`recoverable_by_relogin is False`) plus the one sentence that must be present (`"重新登录无效"` in strategy).
    - **Case 4 is a guard, and is labelled as one.** `test_a_genuine_waf_interception_is_unchanged` (403 + RGV587 → cause `waf`, baseline written, one ledger entry; plus a 429 → `ratelimit` control) is **green on both trees by design** — it constrains the fix's scope rather than reproducing the defect, and its reason is the fix's own failure direction: assigning the cause must not touch the condition, or a genuine human-verification hit is demoted to `upstream_error` and OPT-029's long avoidance loses the only exit a human has (§59/§69/§79).
    - **Verified per case on a pristine `HEAD` worktree** (`git worktree add /tmp/cc_base HEAD`), each with its own measured message: case 1 red with `一次普通 502 被记成了风控成因 'waf'`; case 2 red with `一次普通 502 写入了人机验证的凭证基线`; case 3 red with `档案读到的成因是 'waf'`; case 5 red with `一次普通 5xx（15.0s）把仍在生效的频控成因改成了 'waf'`; case 4 green on both trees by design. After the fix all five green.
    - `tests/test_risk_dossier.py`'s deliberately pinned matrix vocabulary assertion tripped, which is its purpose; the pinned contract was **corrected** to the new set with a `[COOLDOWN-CAUSE]` clause recording what the row is for, the same treatment OPT-035 / `[M2]` / `[S2]` / `[DAY-ZERO]` each received.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_cooldown_cause_fidelity.py` (5 cases). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 107`. Real binaries re-run afterwards: `--parallel --rounds 2` → both `PASS (exit=0, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)`, 925 ms of genuine overlap, `XCXCXC` interleaving.


108. **An Upstream Fault Is Not a Risk-Control Ban at the Background-Touch Boundary (`providers/failover.py:touch_avoidance_reason`, `healing/monitor.py`, `healing/healer.py`, `providers/conversation_gc.py`)**
    - **[TOUCH-CAUSE] §107 fixed the cause *value* and left three of its *readers* behind.** `record_failure` now records a plain 5xx as `upstream_error` instead of inheriting `waf`, and the dossier learned the row. But `touch_avoidance_reason()` — the **single** judgement every background self-maintenance channel asks before touching an account (health probe, token refresh, conversation GC, healer election) — returned `"cooldown"` for **any** positive cooldown, **without consulting the cause**. So all three upstream-fault causes were folded back into risk control on the way out. Measured on the real `CooldownTracker` (`TESTING=1`, `GATEWAY_DISABLE_RISK_LEDGER=1`, zero upstream calls):
      ```
      plain 5xx   cooldown=15.0s  cause='upstream_error'  touch_reason='cooldown'
      timeout     cooldown=45.0s  cause='timeout'         touch_reason='cooldown'
      truncated   cooldown=45.0s  cause='truncated'       touch_reason='cooldown'
      waf         cooldown=45.0s  cause='waf'             touch_reason='cooldown'
      ```
    - **`cooldown` is not a neutral word here — three consumers translate it into a ban.** (a) The probe wrote `风控避让冷却中 (剩余 15s)，已跳过真实探针以规避上游封禁` and logged `避免加剧上游封禁`; (b) the healer election wrote `处于风控避让冷却期的节点 qwen，避免在避让期内触碰该账号`; (c) conversation GC wrote `处于风控避让冷却中，推迟其临时会话删除以避免触碰被封禁/已下线的账号`. So **one upstream 502** told the operator, in three separate logs, that the account was under platform risk control or banned. This is the `[DAY-ZERO]` defect exactly — "an account that has never been touched upstream was described to the operator as a risk-control ban in four places" — with the cause moved from *configuration gap* to *upstream fault*. It is also, verbatim, the `[DAY-ZERO]` rule §64 records and §107 did not finish applying: **"a new cause is not done until every cause-reader knows it."**
    - **The fix folds; it does not rewrite.** `touch_avoidance_reason()` now reads `cooldown_cause()` and returns the cause when it is **not** in `_RISK_CAUSES`, and `"cooldown"` otherwise. The asymmetry is deliberate and load-bearing in both directions: a **risk** cause must keep returning `cooldown` because (i) the three consumer messages are already correct for it, and (ii) `login.py all`'s "a cooldown is exactly what re-login repairs" branch keys on it — narrowing the risk side would close the only repair path for OPT-029's long human-verification avoidance, which is worse than the original defect. An **unreadable** cause (`cooldown_cause()` returning `""`, when neither the record nor the ledger can be read) also keeps `cooldown`: better to say "risk control" once too often than to unblock a single upstream touch because the cause could not be read.
    - **`_UPSTREAM_FAULT_CAUSES` sits next to `_RISK_CAUSES` so the two halves of one classification share a definition.** "Which causes are risk" and "which are not" were about to become two independent tuples at two sites, which is the defect shape this file records most often (`is_disabled`, `compute_key`, `_tool_call_block`, `format_tools_system_prompt`).
    - **The three consumer label maps each gained the three causes, with wording that says what the operator should actually do.** Probe: `上游超时避让中 / 上游流被截断避让中 / 上游故障（5xx）避让中 (剩余 Ns)，已跳过真实探针`; healer: `处于上游超时避让期 …`; GC: `处于上游超时避让中 (剩余 Ns)（上游故障期间不追加上游请求）`. GC's closing sentence was **hardcoded to the ban story** and is now branched too — a shared tail is a shared claim, and the claim was wrong. The probe's warning log likewise carries `（上游故障，不是风控拦截）` instead of `避免加剧上游封禁`.
    - **`touch_gates.causes` — the one published vocabulary — had to grow, or it would lie by omission.** It is the list an operator reads to interpret `per_cause`; a cause the gate can return but the list omits makes any value outside the list unreadable ("some other kind of thing"). It now includes all three.
    - **The guard case is green on both trees by design, and its reason is the fix's own failure direction.** `test_a_genuine_waf_cooldown_still_reads_as_risk_control` pins that a real 403/RGV587 cooldown still reports `cooldown` and still reads as `风控` in the probe. Narrowing the scope to non-risk causes while "tidying" the risk side would break `login.py all`'s repair entry (OPT-029's long WAF avoidance is lifted *only* by a human completing the slider and re-logging in), so this case constrains the fix rather than reproducing the defect — the §59/§69/§79/§107 guard rule.
    - **The test's own banned-word list matched the fix's correct wording.** The first version banned the substring `风控`, and the fixed probe message contains `（上游故障，不是风控拦截）` — so it would have gone red on exactly the right sentence. §62 / [COOLDOWN-CAUSE]'s trap, third occurrence. The judgement is now on **asserted phrases** (`风控避让` / `规避上游封禁` / `被封禁` / `风控冷却`), i.e. on the sentences that make the false claim, plus a positive assertion that the message names the real cause (`上游`).
    - **One case was restructured rather than made to pass.** The healer case originally called `pick_healer_model(monitor)` and, if no log was emitted, fell back to asserting a label table it imported — but `_CAUSE_LABEL` is a **local** dict inside the function, so the fallback was unreachable, and the real signature takes `failing_provider`. It now calls `pick_healer_model(monitor, "glm")` and asserts the skip line the election really writes (`_avoiding()` logs for every avoiding candidate before any status lookup), because a fallback that asserts a table instead of the log line is a proxy assertion about something other than the behaviour ([M1]/§55).
    - **Verified per case on a pristine `HEAD` worktree** (`git worktree add /tmp/tc_base HEAD`), each with its own measured message: case 1 red with `timeout 冷却的触点成因被报成了 'cooldown'`; case 2 red with `一次普通 502 的探针读数里出现了 '风控避让'。原文: 风控避让冷却中 (剩余 15s)，已跳过真实探针以规避上游封禁`; case 3 red with `自愈选举把上游超时说成了 '风控避让': [自愈选举] 跳过处于风控避让冷却期的节点 qwen…`; case 4 red with `会话回收把上游截断说成了 '被封禁': …推迟其临时会话删除以避免触碰被封禁/已下线的账号`; case 6 red with `` `touch_gates.causes` 缺 'timeout' ``; case 5 green on both trees by design. After the fix all six green.
    - Pinned by `tests/test_touch_cause_fidelity.py` (6 cases). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 108`. Real binaries re-run afterwards: `--parallel --rounds 2` → both `PASS (exit=0, tool_markers=2/2, conversation_reused=True)`, 991 ms of genuine overlap, `XCXCXC` interleaving (5 switches across 6 bodies).
109. **The Fourth Background Channel Never Asked the Judgement (`server.py:_refresh_stale_provider` / `_deferred_refresh_after_cooldown`)**
    - **[TOUCH-CAUSE] §108 fixed the judgement and left one of its four consumers behind.** `touch_avoidance_reason()` is documented — in the OPT-034 record, in `healing/task_dispatcher.py`, and in §108 — as the **single** judgement that "四条通道全部向它求值，并在 `server.py` 中只接线一次". Three of them did (probe, healer, GC). The token-refresh channel never called it: `_refresh_stale_provider` gated on `_provider_cooldown_remaining(name) > 0`, a float that answers only *how long*, never *why*. A docstring asserting a wiring that does not exist is the §59/§69/§79 defect class sitting on the one channel an operator reads at **startup**, before any request has been made.
    - **Measured pre-fix** (real `CooldownTracker`, `TESTING=1`, zero upstream calls, refresher replaced by one that raises if called so the measurement cannot silently touch upstream):
      ```
      timeout        remaining=45s   → [网关] qwen 处于风控避让冷却期，暂缓令牌刷新（避免加剧上游封禁）
      truncated      remaining=45s   → 同一句
      upstream_error remaining=15s   → 同一句
      quarantine     remaining=3600s → 同一句
      ```
      Three upstream faults and an **operator ban lease** all narrated as "风控避让 … 避免加剧上游封禁". The lease row is the one that shows reading the *cause* alone would not have been enough either: `cooldown_cause()` returns `""` for a pure lease (it folds into the window via `max()`, not into the cause), so a "read the cause only" fix enters no branch and reports nothing. The judgement is the only reading that answers both halves.
    - **The give-up branch lied twice.** `_deferred_refresh_after_cooldown` waited on the same float and then printed `[网关] qwen 风控冷却未解除（已达等待上限 0.0h），跳过本轮令牌刷新` — a risk-control diagnosis for a timeout, plus `:.0f` rendering a sub-hour wait as `0.0h` (§60b's honest-number rule, one channel over).
    - **One wiring point, one sentence builder.** `server.py` gained `_touch_avoidance_reason(name)` as the single place the refresh channel asks the judgement (fail-open: an exception falls back to `disabled` → `cooldown`-if-positive, so a broken diagnostic can never turn the gate into a no-op), and `_touch_avoidance_sentence(name, reason, suffix)` composes the log line from a site-local noun/tail table so an unknown cause still says something ("处于后台避让期 … 跳过本次上游操作") rather than printing nothing.
    - **The four label tables were deliberately *not* merged, and the reason is measured.** A shared cause→label table across the four channels was drafted; measuring showed the healer writes `处于上游超时避让期`, GC writes `处于上游超时避让中` (and appends `（上游故障期间不追加上游请求）`), the probe composes `上游超时避让中 (剩余 Ns)`, and the refresh line needs a clause about token refresh specifically. The trailing particle is grammatically bound to its own sentence, so a shared table would break three of four for no measurable gain — the §59 "do not build for an unmeasured problem" rule applied to a refactor. What *is* shared is the **cause vocabulary**, which is what a reader actually has to interpret consistently.
    - **`disabled` must not become `cooling`.** The verdict maps to the pre-existing return contract: `disabled` returns `"disabled"` (OPT-034: a disabled node is waiting on a human, never deferred into a retry queue), every other cause returns `"cooling"` (so `cooldown` / `quarantined` / upstream faults still enter the bounded deferred retry, preserving OPT-013's repair path).
    - **A fixture had to be corrected to the new single authority, and it was load-bearing.** `tests/test_refresh_gate.py::_patch_cooldown` faked only the floats, so once the decision moved off them every "cooling" case in that suite would have let a genuinely cooled node be **refreshed** — one real upstream login per case. It now patches both the float and `_touch_avoidance_reason` together (with a comment saying why), and all five call sites were updated. This is §45/§63/§76/§79's convention: correct the fixture to the code's new reading, never assert the old lie.
    - **Verified per case on a pristine `HEAD` worktree** (`git worktree add /tmp/tc_base HEAD`, new test file copied in), each RED with its own measured message: `timeout 的刷新日志里出现了 '风控避让'——一次上游故障被说成了风控。原文: '[网关] qwen 处于风控避让冷却期，暂缓令牌刷新（避免加剧上游封禁）'`; `运维租约被报成了一次软冷却，运维无法从日志里分辨「等租约到期」和「等冷却窗口」`; `放弃语句没有说出真实成因（上游超时），运维会去排查一个不存在的风控封禁`; and the tampering case (§33 technique) `避让期内不得发起静默刷新（那是一次真实的上游登录请求）`. Two guards are green on both trees by design: `test_token_refresh_still_reads_a_genuine_waf_cooldown_as_risk_control` (narrowing the risk side would close `login.py`'s repair path — the §59/§69/§79/§107 rule) and `test_a_healthy_node_is_still_refreshed` (the fix must not turn the gate into a permanent no-op).
    - Pinned by `tests/test_touch_cause_fidelity.py` (12 cases). Full glob sweep `failures: 0 / 108`; `tests/test_refresh_gate.py` (6 cases, OPT-013) and the four neighbouring suites (`test_disabled_propagation.py`, `test_credential_renewal.py`, `test_quarantine.py`, `test_login_sweep.py`) each green.
